 \iff\bigwedge_{i=1}^{9}[c_i=\operatorname{admitted}_i]
 \iff c=c_*.
\]

Complete product membership covers every typed tuple. Equality with the original source decision is checked over that full product, and a rejected coordinate contradicts the corresponding conjunct. These grammar results accompany the six operational witnesses and general graph proofs.


66. Graph induction and locality of path updates

The One graph with no edges is admitted by the original constructor, supplies its identity path and identity reachability, and has no directed cycle. Under extensions retaining prior nodes and edges, every existing complete path and return trace remains valid. Consequently prior reachability on an admitted graph and prior detected cycles are retained.

For a carrier extended by a fresh node and an appended edge selection, any newly valid path whose nodes all belong to the old carrier must contain an added edge absent from the old relation. When each added edge has the fresh node as an endpoint and that node is absent from the old carrier, such a path cannot use an added edge. Hence the original and extended path-validation results are exactly equal on every sequence of old nodes, including rejected sequences. This establishes the locality of path updates in the source fresh-node induction step.

These results retain the explicit conditions of the extension. They do not impose new constructor guards or restrict the original graph operations. Ordered pair-support generation, cut and paired-balance extension are established below; held-selection characterization is also established below. These are results of the completed graph entry.


The base graph is G₁ = ([One],[]), with Valid([One]) = true, Reachable(One,One) = true and CycleExists(G₁) = false. If V ⊆ V' and E ⊆ E', every valid old path retains all node and edge checks. Every old return trace likewise retains each edge and held-prefix comparison. The path and cycle equivalences therefore transfer positive reachability and cycle results.

For V' = V ++ [z] and E' = E ++ A, suppose a sequence P contains only old nodes, is rejected by G and accepted by G'. The accepted path is nonempty and satisfies all node checks already in V. Hence some adjacency must account for the change:

\[
 \exists e\in\operatorname{Adj}(P),\quad e\in A\land e\notin E.
\]

Now assume z ∉ V and every edge of A touches z. Both endpoints of every adjacency in P belong to P and hence to V. No such adjacency can touch z, contradicting the displayed witness. Thus

\[
 \forall P\text{ with nodes in }V,\quad
 \operatorname{Valid}_{(V\mathbin{+\!+}[z],E\mathbin{+\!+}A)}(P)
 =\operatorname{Valid}_{(V,E)}(P).
\]

The equality includes rejected and empty sequences. It follows from the explicit fresh-endpoint condition, without adding a restriction to the original path validator.


67. Admitted graph extension, ordered pairs, cuts and balance

The graph constructor succeeds exactly when its node list is nonempty and distinct, its edge list is distinct, and every endpoint belongs to the node list. If a genuinely fresh node is appended, a distinct added-edge selection whose endpoints lie in the enlarged carrier and whose edges touch that fresh node is disjoint from the old relation. The combined graph therefore satisfies precisely the original constructor checks.

The complete ordered node-pair product extends row by row: each old left-node row retains all old right-node cells followed by its fresh-right cell; the final fresh-left row contains every old right node followed by the fresh self-pair. The equality retains the full generated order and does not assert that every generated pair is selected as an edge.

For an already successful held cut selection, extending the node carrier and appending edges returns exactly the old cut list followed by the added edges whose endpoint held-membership differs. Selection validity follows from the original nonempty and carrier-membership guard; no distinctness requirement is added to the held list. For an internally balanced pair of ingress/egress trace lists, appending one previously absent trace to each side preserves equal counts and separate distinctness, hence preserves the original balance predicate.


Let G = (V,E) be admitted, z ∉ V, and A a distinct added-edge list whose endpoints lie in V' = V ++ [z] and whose every edge touches z. If e belonged to both E and A, an old endpoint would equal z, contradicting z ∉ V. Thus E and A are disjoint, their concatenation is distinct, and all constructor guards hold for G' = (V',E ++ A).

Writing row(u,V) = [(u,v):v ∈ V] as an ordered list, the full pair-support identity is

\[
 \operatorname{Pairs}(V')=
 \mathop{\mathrm{concat}}_{u\in V}(\operatorname{row}(u,V)\mathbin{+\!+}[(u,z)])
 \mathbin{+\!+}(\operatorname{row}(z,V)\mathbin{+\!+}[(z,z)]).
\]

This distributes map and flatMap over append and retains each old row's order. For a previously successful cut with fixed held H, filter distribution gives

\[
 \operatorname{Cut}_H(E\mathbin{+\!+}A)
 =\operatorname{Cut}_H(E)\mathbin{+\!+}
 [e\in A\mid(e_1\in H)\mathbin{\mathrm{xor}}(e_2\in H)].
\]

For balanced ingress and egress lists I,O and fresh respective traces i,o,

\[
 |I\mathbin{+\!+}[i]|=|I|+1=|O|+1=|O\mathbin{+\!+}[o]|.
\]

Fresh append preserves each list's distinctness, and the incoming list remains nonempty, proving the original balance predicate after extension.


68. Held-partition updates and exact paired-balance conditions

Appending a carrier node to a valid held selection replaces each endpoint-membership test by membership in the old selection or in that appended singleton. Filtering the complete original edge list by the resulting unequal-membership test gives exactly the updated cut, including the original allowance for repeated held nodes. An edge whose endpoints differ from the appended node has an unchanged cut decision.

For an admitted old graph, a genuinely fresh node belongs to neither endpoint of any old edge. If that node is appended to both carrier and held selection while new edges are appended, the resulting cut is exactly the prior cut followed by the added edges crossing the enlarged held partition. The equation preserves original list order.

Appending one ingress trace and one egress trace yields internal balance if and only if the original trace counts agree, each original trace list is distinct, and neither appended trace already occurs on its respective side. This characterization includes empty original lists: their first trace pair is balanced. For an already balanced pair, the exact remaining requirements are freshness on both sides. No restriction on trace contents is added to the original operation.


Define the endpoint indicator χ_H(x) by membership in the held list. Appending y gives χ_(H++[y])(x) = χ_H(x) ∨ (x=y). Therefore the updated cut is exactly

\[
 [e\in E\mid(\chi_H(e_1)\lor e_1=y)
 \mathbin{\mathrm{xor}}(\chi_H(e_2)\lor e_2=y)].
\]

If neither endpoint is y, both added equalities are false and the old cut decision is unchanged. When y = z is fresh to an admitted old graph, this applies to every old edge. For H' = H ++ [z], the combined carrier/edge extension consequently satisfies

\[
 \operatorname{Cut}_{H'}(E\mathbin{+\!+}A)
 =\operatorname{Cut}_{H}(E)\mathbin{+\!+}\operatorname{Cut}_{H'}(A),
\]

where the rightmost term denotes the same ordered crossing-edge filter. Repeated members in H affect neither the indicator nor the source's valid-selection guard.

The exact paired-balance equivalence is

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{Balance}(I\mathbin{+\!+}[i],O\mathbin{+\!+}[o])\iff{}&
 |I|=|O|\land\operatorname{Distinct}(I)\land\operatorname{Distinct}(O)\\
 &\land i\notin I\land o\notin O.
 \end{aligned}
\]

The appended incoming list is automatically nonempty; equal successor lengths cancel to |I| = |O|, and distinctness of each append is exactly prior distinctness plus absence of the new trace. In particular Balance([i],[o]) = true for arbitrary traces i,o, including empty traces, because trace contents are unrestricted by this operation.

The registered common-refinement entry combines the general nested pair-cell, complete lifting, constructive bijection and uniform-refinement proofs with all 1,024 original witness forms. Together these results complete the registered Part Equivalence entry.


For ordered source lists A,B, write P(A,B) = concat_(a in A)[label(a,b): b in B]. The lifted selections are L_A = P(A_held,B_all) and L_B = P(A_all,B_held). The source generates index pairs by zipping [0,...,|L_A|-1] with [0,...,|L_B|-1]. Every generated index is bounded, so its acceptance condition reduces exactly to

\[
 \operatorname{hasGeneratedBijection}(L_A,L_B)=\mathrm{true}
 \iff |L_A|=|L_B|.
\]

With equal lengths n, the generated pairs are [(i,i): 0 ≤ i < n]; every left and right index occurs. The forward and backward maps satisfy g(f(i)) = i and f(g(j)) = j. For a uniform descendant type C, the refined maps (a,c) ↦ (f(a),c) and (b,c) ↦ (g(b),c) retain both inverse identities. On the ordered source lists, induction over A and distribution of length over append prove that equal source lengths give equal lengths of P(A,C), hence preserve the generated pairing. Origin cells satisfy (a,b) ∈ originCells(A,B) iff a ∈ A and b ∈ B; labels retain this origin witness without requiring label injectivity.

The original equal-selection control has lifted lengths 1·4 = 2·2 = 4. The unequal control has lengths 1·3 = 3 and 2·1 = 2, so the same predicate rejects it. The complete grammar retains its eight binary choices and one four-way pairing choice, giving 2^8·4 = 1,024 forms.


71. Finite Form source input correspondence

The source validator requires a tuple at a Form root and accesses either tuple or list containers at the held edges. The formalisation retains both containers and their mixed combinations, arbitrary finite child forms and ignored trailing edge items. Its acceptance equivalence characterizes precisely the finite One/Fold productions over this representation. Original short-circuit order preserves false returns and index errors, including distinct tuple and list error messages. Canonicalization validates before recursively emitting the ordered trace, and every accepted represented input has a generated canonical Form. Canonical equality retains the established identity and equivalence laws. Fresh native execution reproduces all 288 Grammar and 192 Enforcement candidate decisions, complete evidence values and hashes. These results complete both registered entries in dependency order.


Let T denote a tuple constructor and E range independently over tuple or list constructors at each edge. The finite acceptance productions are

\[
 \frac{}{\operatorname{Accepted}(T[\text{One}])},\qquad
 \frac{\operatorname{Accepted}(l)\quad\operatorname{Accepted}(r)}
 {\operatorname{Accepted}(T[\text{Fold},E_L[\text{held-a},l,\ldots],
 E_R[\text{held-b},r,\ldots],\text{return}])}.
\]

The trailing items at each edge are arbitrary and ignored, while the outer tuple has exactly the displayed length. Case analysis on the validator's ordered tests extracts one of these productions from a true result. Induction on a production proves the converse, retaining all four tuple/list edge combinations:

\[
 \operatorname{valid}(v)=\operatorname{ok}(\mathrm{true})
 \iff \operatorname{Accepted}(v).
\]

Validation errors propagate unchanged; a false validation returns the source's outside-grammar error before canonical recursion. For accepted inputs the canonical trace C obeys

\[
 C(T[\text{One}])=T[\text{One}],\qquad
 C(\operatorname{FoldRaw}(l,r))=
 T[\text{Fold},\text{held-a},C(l),\text{held-b},C(r),\text{return}].
\]

Here FoldRaw denotes any of the four admitted edge-container combinations above. Induction on the acceptance production constructs f = One in the base case and f = Fold(f_l,f_r) in the recursive case. Thus valid(v) = ok(true) implies the existence of a finite Form f with canonical(v) = ok(canonicalTrace(f)). This derivation preserves both child order and error short-circuiting.

Write t(x) for host truth testing. For Boolean protocol fields p (target before seal), m (measurement changes law), r (all rows) and s (identified source), the exact acceptance theorem is

\[
 A(p,m,r,s)=\neg p\land\neg m\land r\land s,\qquad
 A(p,m,r,s)=\mathrm{true}\iff
 p=\mathrm{false}\land m=\mathrm{false}\land r=\mathrm{true}\land s=\mathrm{true}.
\]

For general literal host values, define x & y = y when t(x) is true and x otherwise. The source expression is ((¬t(p) & ¬t(m)) & r) & s, where negated truth values are Boolean host values. Splitting on the first two truth tests gives the exact operand-returning equation

\[
 A_{\rm host}(p,m,r,s)=
 \begin{cases}
 \mathrm{false},&t(p)\lor t(m),\\
 r,&\neg t(p)\land\neg t(m)\land\neg t(r),\\
 s,&\neg t(p)\land\neg t(m)\land t(r).
 \end{cases}
\]

Truth testing is false for null, zero, empty text and empty arrays/objects; it is true for nonzero integers and nonempty containers/text, and agrees with a Boolean's value. Consequently A_host(false,false,true,"source-id") = "source-id" and A_host(false,false,[],"source-id") = []. Restricting all four operands to Booleans recovers A by exhaustive Boolean cases, without coercing arbitrary source identifiers to Boolean outputs.


74. Finite incidence and open-family derivations

The incidence predicate accepts exactly a nonempty distinct cell carrier and a distinct boundary relation whose endpoints belong to that carrier and whose pairs are not self-incidences. The adjacency predicate, which does not itself impose the incidence admission guard, accepts exactly two different cells with a shared retained face. General native proofs establish this equivalence, symmetry and irreflexivity.

Each proposed open is projected onto carrier order and an empty projection is represented by the original empty-One literal. The finite topology check retains the original canonical-set cardinality comparison, presence of empty One and the whole carrier, and exhaustive pairwise union/intersection closure. Its acceptance theorem states precisely these conditions. Structural induction then establishes membership of every finite generated join and intersection of admitted canonical opens. Off-carrier labels and collisions with the literal empty-One marker retain their original executable behavior.

Continuity constructs its image map with the last supplied row for each key, requires exactly the source point keys, and tests each target open's source-ordered inverse image against the supplied source open family. No extra topology validation or normalization of the supplied source family is inserted. Native proofs establish the exact key-support and inverse-image conditions and last-row precedence. The original incidence, adjacency, finite-topology and continuity witnesses hold. The path-search operation, returned-path soundness, general minimality and unreachable-case equivalence are established below.


For a carrier list C and boundary list B, the admission and adjacency equivalences are

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{Inc}(C,B)\iff{}& C\ne[]\land\operatorname{Distinct}(C)
 \land\operatorname{Distinct}(B)\\
 &\land\forall(a,b)\in B,\ a\in C\land b\in C\land a\ne b,\\
 \operatorname{Adj}_B(x,y)\iff{}&x\ne y\land\exists f,\ (f,x)\in B\land(f,y)\in B.
 \end{aligned}
\]

The ordered face list is F_B(c) = [a : (a,c) occurs in B]. Swapping the two face-membership witnesses proves adjacency symmetry; x ≠ x excludes self-adjacency. Neither implication inserts an incidence guard into Adj.

Let e denote the original empty-One literal, and write E(L) = e if L = [], otherwise L. For a point list P, define

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 N_P(U)&=E([x\in P\mid x\in U]),\\
 U\mathbin{\vee_P}V&=E([x\in P\mid x\in U\lor x\in V]),\\
 U\mathbin{\wedge_P}V&=E([x\in P\mid x\in U\land x\in V]),\\
 K&=\operatorname{distinctWords}([N_P(U):U\in\mathcal O]).
 \end{aligned}
\]

These are ordered-list operations with the source literal e, including its original collision behavior. The topology predicate is exactly

\[
 P\ne[]\land\operatorname{Distinct}(P)\land |K|=|\mathcal O|
 \land e\in K\land P\in K
 \land\forall U,V\in K,\ U\vee_P V\in K\land U\wedge_P V\in K.
\]

The generated join has J([])=e and J(U::R)=U∨_P J(R); the generated intersection has I([])=P and I(U::R)=U∧_P I(R). For a list R of members of K, induction begins with e∈K or P∈K and applies the displayed binary closure at each cons, proving J(R),I(R)∈K.

For a supplied mapping list M, f_M(x) is the last value whose key is x, if present. Hence f_(M++[(x,y)])(x)=y. Define the inverse-open list by V_M(U)=E([x∈P : f_M(x) is present and belongs to U]). Then the source continuity predicate is precisely

\[
 \operatorname{keys}(M)\subseteq P\ \land\ P\subseteq\operatorname{keys}(M)
 \ \land\ \forall U\in\mathcal O_{\rm target},\ V_M(U)\in\mathcal O_{\rm source}.
\]

The inclusions express list membership, so duplicate mapping keys remain admitted and use last-row precedence. The supplied source family is tested directly.


75. Boundary-depth dimension

For a registered finite incidence complex, the dimension function returns one for a cell with no faces and one plus the maximum of the recursively derived face dimensions otherwise. The active descent stack rejects a return to a cell already on that stack. Independent branches share the parent stack, preserving the original push/pop behavior; an unrelated cycle outside the queried descent does not cause rejection. Termination follows constructively by removing each newly visited label from the finite support. This support introduces no depth bound or scientific parameter.

A native inductive derivation records every face in source order, its own complete derivation and the maximum rule at the parent. The successful computation is equivalent in both directions to this derivation for every admitted input and result. All successful dimensions are positive; the original carrier-admission and return-cycle error conditions are retained. Execution reproduces the original triangle dimension of three and edge dimension of two, together with leaf, invalid-carrier, reachable-cycle, unrelated-cycle and shared-descendant cases.


With visiting stack S and ordered faces F_B(c)=[f_1,...,f_k], successful depth evaluation follows

\[
 d_S(c)=
 \begin{cases}
 \operatorname{error}(\text{return cycle}),&c\in S,\\
 1+\max(0,d_{S++[c]}(f_1),\ldots,d_{S++[c]}(f_k)),&c\notin S,
 \end{cases}
\]

where any child error is propagated in source order before a numerical maximum is returned. The maximum of an empty child list is zero, giving a leaf dimension of one. Every child receives S++[c], rather than the final stack from a sibling branch.

For successful children with ordered heights h_1,...,h_k, the native derivation rule is

\[
 \frac{c\notin S\qquad
       D_B(S++[c],f_1,h_1)\quad\cdots\quad D_B(S++[c],f_k,h_k)}
 {D_B(S,c,1+\max(0,h_1,\ldots,h_k))}.
\]

The auxiliary support initially contains c followed by all first endpoints of B. At a fresh node, removing every occurrence of c strictly decreases its length; each child remains covered by unseen support or the visiting stack. Well-founded induction therefore yields both directions

\[
 \operatorname{boundaryDepth}(B,S,c)=\operatorname{ok}(h)
 \iff D_B(S,c,h).
\]

Here boundaryDepth denotes a state carrying the stated support-coverage invariant. Under Inc(C,B) and c∈C, the public dimension call starts with S=[] and has the same equivalence. Every successful h is a successor and thus h>0. In the original witness, p,q,r have no faces and dimension 1; pq and qr have dimension 1+max(1,1)=2; triangle has dimension 1+max(2,2)=3.


76. Ordered undirected path search

The path-distance procedure checks only membership of its two endpoints in the supplied carrier. Equal endpoints return the original empty-One value. For different endpoints, the queue stores both each held node and its full path. A newly visited node scans the original edge list in order, extending only to unvisited neighbors; the first target extension is returned. Pending extensions retain duplicate occurrences and source order. Edge labels outside the supplied carrier remain traversable, as in the source procedure.

The formal implementation has a constructive termination proof over unseen edge-endpoint support and queue length. Its extension decision is proved equivalent to the literal early-return edge scan. Every returned nonidentity path has a native inductive trace beginning at the origin and extended by original undirected incidences to the target. Endpoint rejection, empty-One identity, exhausted queue, visited-node skipping and fresh expansion are explicit native laws. Source-case execution preserves reversed-edge traversal, off-carrier intermediate labels, self-edges, duplicate queue entries and edge-order tie selection. Exhaustive checks on all 64 directed edge subsets over three labels and all 576 endpoint pairs agree with independent enumeration of simple paths for reachability and minimal length. The finite checks accompany the general shortest-path and unreachable-case theorems below.


For each source edge (a,b), the neighbor of u is b when a=u, otherwise a when b=u, and absent otherwise. Let N_E(u,V) be the resulting ordered neighbor list after removing members of V; duplicate occurrences remain. For queue head (u,p), tail Q and visited list V, put V'=V++[u]. The loop equations are

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 L(V,[]) &= \mathrm{None},\\
 L(V,(u,p)::Q)&=L(V,Q) &&(u\in V),\\
 L(V,(u,p)::Q)&=\mathrm{Some}(p++[t]) &&(u\notin V,\ t\in N_E(u,V')),\\
 L(V,(u,p)::Q)&=L\bigl(V',Q++[(v,p++[v]):v\in N_E(u,V')]\bigr)
 &&(u\notin V,\ t\notin N_E(u,V')).
 \end{aligned}
\]

The initial queue is [(o,[o])]. The unseen support initially contains o and both endpoint lists of E. Skipping a visited head reduces queue length; fresh expansion removes its node from unseen support. Thus (|unseen|,|queue|) strictly decreases lexicographically on every recursive call.

Returned-path soundness uses the constructors

\[
 \frac{}{T_E(o,o,[o])},\qquad
 \frac{T_E(o,u,p)\quad((u,v)\in E\lor(v,u)\in E)}{T_E(o,v,p++[v])}.
\]

Every queued extension has this trace, so a target extension does too. The public endpoint guard is o,t∈C; it returns an error otherwise, returns Some(e) for o=t, and invokes L for distinct endpoints. This guard imposes no carrier-membership test on intermediate edge endpoints.


77. General shortest-path and reachability derivations

The search queue preserves nondecreasing path lengths and a width of at most one edge. When a node is visited, every adjacent node that remains unvisited has a queued route bounded by any path to that visited node followed by the edge. This invariant, together with the retained origin route, supplies a frontier route no longer than any proposed trace to an unvisited endpoint. Since the target is returned upon discovery and is never queued, the final extension is no longer than any alternative trace to that target.

For every finite source edge list and distinct admitted endpoints, every undirected trace therefore yields a successful returned path of no greater length. Combined with returned-path soundness, this proves minimality of the actual result against all finite undirected alternatives. The procedure returns None if and only if no such trace exists. The proof retains the original carrier guard and does not require unique edges, globally admitted edge endpoints, or exclusion of loops. Equal endpoints retain the separate source identity result, empty One. These statements are general native proofs, with zero axiom dependencies; they do not depend on a finite regression bound.


For a queue Q, define B_Q(v,n) to mean that some (v,p)∈Q has |p|≤n. The preserved frontier conditions are

\[
 o\in V\lor B_Q(o,1),\qquad
 \forall u\in V,\ T_E(o,u,p)\land(u\sim_E v)
 \Rightarrow v\in V\lor B_Q(v,|p|+1),
\]

where u∼_E v means either orientation occurs in E. Queue order requires each head path to be no longer than every later path; queue width requires |p_2|≤|p_1|+1 for every two queued paths. Skipping a visited head preserves frontier coverage. Fresh expansion supplies each still-unvisited neighbor with the extended head path and retains the old queue tail; the width bound ensures the appended extensions preserve order.

Induction on T_E(o,v,p) gives a queued path of length at most |p| whenever v∉V. If v is also absent from the queue, the stronger bound is |q|+1≤|p|: the last trace edge either has a visited predecessor, which would force v to be queued, or has an unvisited predecessor to which the induction bound applies. The target is never queued because it is returned immediately upon discovery. At that return, queue order gives

\[
 |p_{\rm head}++[t]|=|p_{\rm head}|+1\le |q|+1\le |p_{\rm alternative}|.
\]

Consequently, for distinct admitted endpoints, every alternative trace yields a successful result r with |r|≤|p_alternative|. Determinism identifies r with any already returned result, proving minimality. Finally,

\[
 \operatorname{pathDistance}(C,E,o,t)=\operatorname{ok}(\mathrm{None})
 \iff\neg\exists p,\ T_E(o,t,p)
 \qquad(o,t\in C,\ o\ne t).
\]

A trace contradicts None by completeness; a successful result contradicts absence of traces by soundness. These implications quantify over arbitrary finite edge lists and finite alternative traces.

The ten registered binary dimensions generate all 1,024 kernels in the original order. Connectedness and reversible incidence-preserving deformation retain their original named criteria and source explanations; the source entry introduces no separate executable procedure for those clauses.

Together with the general incidence, dimension, adjacency, shortest-path, topology and continuity derivations, these results complete the registered Geometry/Topology entry. This entry is included in the cumulative completed census reported in the model and scope section.


Writing each registered coordinate as admitted or rejected, the full geometry grammar is the ordered product of the ten two-element coordinate lists. Thus

\[
 |\mathcal G|=\prod_{i=1}^{10}2=1,024,\qquad
 \operatorname{accept}(c)=\mathrm{true}\iff
 \bigwedge_{i=1}^{10}(c_i=\mathrm{admitted})\iff c=c_*.
\]

The coordinates are cells, incidence, dimension, adjacency, distance, opens, connectedness, continuity, deformation and addition. 79. Exact events and common-refinement weights

An admitted event preserves its supplied order and is a duplicate-free held selection of a complete nonempty support. The native admission results establish support positivity and uniqueness, event uniqueness and inclusion, and the bound of held cardinality by support cardinality. Empty events retain the source empty-One marker. The original literal-marker comparison is preserved when a registered state itself has the label empty-One.

A nonmarker event weight is the reduced positive held-count/whole-count fraction. Native proofs establish exact cross-multiplication equality with the original counts, positive numerator, coprime numerator and denominator, and the through-One bound. Conditioning first validates the event and then the condition in the original order. Its common refinement selects exactly the support states belonging to both held sets; an empty refinement or marker condition returns empty One. Every fractional result is the exact refinement-count/condition-count ratio and is bounded by the whole. No fitted quantity, stochastic premise or additional scientific parameter enters these operations. The observation-class construction and complete probability program are included below.


For support S and held list H, admission is exactly the conjunction S≠[], Distinct(S), Distinct(H), and H⊆S, with support checked before H. Its event result is e when H=[] and H otherwise, where e is the source empty-One literal. For nonmarker results, let a=|H|, b=|S|, g=gcd(a,b). Reduction gives

\[
 n=a/g,\qquad d=b/g,\qquad ad=nb,\qquad
 n>0,\quad d>0,\quad\gcd(n,d)=1,\quad n\le d.
\]

The cardinality bound a≤b is constructive: for H=x::R, remove x from S; distinctness ensures R is still contained in that remainder, induction bounds |R|, and removal strictly decreases |S|. Thus |H|≤|S|, and positive-fraction normalization preserves the through-One bound. The marker branch is tested on the returned event value itself, so a nonempty held list equal to e retains the original marker behavior.

For admitted event H and condition C, the common-refinement list is

\[
 R=[x\in S\mid x\in H\land x\in C],\qquad
 x\in R\iff x\in S\land x\in H\land x\in C.
\]

After validating H and then C, the procedure returns e if the condition result equals e or R=[]. Otherwise it reduces |R|/|C| to n/d and proves

\[
 |R|d=n|C|,\qquad 0<n\le d,\qquad\gcd(n,d)=1.
\]

Filtering preserves distinctness of S and gives R⊆C, so the same cardinality argument yields |R|≤|C|. The original first error is propagated before any later condition check.


80. Pair-cell factorization and exact modal summaries

The independence operation extracts marginal labels in their first-occurrence order and compares the supplied joint support with their complete ordered Cartesian product. The native product-filter identity establishes that retaining pair cells by both held-event predicates yields the Cartesian product of the retained marginal cells. Its cardinality is the product of the two retained marginal cardinalities. For duplicate-free held selections contained in their marginal supports, these cardinalities equal the supplied held counts; cross-multiplication therefore proves exact joint-weight factorization. The operational procedure retains the source rejection of incomplete or differently ordered products, its nonempty-event guard, raw event-list lengths and empty-joint division error.

The modal summary counts every occurrence in the complete trace, forms the greatest count over first-occurrence labels and retains every label attaining it in that order. Native proofs characterize membership exactly as trace membership with frequency at least that of every other trace label. Every nonempty trace has a modal label, the output contains no duplicate labels, and every tied alternative is retained. The empty trace retains the original rejection. These operations introduce no stochastic parameter.


Let A and B be the first-occurrence marginal lists extracted from joint J, and let P(A,B) list every (a,b) in outer-A, inner-B order. For held lists H,K, filter distribution gives the exact ordered identity

\[
 [ (a,b)\in P(A,B)\mid a\in H\land b\in K]
 =P([a\in A\mid a\in H],[b\in B\mid b\in K]).
\]

Induction over A proves the identity row by row; distributing length over append gives |P(A,B)|=|B|·|A|. When H,K are distinct and contained in their respective distinct supports, each filtered marginal has the held cardinality. Hence, for the complete ordered product J=P(A,B), with J_HK the retained joint list,

\[
 |J_{HK}|=|K|\,|H|,\qquad |J|=|B|\,|A|,
 \qquad |J_{HK}|\,|A|\,|B|=|H|\,|K|\,|J|.
\]

For nonempty admitted events and joint support, this is the exact source cross-multiplication test for joint-weight factorization. The executable procedure still uses raw |H| and |K| outside these theorem hypotheses: it does not silently deduplicate them. Its preliminary ordered-product equality test also retains the original rejection of reordered joint lists.

For a trace X, define c_X(a)=|[x∈X:x=a]| and let L be its first-occurrence labels. The maximum recurrence and mode list are

\[
 m_X([])=0,\qquad m_X(a::R)=\max(c_X(a),m_X(R)),\qquad
 M(X)=[a\in L\mid c_X(a)=m_X(L)].
\]

Induction establishes that m_X(L) bounds every label count and is at most any common upper bound. Therefore

\[
 a\in M(X)\iff a\in X\land\forall b\in X,\ c_X(b)\le c_X(a).
\]

For nonempty L, the recurrence attains its maximum either at its head or at the inductively selected tail label, proving existence of a mode. Filtering a distinct first-occurrence list preserves distinctness and order. If c_X(b)=c_X(a) for a retained a and b∈X, the same maximality inequality proves b is retained, including every tie.


81. Observation partitions and completed finite probability entry

An observation assigns each admitted support state its final dictionary value. Repeated mapping keys retain their last value, and admission requires equality of mapping-key and support sets. Native proofs establish that every support state has an image and that the image trace retains the support length. Distinct class labels are taken in first-occurrence order. Each class consists exactly of the support rows mapped to its label, retaining their original order and multiplicity. Every support state is covered, each emitted class is nonempty, and a state cannot belong to classes with different labels. Observation therefore closes distinctions between deterministic support states without adding a stochastic evolution law.

The complete nine-dimensional source product contains 512 candidate kernels. Native enumeration and criteria proofs establish the unique all-admitted kernel and rejection of each substituted coordinate. All six original operational witnesses evaluate successfully; their joint passing result is kernel checked. Native execution matches every source registration, census, decision, closure, control and digest field for all 512 identifier lookups, and preserves unknown-identifier refusal. The original induction, derivation exposition, correspondence terminology and empirical exclusions are retained. These results complete the V3 finite probability and descriptive-statistics entry. Inferential claims about natural measurements retain V3's requirement for preregistered blind validation through the empirical engine.


Write f_M(x) for the final mapping value at key x. Admission is the pair of membership inclusions keys(M)⊆S and S⊆keys(M). Induction on the mapping list proves

\[
 (\exists y,\ f_M(x)=\operatorname{Some}(y))\iff x\in\operatorname{keys}(M).
\]

Thus every admitted support row contributes exactly one value to the image trace Y, and induction on S gives |Y|=|S|. Define L=firstLabels(Y) and F_y=[x∈S:f_M(x)=Some(y)]. The emitted rows are [(y,F_y):y∈L], with exact membership law

\[
 (y,T)\in\operatorname{classRows}(S,M)
 \iff (\exists x\in S,\ f_M(x)=\operatorname{Some}(y))\land T=F_y.
\]

Every emitted row therefore has a witness x and is nonempty. For each x∈S, the admitted mapping supplies y and hence its emitted class F_y containing x. If x∈F_y and x∈F_z, then Some(y)=f_M(x)=Some(z), so y=z. This proves coverage and separation of differently labelled classes while retaining support order and any repeated support occurrences. The observation operation's key-set guard does not add a distinctness requirement to its supplied support list.

The nine binary probability coordinates give 2^9=512 kernels. The conjunction of their original admission criteria holds exactly at the all-admitted tuple; each rejected coordinate falsifies its corresponding conjunct. The finite classification and operational witness results accompany the general event, factorization, mode and partition derivations above.


82. Exact finite optimization and retained alternatives

The optimization carrier is nonempty and canonical. Its feasibility relation retains the final verdict for every candidate key and must cover exactly the carrier's keys. The feasible selection preserves candidate order and contains precisely the candidates with true verdicts. A preference cell must join two distinct feasible forms. The undominated selection retains every feasible occurrence with no incoming preference cell; a native theorem supplies a distinct feasible dominator for every excluded feasible form. No acyclicity or extra ordering assumption is imposed by the source procedure.

A unique optimum is returned exactly when the complete held list is a singleton. Every other result retains the original equivalence-class tag and full list, including ties, incomparable alternatives and an empty result from a preference cycle. Constraints require membership in every supplied allowed set. Pareto dominance requires every criterion to admit the directed comparison and at least one to witness strict preference; self-comparisons cannot eliminate candidates. Native frontier membership and elimination-witness results establish complete selection and retention of incomparable forms, including the original empty-criteria behavior.

The approximation certificate accepts the original signed-integer domain and rejects a nonpositive optimum or candidate count below that optimum. Every accepted result is the reduced positive optimum-count/candidate-count part. Native ratio and through-One results establish exactness without rounding, negative penalties or a tuning parameter. Shared-interface decomposition retains the source's complete-factorization condition; no additional decomposition algorithm is introduced.

All 512 kernels in the original nine-dimensional grammar are retained, with native enumeration, criteria, uniqueness and coordinate-rejection proofs. All six original operational witness computations pass. Full native program execution matches every pinned registration, candidate, decision, closure, control and digest field and the unknown-identifier error. These results complete the finite optimization entry; complexity and particular objective-family laws retain their original placement in the algorithms and complexity branches.


For canonical nonempty carrier C and verdict rows R, the key guard is keys(R)⊆C and C⊆keys(R). With v_R(x) the last verdict at x, feasibility is the ordered filter

\[
 F=[x\in C\mid v_R(x)=\operatorname{Some}(\mathrm{true})],\qquad
 v_{R++[(x,b)]}(x)=\operatorname{Some}(b).
\]

A preference list P is admitted exactly when every (a,b)∈P has a,b∈F and a≠b. For nonempty F, its retained list U obeys

\[
 x\in U\iff x\in F\land\forall y,\ (y,x)\notin P.
\]

If x∈F but x∉U, the failed filter supplies a pair (y,x)∈P; preference admission then gives y∈F and y≠x. This witness need not itself be undominated: cyclic preferences retain their original possibility of U=[]. Classification returns unique(x) iff U=[x]; otherwise it returns the source retained-equivalence-class tag with U unchanged. Constraints [(name_i,A_i)] accept x iff x∈A_i for every supplied row.

For criteria Q=[(name_i,P_i)], the source dominance predicate is

\[
 D_Q(a,b)\iff
 [\forall i,\ (a,b)\in P_i\lor a=b]\land
 [\exists i,\ (a,b)\in P_i].
\]

The Pareto filter separately excludes self-elimination and has the exact law

\[
 x\in\operatorname{Frontier}(F,Q)\iff
 x\in F\land\forall y\in F,\ y\ne x\Rightarrow\neg D_Q(y,x).
\]

An excluded feasible x has a distinct feasible y satisfying D_Q(y,x), obtained from the source existential test. If each distinct competitor lacks the directed pair in at least one criterion, its universal dominance clause fails and x remains. For Q=[], the existential clause is false, so Frontier(F,[])=F.

The signed-integer approximation call accepts exactly 0<o≤c. For accepted counts, put g=gcd(o,c), n=o/g and d=c/g. The normalized part satisfies

\[
 od=nc,\qquad 0<n\le d,\qquad\gcd(n,d)=1.
\]

Negative inputs, o=0 and o>c take the original error branch; no rounding or selected numerical tolerance enters the ratio. The nine binary grammar coordinates enumerate 2^9=512 candidates; their criteria conjunction accepts only the all-admitted tuple, with each substituted rejected coordinate falsifying its own conjunct.


83. Generated trajectories and predecessor records

Finite dynamics admission requires a nonempty canonical state list, distinct retained transition cells and both endpoints of every cell inside the state carrier. Trajectory validation has the separate original domain: it requires a nonempty trace and each adjacent ordered pair in the supplied transition relation. It introduces no additional carrier-membership guard. Native membership results establish these exact conditions, and composition at a shared canonical interface is equivalent to validity of both component trajectories.

Elapsed time retains the complete ordered adjacent-transition trace. Its length is one fewer than the number of retained states; every trace with at least two states has positive transition count. A singleton trajectory has empty-One duration and an empty trajectory is rejected. Fixed forms retain precisely the source rows with identical source and target, while predecessor records retain every source row mapped to the requested target, preserving order and multiplicity.

Record-based reversibility requires inclusion of every original transition in a duplicate-free retained ledger. Native results show that each source predecessor remains recoverable from that ledger and that omission of any original transition fails the check. Extra retained rows remain governed by the original inclusion test; no stronger equality condition is imposed. First-return selection, registered-perturbation reachability and the complete dynamical program are included below.


Let S be the state list, E the ordered transition list, and A(p) the ordered adjacent pairs of trajectory p. The admission predicates are

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{Dynamics}(S,E)\iff{}&S\ne[]\land\operatorname{Distinct}(S)
 \land\operatorname{Distinct}(E)\land\forall(a,b)\in E,\ a,b\in S,\\
 \operatorname{Trajectory}_E(p)\iff{}&p\ne[]\land\forall e\in A(p),\ e\in E.
 \end{aligned}
\]

Thus [x] is a valid trajectory for any x, since its adjacent-pair list is empty. The empty trajectory is rejected. For lists L,R and common interface x,

\[
 A(L++[x]++R)=A(L++[x])++A(x::R).
\]

Distributing universal membership over this append proves trajectory validity of the joined list iff both component trajectories are valid. This establishes composition without adding a state-carrier guard.

The time operation retains the actual adjacent transition list:

\[
 \operatorname{Time}([x])=e,\qquad
 \operatorname{Time}(x::y::R)=(x,y)::A(y::R),\qquad
 |A(x::R)|=|R|.
\]

The final equation follows by induction on R: the empty tail gives zero and each cons contributes one adjacent pair. Hence a trajectory with at least two states has positive transition count. The source distinguishes the singleton marker e=["empty-One"] from a transition list; Time([]) returns its original error.

Filtering E before projecting its first component gives

\[
 \operatorname{Fixed}(E)=[a:(a,b)\in E,\ a=b],\qquad
 \operatorname{Pred}_E(t)=[a:(a,t)\text{ occurs in }E].
\]

Therefore x∈Fixed(E) iff (x,x)∈E, and x∈Pred_E(t) iff (x,t)∈E, while the lists retain source order and repeated occurrences. For a retained ledger L, the exact reversibility test is

\[
 \operatorname{Reversible}(E,L)\iff E\subseteq L\land\operatorname{Distinct}(L).
\]

If (x,t)∈E and the test passes, inclusion gives (x,t)∈L and hence x∈Pred_L(t). If an original edge is missing from L, inclusion is false and the test fails. Extra ledger rows are not excluded by this predicate.


84. First returns and complete registered perturbation stability

The first-return operation scans terminal positions in source order and selects the earliest equal prior state. The returned trace is the exact inclusive slice between those positions. Native proofs establish that the prefix before the repeated terminal contains no duplicate states and that no matching state precedes the selected starting occurrence. No return is produced exactly when the entire trajectory is duplicate-free. Every returned slice has at least two state occurrences with equal endpoints; when the original trajectory is valid, the slice retains valid transitions throughout.

Registered perturbation stability uses the original directed breadth-first queue. The target is checked before the visited test; visiting appends the current state, and successors are appended in transition-row order with the source duplicate behavior retained. A constructive finite-support measure proves termination without an arbitrary search bound. The native equivalence proves successful search exactly when a finite retained transition derivation reaches the terminal. Stability holds precisely when this is true for every registered perturbation. Empty perturbation lists retain the original vacuous result, and identity reachability requires no additional state-carrier admission.

All 1,024 kernels in the ten-dimensional source grammar are retained and verified. All seven original operational witnesses pass, with stability obtained from explicit transition derivations. Full native execution matches every original registration, candidate decision, closure, control and digest field, including unknown-identifier refusal. Together with the trajectory and predecessor results, these complete the exact finite Dynamical Systems entry. Continuum differential descriptions retain their original status as possible measured or finite-approximation correspondences, not premises of the entry.


Let suffix(x,S) return the suffix beginning at the first occurrence of x in S, or None if x is absent. The return scan obeys

\[
 R(S,[])=\mathrm{None},\qquad
 R(S,x::T)=
 \begin{cases}
 \mathrm{Some}(H++[x]),&\operatorname{suffix}(x,S)=\mathrm{Some}(H),\\
 R(S++[x],T),&x\notin S.
 \end{cases}
\]

Starting with S=[], every recursive step appends a fresh state, so S remains distinct until the first successful suffix lookup. Induction on the unscanned tail gives

\[
 \operatorname{firstReturn}(p)=\mathrm{None}\iff\operatorname{Distinct}(p).
\]

If the result is Some(h), the same induction and the first-suffix theorem construct lists B,M,A and terminal x such that

\[
 p=(B++x::M)++x::A,\qquad h=(x::M)++[x],\qquad
 \operatorname{Distinct}(B++x::M),\qquad x\notin B.
\]

Thus no terminal repetition occurs before the displayed second x, and the selected starting x has no earlier matching occurrence. The returned length is |M|+2. For a valid trajectory p, applying the trajectory-composition equivalence first at the repeated terminal and then at the selected start proves validity of h.

For directed transitions E, write O_E(u,V)=[b:(u,b) occurs in E and b∉V], retaining row order and duplicates. The stability search with target t has equations

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 B_t(V,[])&=\mathrm{false},\\
 B_t(V,u::Q)&=\mathrm{true} &&(u=t),\\
 B_t(V,u::Q)&=B_t(V,Q) &&(u\ne t,\ u\in V),\\
 B_t(V,u::Q)&=B_t(V++[u],Q++O_E(u,V++[u])) &&(u\ne t,\ u\notin V).
 \end{aligned}
\]

The initial frontier is [s]. The finite support is s followed by all transition targets; fresh expansion removes u from unseen support, while visited-node skipping shortens the frontier. The lexicographic pair (|unseen|,|frontier|) therefore decreases at each recursive call.

The transition derivation is generated by s↝_E s and the rule s↝_E u, (u,v)∈E ⇒ s↝_E v. Every queued node has such a derivation, proving successful search sound. Conversely, failed search supplies an exhausted visited set containing s, closed under E and excluding t. Induction on a derivation s↝_E t puts t in that set, a contradiction. Hence

\[
 B_t([],[s])=\mathrm{true}\iff s\rightsquigarrow_E t,\qquad
 \operatorname{Stable}(E,t,P)=\mathrm{true}\iff\forall s\in P,\ s\rightsquigarrow_E t.
\]

The universal condition is true for P=[], while the identity constructor proves t↝_E t without a carrier guard. Any registered s lacking a derivation falsifies stability. The ten binary source coordinates enumerate 2^10=1,024 kernels, and their admission conjunction selects exactly the all-admitted tuple.


85. Finite logic and registered proof traces

A proposition retains a nonempty distinguished form and one of the two original held orientations. A proof step retains nonempty ordered premises, its conclusion and a nonempty rule name. The native constructors preserve the exact source guards and error cases; round-trip results establish that their represented values retain every original field. Denial switches orientation without changing the form, is involutive and produces a distinct oriented proposition. Conjunction retains both constituents with the original joint tag. Disjunction retains a supplied generated branch and both ordered alternatives, rejecting a branch outside that pair.

The proof checker walks the supplied steps in order. Every premise must already be available, and the complete rule-name/premise-tuple/conclusion record must occur in the registry before the conclusion is appended. Native equivalence establishes that acceptance is exactly an inductive trace satisfying those conditions at every step. Relative soundness follows by induction: any property held by the initial premises and preserved by every applicable registered rule holds for all resulting available propositions. This is relative registered-rule preservation, with no additional logical axiom or authority-based admission.

Consistency checks that no admitted proposition has its held denial also admitted. Registered-grammar completeness separately requires distinct generated propositions and a held decision for each proposition or its denial. These checks retain their original empty and duplicate-list behavior and make no global completeness claim beyond the supplied grammar.

All 1,024 source kernels and all seven operational witnesses are retained and verified. Native program execution matches every pinned registration, census, decision, closure, control and digest field, including unknown-identifier refusal. These results complete the finite generated Logic and Proof entry. Computability, self-reference, undecidability and incompleteness retain their original placement in the later formal-computation branch.


Represent a proposition as p=(f,o), with nonempty form f and orientation o∈{held,complementary-held}. Denial is d(f,o)=(f,flip(o)), so the two orientation cases establish

\[
 d(d(p))=p,\qquad\operatorname{form}(d(p))=\operatorname{form}(p),\qquad d(p)\ne p.
\]

Conjunction returns ("joint-complete",p,q). Disjunction with supplied witness h returns ("held-alternative",h,(p,q)) exactly when h=p or h=q; otherwise it returns the source error. A proof step e=(P,c,r) requires P≠[] and a nonempty rule name r. Reconstructing a proposition or step from these same fields returns the original represented value.

Let A be available propositions, R the registry of complete triples (rule name, ordered premises, conclusion), and E the ordered steps. The checker equation is

\[
 \operatorname{Check}(A,[],R)=\mathrm{true},\qquad
 \operatorname{Check}(A,e::E,R)=
 \begin{cases}
 \mathrm{false},&P_e\not\subseteq A,\\
 \mathrm{false},&P_e\subseteq A\ \land\ (r_e,P_e,c_e)\notin R,\\
 \operatorname{Check}(A++[c_e],E,R),&P_e\subseteq A\ \land\ (r_e,P_e,c_e)\in R.
 \end{cases}
\]

The premise condition is membership of each listed premise; the registry check retains the entire ordered premise tuple. The inductive accepted trace has a done constructor for [] and a step constructor with precisely the two displayed admission premises and an accepted tail at A++[c_e]. Induction on E proves Check(A,E,R)=true iff this accepted trace exists.

For any property Φ of propositions, assume every member of A satisfies Φ and each registered triple preserves Φ from all of its premises to its conclusion. At an accepted step, premise inclusion transfers Φ from A to P_e; the registered preservation hypothesis gives Φ(c_e). Every member of A++[c_e] therefore satisfies Φ. Induction through the accepted tail yields

\[
 \forall q\in\operatorname{afterSteps}(A,E),\ \Phi(q).
\]

This is the stated relative preservation theorem with its hypotheses retained explicitly. The two separate checks have exact meanings

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{Consistent}(A)&\iff\forall p\in A,\ d(p)\notin A,\\
 \operatorname{GrammarComplete}(G,D)&\iff\operatorname{Distinct}(G)
 \land\forall p\in G,\ p\in D\lor d(p)\in D.
 \end{aligned}
\]

An opposing pair directly falsifies consistency. Empty proof traces are accepted and an empty generated grammar satisfies its completeness check. The latter check does not add consistency of D as another condition. The ten binary source dimensions give 2^10=1,024 candidate kernels with the original unique all-admitted tuple.


86. Compositional paths and finite type constructors

The native arrow constructor retains name, source, target and the complete nonempty trace, with precisely the original nonempty-field admission rule. Identity constructs the original empty-One return record. Composition rejects unequal interfaces; every successful composite retains the first source, second target and both traces in their original order. Canonical paths remove exactly those items whose text begins with the registered empty-One-return prefix. Canonicalization distributes over trace concatenation. Consequently both successful groupings of three arrows have the same interfaces and canonical transition path, by constructive list association.

Type membership uses the complete source key-set comparison and the last registered verdict for each key. It preserves carrier order and repeated occurrences. The product is the full ordered pair support, with membership equivalent to membership in both factors. The sum retains the original left-held and right-held tags; its membership theorem identifies exactly the corresponding source alternative. Empty and repeated carriers retain the original source behavior. These results formalise the path-association and finite type-constructor components of the registered compositional entry.


Write an arrow as a=(n_a,s_a,t_a,T_a). Its constructor admits it exactly when the name, source, target and trace are nonempty. Composition in the order a then b first requires t_a=s_b and then applies this same constructor to

\[
 J(a,b)=(n_b\mathbin{+}\text{∘}\mathbin{+}n_a,\ s_a,\ t_b,\ T_a++T_b).
\]

For a trace T, let K(T) filter out every item with the source byte prefix "empty-One-return". This includes every matching prefix, rather than only the exact identity record. Filter distribution over append proves

\[
 K(T_a++T_b)=K(T_a)++K(T_b).
\]

For successful three-arrow compositions, both groupings have source s_a and target t_c. Their canonical paths agree because

\[
 (K(T_a)++K(T_b))++K(T_c)
 =K(T_a)++(K(T_b)++K(T_c)),
\]

by induction establishing list-append associativity. This is the source's interface and canonical-path equality.

For carrier C and predicate rows R, admission requires keys(R)⊆C and C⊆keys(R), with membership rather than multiplicity comparison. The type list is [x∈C:v_R(x)=Some(true)], where v_R uses the last verdict at x. Thus empty C and R return [], while C=[a,a], R=[(a,false),(a,true)] return [a,a]. For ordered factor lists A,B,

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 A\times B&=\operatorname{concat}_{a\in A}[(a,b):b\in B],\\
 (a,b)\in A\times B&\iff a\in A\land b\in B,\\
 A\mathbin{\sqcup}B&=[(\text{left-held},a):a\in A]++[(\text{right-held},b):b\in B],\\
 (t,x)\in A\mathbin{\sqcup}B&\iff
 (t=\text{left-held}\land x\in A)\lor(t=\text{right-held}\land x\in B).
 \end{aligned}
\]

The product equivalence follows by extracting an outer-list member and then an inner mapped member; its converse constructs those same occurrences. Sum membership splits the append and recovers the exact tag from its mapped pair. Both constructions preserve duplicates and order.


87. Identity, transport and route comparison

For every nonempty source object, the exact original identity constructor succeeds. Its empty-One return contributes no canonical transition. Successful left and right identity compositions retain the other arrow's source, target and canonical path. Composition of source-admitted arrows exists precisely when the first target equals the second source; original field admission and complete trace retention are preserved. These results derive the registered identity and interface conditions constructively.

The native transport predicate retains the exact source procedure: the last object and arrow mapping for each key is used, every supplied arrow must have its name and endpoints mapped, and its image endpoints must equal the mapped endpoints. Native equivalence gives these conditions for every supplied arrow. The predicate retains the source's empty-input behavior and does not introduce additional executable checks.

Naturality replays each supplied nonempty route in order, propagating the original first composition failure. The resulting trace is exactly the concatenation of the input arrow traces. Route comparison succeeds precisely when source, target and the complete canonical traces agree. Empty input retains the source indexing error. All 1,024 original kernels, seven operational witnesses and complete generated-program outputs are verified against the pinned V3 records, completing the registered finite compositional entry.


For nonempty object x, the identity record is

\[
 I_x=(\text{id:}x,\ x,\ x,\ [\text{empty-One-return:}x]),\qquad K(T_{I_x})=[].
\]

The prefix calculation removes its sole trace item. Consequently successful left and right identity compositions preserve the other arrow's interfaces, with canonical paths []++K(T_a)=K(T_a) and K(T_a)++[]=K(T_a). For admitted a,b, their concatenated trace is nonempty, the joined name contains the composition symbol, and the retained source/target are nonempty. Thus J(a,b) is admitted, proving

\[
 (\exists c,\ \operatorname{compose}(a,b)=\operatorname{ok}(c))\iff t_a=s_b.
\]

Let O and M be the last-value object and arrow lookup maps. The transport predicate is exactly

\[
 \forall a\in A,\ \exists b,s,t,\
 M(n_a)=\operatorname{Some}(b)\land O(s_a)=\operatorname{Some}(s)
 \land O(t_a)=\operatorname{Some}(t)\land s_b=s\land t_b=t.
\]

Lookup satisfies lookup(R++[(k,v)],k)=Some(v), by induction on R. Empty A makes the universal predicate true. No additional path-composition or identity test is inserted into this source predicate.

Route replay begins at its first arrow and repeatedly composes with the next. Formally FoldRest(a,[])=ok(a); FoldRest(a,b::R) propagates a composition error immediately, or recurses on its successful result and R. The empty route gives the original tuple-index error. On every successful nonempty route [a_1,...,a_n], induction using trace retention gives

\[
 s_{\rm result}=s_{a_1},\qquad
 T_{\rm result}=T_{a_1}++\cdots++T_{a_n}.
\]

The left route is replayed before the right. Once both return l,r, the comparison succeeds exactly when

\[
 s_l=s_r\land t_l=t_r\land K(T_l)=K(T_r).
\]

The first left failure is propagated without replaying the right; a right failure is propagated after successful left replay. These are the complete source route-comparison conditions. The ten binary grammar coordinates enumerate 2^10=1,024 candidates, with acceptance equivalent to their original all-admitted conjunction.


88. Symbols and observation-relative distinction

A source symbol retains its canonical form and held label as an ordered pair. Alphabet admission requires a nonempty distinct list of these pairs and nonempty fields, with the original guard order and without changing source labels. Observation validation requires a nonempty alphabet, equal row and alphabet counts, distinct observation source pairs, exact source-set coverage and nonempty observation labels.

The native lookup selects the label only when exactly one source row matches the full symbol pair. Constructive results establish that every member of a validated alphabet has exactly one nonempty observed label and that each returned label retains its original observation row. Two source-admitted labels produce a retained distinction exactly when those labels differ. The operation is irreflexive, including its original early return before observation lookup, and is symmetric on a validated alphabet. These results formalise the alphabet, observation-admission and pairwise-distinction components of the registered symbol entry.


For symbol s=(form,label), let Held(s) mean both fields are nonempty. The alphabet constructor checks A≠[] and Distinct(A), then checks Held(s) for every s∈A and returns A unchanged. For observation rows O=[(s_i,l_i)], write K=map(first,O). The separate observation predicate is exactly

\[
 A\ne[]\land |O|=|A|\land\operatorname{Distinct}(K)
 \land K\subseteq A\land A\subseteq K
 \land\forall(s,l)\in O,\ l\ne\text{empty string}.
\]

Define L_O(s)=[l:(s,l) occurs in O], retaining row order and matching the entire symbol pair. Lookup has the exact success equivalence

\[
 \operatorname{observedLabel}(O,s)=\operatorname{ok}(l)
 \iff L_O(s)=[l].
\]

Coverage supplies a row for s∈A, and distinct source keys exclude a second matching row. Thus validation implies a unique lookup result l; its original row (s,l)∈O and the final validation clause imply that l is nonempty. Zero or multiple matching rows retain the original lookup error.

Distinction first returns ok(false) when s=t, even if lookup would fail. Otherwise it looks up s and then t, propagating the first error. For successful labels l,r, its result is

\[
 \operatorname{distinguishable}(O,s,t)=\operatorname{ok}(\operatorname{decide}(l\ne r)).
\]

If s=t, lookup determinism forces l=r, so this equation also agrees with the early-return branch. Inequality symmetry proves symmetry whenever both lookups succeed, and therefore for members of a validated alphabet. Irreflexivity follows directly from the early branch without validation.


89. Observation classes, complete distinction ledgers and extension

Observation classes retain the first occurrence order of the observed labels and the original alphabet order of every source fibre. Constructive results establish that their labels are distinct, their fibres are nonempty, every source symbol occurs in its corresponding fibre, and a symbol cannot belong to two fibres with unequal labels. This partitions the complete admitted alphabet without replacing or erasing microform identity.

The pair ledger generates every original positional pair with the earlier symbol first. Its separate retained and closed passes preserve the source order and original lookup-error behavior. On validated observations both passes succeed, their outputs are disjoint, and their union exhausts the generated pair list. A singleton alphabet has no pair distinction.

Appending a fresh symbol generates one new pair with each prior symbol in the original nested-loop order, while preserving prior pairs. Appending its one observation row supplies its unique nonempty label and preserves every old lookup and distinction; every member of the extended alphabet still has a nonempty observed label. Under coarsening, a previously closed distinction remains closed, and a previously retained distinction closes exactly when its image labels are merged. These results establish the source successor and coarsening clauses.

All 512 original symbol kernels and all six operational witnesses are retained and verified. Fresh native execution matches the pinned registration, census, decisions, closure, controls and digest fields, including unknown-identifier refusal. This completes the registered finite Symbol Distinction entry, preserving its original exclusion of linguistic meaning, occurrence frequencies and downstream information quantities.


Collecting the unique labels in alphabet order produces rows R with map(first,R)=A. Their membership equivalence is

\[
 (s,l)\in R\iff s\in A\land\operatorname{observedLabel}(O,s)=\operatorname{ok}(l).
\]

Let F_l=[s:(s,l) occurs in R] and L=firstLabels(map(second,R)). The class list [(l,F_l):l∈L] has distinct labels. Every l∈L comes from a row, proving F_l is nonempty; every collected row places its symbol in the corresponding fibre. If s∈F_l and s∈F_r, the two successful lookups are equal and hence l=r. Collection and filtering preserve alphabet order rather than replacing symbols with their labels.

The positional-pair generator has the exact recurrence

\[
 P([])=[],\qquad P(a::R)=[(a,b):b\in R]++P(R).
\]

On validated observations, let D(p) be the defined Boolean distinction for each pair. The retained and closed passes return, in generated order,

\[
 U=[p\in P(A):D(p)=\mathrm{true}],\qquad
 C=[p\in P(A):D(p)=\mathrm{false}].
\]

Their membership laws imply U∩C is empty: a shared pair would have both Boolean results. Every generated pair has one of those results, proving exhaustiveness as membership in U or C. This does not assert that concatenating the two passes reconstructs the original interleaved order. A singleton alphabet generates P([a])=[].

For a fresh symbol z, define E_z([])=[] and

\[
 E_z(a::R)=([(a,b):b\in R]++[(a,z)])++E_z(R).
\]

Distributing map over append proves P(A++[z])=E_z(A). Thus every prior pair remains and every old a contributes (a,z), in its original nested-loop position. Appending observation row (z,l) gives

\[
 L_{O++[(z,l)]}(s)=L_O(s)++
 \begin{cases}[l],&z=s,\\\lbrack\rbrack,&z\ne s.\end{cases}
\]

For z∉A and validated O, key coverage gives z∉keys(O), so its new lookup is uniquely l. Old lookups are unchanged because z differs from every old symbol. When l is nonempty, every member of A++[z] consequently has a nonempty label, and each old distinction result is unchanged.

For a coarsening map m and fine/coarse lookups related by l↦m(l), the labelled-distinction equation gives

\[
 D_{\rm fine}(s,t)=\mathrm{true}\land D_{\rm coarse}(s,t)=\mathrm{false}
 \iff l\ne r\land m(l)=m(r).
\]

If l=r, their images are equal and both distinctions are closed. These implications retain the stipulated fine and coarse lookup correspondences. The nine binary symbol coordinates enumerate 2^9=512 kernels, with the original conjunction selecting the all-admitted tuple.


90. Encoding classes and exact inverse decoding

Encoding admission retains the original nonempty, distinct source and code-support lists, complete equal-length source relation, distinct origin keys and code-support membership. Codewords are exact held-label lists; their original empty-word behavior is preserved. Source classes occur in first-use encoding order and retain source order and multiplicity. Native results characterize membership, distinct class keys, source provenance and nonempty used classes on admitted inputs.

The exact decoder exists if and only if every used codeword class contains a single source occurrence. Its ordered inverse relation preserves those codeword keys. Decoder lookup requires exactly one matching image and retains the original error on zero or multiple matches. Constructive proofs establish that applying the resulting decoder to every row of an admitted lossless encoding returns the original canonical source. The raw operations retain their original empty and malformed-input behavior independently of encoding admission.


For source list S, code-support list W and encoding rows E, the exact admission condition is

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 &S\ne[]\land W\ne[]\land\operatorname{Distinct}(S)\land\operatorname{Distinct}(W)\\
 &\land |E|=|S|\land\operatorname{keys}(E)\subseteq S\land S\subseteq\operatorname{keys}(E)\\
 &\land\operatorname{Distinct}(\operatorname{keys}(E))
 \land\forall(s,w)\in E,\ w\in W.
 \end{aligned}
\]

Each word w is a list of held labels; this guard does not require w itself to be nonempty. Put U=firstCodewords(map(second,E)) and F_w=[s∈S:(s,w)∈E]. Then

\[
 s\in F_w\iff s\in S\land(s,w)\in E,\qquad
 (w,T)\in\operatorname{sourceClasses}(S,E)\iff w\in\operatorname{map}(\mathrm{second},E)\land T=F_w.
\]

First-occurrence filtering gives distinct U in encoding order, while each F_w preserves source order. On admitted inputs, every used w has an encoding row whose source lies in S, so every emitted class is nonempty.

The inverse operation traverses these classes in order. It maps (w,[s]) to (w,s), rejects a class of zero or multiple occurrences, and returns the empty decoder on an empty class list. Induction over the class list proves

\[
 (\exists D,\ \operatorname{exactDecoder}(S,E)=\operatorname{Some}(D))
 \iff\forall(w,F)\in\operatorname{sourceClasses}(S,E),\ \exists s,\ F=[s].
\]

On success, the decoder keys equal the class keys in order, and (w,s)∈D iff (w,[s]) is a source class. Decoder lookup succeeds exactly when [s':(w,s') occurs in D]=[s]. For an admitted row (s,w)∈E, source provenance gives s∈F_w; the singleton-class condition forces F_w=[s]. Hence (w,s)∈D. Distinct class keys give exactly one decoder match, establishing

\[
 \operatorname{decode}(w,D)=\operatorname{ok}(s)
 \quad\text{for every }(s,w)\in E.
\]

This is the exact left-inverse result, retaining the admitted-encoding and successful-decoder hypotheses. The raw class/inverse operations retain their separate empty-input behavior.


91. Closed encoding distinctions and exact composition

The closed-distinction ledger enumerates source pairs in their original positional order and compares each source's first encoding image. On admitted encodings the computation is total. A pair occurs in the closed ledger precisely when it is one of the generated source pairs and both members belong to the same retained codeword class. The empty and singleton source cases retain empty ledgers; missing first images retain the original error behavior on unvalidated inputs.

Encoding composition requires distinct second-stage interface keys and an image for every first-stage codeword. Native existence is equivalent to these exact conditions. Every source occurrence remains in its original order, and an output relation holds precisely when a first-stage source-to-codeword relation joins a second-stage codeword-to-output relation. No source alphabet or injectivity condition beyond the original composition guard is added.


Let f_E(s) be the first encoding image for s, when present. The source pair list obeys P([])=[] and P(a::R)=[(a,b):b∈R]++P(R). The closed-pair operation looks up a before b, returns its original StopIteration-derived error if either image is missing, and otherwise tests equality of the two images. On admitted inputs its ordered ledger C therefore satisfies

\[
 (a,b)\in C\iff (a,b)\in P(S)\land
 \exists w,\ a\in F_w\land b\in F_w.
\]

In one direction, a shared class supplies both encoding rows; distinct admitted source keys identify their first images as w. In the other, equal first images supply the two original rows and, with a,b∈S, give membership in F_w. The pair generator returns [] on empty or singleton sources, and the selection retains its source order.

For first-stage E and second-stage rows H, composition admission is exactly

\[
 \operatorname{Distinct}(\operatorname{keys}(H))\land
 \forall(s,w)\in E,\ w\in\operatorname{keys}(H).
\]

Coverage supplies a second-stage image for each row; induction on E constructs the complete output. Conversely, a successful public composition must have passed this guard. The recursive application maps (s,w)::R to (s,f_H(w)) followed by the recursively mapped tail, propagating any missing-image error. Thus a successful output J obeys

\[
 \operatorname{keys}(J)=\operatorname{keys}(E),\qquad
 (s,v)\in J\iff\exists w,\ (s,w)\in E\land(w,v)\in H.
\]

The first equality is an ordered-list identity, preserving repeated source occurrences. For the second, induction supplies the intermediate lookup; distinct second-stage keys make lookup equivalent to the recorded row (w,v). No separate source-alphabet validation or injectivity premise is added to this composition condition.


92. Encoding source extension and finite closure

For a fresh source, appending its one encoding pair leaves every previous source-to-code membership unchanged. Every codeword class retains its original source order and gains the fresh source at the end exactly when it is the newly assigned codeword. The used-codeword set gains precisely that word. A single source and one codeword have the singleton inverse. If an encoding has an exact decoder and the appended word is unused, all old singleton classes remain singleton and the new class is the singleton fresh source; therefore the extended exact decoder exists. If the appended word is already used, its nonempty class gains the fresh source and is no longer singleton; the exact decoder is absent and that merged class remains explicitly recorded.

These source-extension statements hold for arbitrary finite source lists satisfying the original admission and freshness conditions. They do not rely on the witness source size. All 1,024 original encoding kernels and all five operational witnesses are retained. Fresh native execution agrees with every pinned registration, census, decision, closure, control and digest field, including unknown-identifier refusal. The Encoding and Decoding entry is complete within its original finite representation boundary; code-length value, compression, channel reliability and correction remain subsequent source subjects.


Let S'=S++[z] and E'=E++[(z,w)], where z∉S and E is admitted on S. Key coverage implies z∉keys(E). For every old s∈S and word v, splitting membership in the appended relation gives

\[
 (s,v)\in E'\iff(s,v)\in E,\qquad
 (z,v)\in E'\iff v=w.
\]

The new pair cannot match an old source because s≠z. Conversely, no old pair has source z, so its only new image is w. Induction through the ordered source filter therefore establishes

\[
 F'_v=F_v++\begin{cases}[z],&v=w,\\\lbrack\rbrack,&v\ne w,\end{cases}
 \qquad
 v\in\operatorname{images}(E')\iff v\in\operatorname{images}(E)\lor v=w.
\]

These are respectively an ordered-list identity and a membership equivalence; the old source order is retained in every class. The singleton base is computed directly:

\[
 \operatorname{exactDecoder}([z],[(z,w)])=\operatorname{Some}([(w,z)]).
\]

Suppose the old exact decoder exists and w is unused. For any old used v, v≠w, so F'_v=F_v remains singleton by the old decoder theorem. Since w is unused, F_w=[] and hence F'_w=[z]. The displayed image-membership equivalence exhausts the new classes: each is either an old singleton or this new singleton. Applying the singleton-class criterion constructs an extended exact decoder.

If w is already used, admission supplies an old member of F_w. Then F'_w=F_w++[z] cannot be singleton: writing F_w=x::R, equality with [y] would force R++[z]=[], contradicted by z's membership in that append. The new encoding still emits the class for w, so a successful inverse would require this nonsingleton list to be singleton. Therefore

\[
 w\in\operatorname{images}(E)\ \Longrightarrow\
 \operatorname{exactDecoder}(S',E')=\mathrm{None}.
\]

The successor proof quantifies over the original finite lists and every fresh source; it does not assume a particular codeword length, nor replace a merged class with an arbitrary representative. The ten binary source coordinates enumerate 2^10=1,024 kernels, with their original admission conjunction selecting exactly the all-admitted tuple.


93. Complete information support and position generation

The information-quantity support operation requires at least two distinct held labels and a nonempty canonical position trace. Its complete ordered Cartesian support contains exactly the words whose length equals the position-trace length and whose labels belong to the held alphabet. Native proofs establish completeness and duplicate freedom for every finite admitted input. Appending one position extends every prior word once with every held label in the original product order.

For every generated prefix and suffix, inserting any held label produces a member of the corresponding complete support. Inserting two distinct held labels with the same prefix and suffix produces distinct words. These statements establish the arbitrary finite independent-position support relation. Support composition retains the original left-outer/right-inner pair order and concatenates the two source words. Each output occurs precisely through such a source pair; raw duplicate or variable-length input supports retain their original behavior. No logarithmic value or additional information scale is introduced.


For an ordered held alphabet L, the source word generator is

\[
 W_L(0)=[\lbrack\rbrack],\qquad
 W_L(n+1)=\operatorname{concat}_{a\in L}[a::w:w\in W_L(n)].
\]

The public support operation first requires |L|≥2 and Distinct(L), then requires a nonempty distinct position list P. It returns W_L(|P|). Induction on n proves the complete membership equivalence

\[
 w\in W_L(n)\iff |w|=n\land\forall a\in w,\ a\in L.
\]

At n=0 the sole generated word is the empty list. At a successor, extracting a flatMap row gives a∈L and a generated tail of length n. Conversely a word of length n+1 has a head a∈L and a tail satisfying the induction hypothesis, reconstructing that same generated row.

Duplicate freedom follows by induction too. Within a row, a::u=a::v forces u=v, preserving distinct tails. Across different head rows, a::u=b::v forces a=b, contradicting distinct head labels. Appending one position has the ordered-list identity

\[
 W_L(n+1)=\operatorname{concat}_{w\in W_L(n)}[w++[a]:a\in L].
\]

To prove it, append-extension distributes over concatenated rows and commutes with a fixed head prefix. The base n=0 gives the ordered singleton words; the successor follows by distributing over the original outer label rows. Thus the identity preserves the exact generated order, not only membership.

For u∈W_L(n), v∈W_L(m) and a∈L, the membership characterization and append-length equation yield

\[
 u++v\in W_L(n+m),\qquad
 u++a::v\in W_L(n+(m+1)).
\]

If a≠b, equality u++a::v=u++b::v would cancel the common prefix by induction and then force a=b by head equality. Hence the two held alternatives are distinct at every finite position with the same surrounding context.

For arbitrary nonempty support lists A,B, composition returns the original nested concatenation

\[
 C(A,B)=\operatorname{concat}_{u\in A}[u++v:v\in B],\qquad
 w\in C(A,B)\iff\exists u\in A,\exists v\in B,\ w=u++v.
\]

The equivalence follows by extracting or constructing the outer and inner mapped occurrences. Empty factors take the original error branch. On raw supports no distinctness or equal-length guard is added, so repeated concatenations and variable-length words retain their source behavior.


94. Exact information profiles and independent position traces

For every admitted support, the positional pair construction contains each unordered nonidentity pair once, with its earlier support member first. The native derivation establishes endpoint provenance, absence of self-pairs and reversed duplicates, complete pair coverage and unique records. Observation admission requires unique image keys and exact support-key coverage before the support-pair validation. Empty observation labels remain permitted. The profile retains the support trace, first-occurrence observation-class order, retained distinctions with unequal images, and closed distinctions with equal images. These ledgers are disjoint and exhaust the complete support pairs. Fine observation retains every pair and closes none; coarsening cannot reopen a closed distinction and can only remove retained distinctions.

The position-trace computation explicitly tests both first held labels for every generated prefix and suffix at each position. Constructive proofs show that the complete support contains both alternatives at every finite depth and that unequal held labels give distinct words. The admitted computation returns exactly one one-Fold-distinction record for every original position, in order. Appending a position preserves the preceding unit trace and appends its unit; composition concatenates the position traces. Together with complete word generation and pair coverage, these results establish the original finite successor clause without a depth table or logarithmic proof quantity.
