All 512 quantity kernels and all five operational witnesses are retained. Fresh native execution matches the pinned registration, census, decisions, closure, controls and all digest fields, including unknown-identifier refusal. Information Quantity is complete within the original exact finite boundary; conventional logarithmic units remain post-seal correspondence summaries rather than SFT proof values.


For distinct nonempty support S, generate P([])=[] and P(a::R)=[(a,b):b∈R]++P(R). Induction proves that every pair has endpoints in S and unequal endpoints. Each head-row pair is unique, all tail pairs are unique by induction, and a head-row pair cannot equal a tail pair because its first endpoint a does not occur in R. For any distinct a,b∈S, either one is the current head, producing the corresponding ordered pair, or both lie in the tail and induction applies. Thus

\[
 a,b\in S\land a\ne b\ \Longrightarrow\
 (a,b)\in P(S)\lor(b,a)\in P(S),
 \qquad (a,b)\in P(S)\Longrightarrow(b,a)\notin P(S).
\]

The original nonidentity filter consequently leaves P(S) unchanged. Together with duplicate freedom, these facts establish exactly one record for each unordered distinct pair.

For observation rows O, admission requires Distinct(keys(O)), keys(O)⊆S and S⊆keys(O). It precedes the nonempty/distinct support check. The image f_O(w) is its first lookup value, with the raw missing-lookup fallback retained; under admission every support word has a matching row and the fallback is not used. Empty label strings remain valid. The profile is

\[
 \bigl(S,\ \operatorname{firstLabels}([f_O(w):w\in S]),\ U_O,\ C_O\bigr),
\]

where U_O=[(a,b)∈P(S):f_O(a)≠f_O(b)] and C_O=[(a,b)∈P(S):f_O(a)=f_O(b)]. Equality case analysis proves each generated pair belongs to one pass; equality and inequality cannot both hold, proving disjointness. A class label l occurs iff some w∈S satisfies f_O(w)=l, and first-occurrence filtering makes those labels distinct while preserving their order.

For a fine observation, f_O(a)=f_O(b) implies a=b on S. Since every generated pair has unequal endpoints, every pair is retained and none is closed. For compatible coarse images f_C=m∘f_O on the pair endpoints, equality is preserved by m, giving

\[
 C_O\subseteq C_C,\qquad U_C\subseteq U_O.
\]

The second inclusion follows by contradiction: equal fine images would force equal coarse images, contradicting coarse retention.

Let L begin with its first two distinct held labels a,b, and let n=|P|. At position i, with j positions following it, the actual source check is

\[
 \forall u\in W_L(i),\ \forall v\in W_L(j),\
 u++a::v\in W_L(i+(j+1))\land u++b::v\in W_L(i+(j+1)).
\]

The independent-position theorem proves both membership clauses for every prefix and suffix; prefix cancellation proves the two words differ. Induction over P increments i and decreases the remaining tail, retaining i+|remaining|=n. Hence every check succeeds on the admitted complete support and the returned trace is exactly

\[
 T(P)=[(p,\text{one-Fold-distinction}):p\in P],\qquad |T(P)|=|P|.
\]

Map distribution over append yields T(P++Q)=T(P)++T(Q), and in particular T(P++[z])=T(P)++[(z,one-Fold-distinction)]. Invalid support inputs propagate their original error before these position checks. The nine binary source coordinates enumerate 2^9=512 kernels with the original all-admitted conjunction.


95. Deterministic entropy partitions and observation refinement

The exact entropy partition requires nonempty duplicate-free microstate support, unique observation keys and exact support-key coverage. Class labels follow their first observed occurrence in the original support order; each fibre retains its microstates in that order. Native proofs establish complete coverage, disjoint classes, nonempty fibres and distinct members. Unresolved distinctions are the complete positional pair list within each class. Empty and singleton lists return structural empty One; the general empty-One result is equivalent to at most one input member. The complete closed-pair output flattens these class ledgers in class order.

The original refinement operation has a broader raw domain than partition admission: it tests only the two observation key sets against support and retains dictionary last-row behavior for repeated keys. Its native characterization states exactly that equality under the fine observation implies equality under the coarse observation for every support pair. The relation is reflexive on matching-key observations and transitive. For admitted exact partitions, refinement holds if and only if every fine class is contained in a coarse class. These results retain deterministic observation provenance without introducing a stochastic source or scalar entropy value.


Let S be the microstate support and O the observation rows. Partition admission is

\[
 S\ne[]\land\operatorname{Distinct}(S)\land\operatorname{Distinct}(\operatorname{keys}(O))
 \land\operatorname{keys}(O)\subseteq S\land S\subseteq\operatorname{keys}(O).
\]

Writing f_O(s) for the source's last-row lookup, define F_l=[s∈S:f_O(s)=Some(l)]. The emitted partition is [(l,F_l):l∈firstLabels(observedValues(O,S))]. An emitted row has a witnessing source state, so its fibre is nonempty. Key coverage supplies each state's image, hence its fibre; equality of the deterministic lookup proves that intersecting fibres have equal labels. Filtering the distinct support preserves distinctness within every fibre and its original order.

For any finite member list M, let P(M) be its positional pair list. The structural uncertainty result is

\[
 U(M)=\begin{cases}\mathrm{emptyOne},&P(M)=[],\\
 \mathrm{pairs}(P(M)),&P(M)\ne[].\end{cases}
\]

The pair recurrence gives P([])=P([x])=[]; for x::y::R its first row contains (x,y), so it is nonempty. Therefore U(M)=emptyOne iff M has at most one occurrence, even on the raw list domain. This structural result is separate from any ordinary microstate string named "empty-One". For an admitted partition [(l_i,F_i)], the full closed ledger is concat_i P(F_i), retaining class order and each within-class pair order.

The raw refinement predicate requires key-set coverage for both observations but does not add their partition-admission guards. Its exact condition is

\[
 O_f\preceq_S O_c\iff
 \operatorname{keysMatch}(S,O_f)\land\operatorname{keysMatch}(S,O_c)
 \land\forall a,b\in S,\ f_{O_f}(a)=f_{O_f}(b)\Rightarrow f_{O_c}(a)=f_{O_c}(b).
\]

Reflexivity follows from identity of the equality implication. For transitivity, apply the fine-to-middle implication and then the middle-to-coarse implication to the same pair. Repeated raw keys retain last-row lookup, and the empty support with empty observations satisfies the predicate.

For two admitted partitions, select a base member x of any fine fibre F. Every y∈F has the same fine image as x; refinement forces its coarse image to equal x's, placing all of F in the coarse fibre containing x. Conversely, if every fine fibre lies in some coarse fibre, equal fine images put a,b into the same fine fibre and hence into one coarse fibre, proving equal coarse images. Thus

\[
 O_f\preceq_S O_c\iff
 \forall F\in\mathcal P_f,\ \exists C\in\mathcal P_c,\ F\subseteq C,
\]

where the quantifiers range over the member lists of the admitted labelled partition rows. This proves the source refinement relation from finite observation records without assigning a scalar entropy value.


96. Exact entropy parts and closed-pair monotonicity

Each entropy record retains its observation label, exact positive support part, complete microstate trace and structural unresolved-distinction value. The native construction preserves the partition's record order and uses the exact reduced fraction of the class cardinality over the complete support cardinality. A cross-product identity proves preservation of that rational value, and the part is positive and at most the whole. Literal empty-One microstate labels remain ordinary retained microstates; structural empty-One uncertainty is the separate unresolved-distinction result.

Refinement implies inclusion of each fine class's positional pairs in its containing coarse class. The complete closed-pair ledger has no repeated pair records, both within a class and across disjoint classes. Native finite-list counting therefore proves that a finer admitted observation has no more closed pairs than an admitted coarser observation it refines. The result applies to arbitrary finite admitted support and retains every pair's source coordinates.


For an admitted support S and partition [(l_i,F_i)], put N=|S| and m_i=|F_i|. Admission and nonempty fibres give N>0 and m_i>0. The exact record constructor is

\[
 R_i=(l_i,\operatorname{normalizePositive}(m_i,N),F_i,U(F_i)).
\]

Writing the normalized positive fraction as (a_i,b_i), normalization proves

\[
 m_i b_i=a_i N,\qquad a_i>0,\quad b_i>0.
\]

Since F_i is an ordered filter of S, induction on S gives m_i≤N: retaining a head increments both lengths, while discarding it increments only the support length. Normalization therefore yields a_i≤b_i and hence 0<a_i/b_i≤1. The ledger recursion emits R_i followed by the recursively constructed tail. Induction on the partition proves that projecting each record to (label,microstates) returns the original partition exactly, while each unresolved-distinction field remains U(F_i). A partition error is returned unchanged; no record is manufactured for an inadmissible input.

Let P(X) denote the positional pairs of a list. Its ordered recurrence is

\[
 P([])=[],\qquad P(x::X)=[(x,y):y\in X]\mathbin{++}P(X).
\]

For predicates p,q with p(x)⇒q(x) on S, induction through this recurrence proves

\[
 P(\operatorname{filter}_p S)\subseteq P(\operatorname{filter}_q S).
\]

If p rejects the head, every pair comes from the tail and survives the induction. If p accepts it, q accepts it too; a head pair survives because its second coordinate also satisfies the implication, and a tail pair survives by induction. Thus the proof retains the source orientation of pairs, rather than replacing positional pairs by arbitrary ordered pairs.

Write C(O)=concat_i P(F_i) for the complete closed ledger. Under admitted fine and coarse observations, refinement supplies a containing coarse fibre for every fine fibre. Applying filtered-pair containment inside each such fibre gives

\[
 O_f\preceq_S O_c\ \Longrightarrow\ C(O_f)\subseteq C(O_c).
\]

For a distinct support, P(S) has no duplicates: head pairs have different second coordinates, the tail is duplicate-free by induction, and a head pair cannot occur in the tail because its first coordinate is absent there. Each fibre inherits distinctness. Across different fibres, a repeated pair would place its first coordinate in both fibres, forcing their labels equal, contrary to distinct emitted labels. Consequently C(O) is duplicate-free for every admitted partition.

Finally, for a duplicate-free list A contained in B, remove every occurrence of the head a of A from B. Membership of a makes the filtered list strictly shorter; distinctness of A ensures its tail is still contained in that filtered list. Induction gives

\[
 |\operatorname{tail}(A)|\le|B\setminus\{a\}|<|B|,
 \qquad |A|\le|B|.
\]

Applying this finite-list argument to the closed ledgers establishes

\[
 O_f\preceq_S O_c\ \Longrightarrow\ |C(O_f)|\le|C(O_c)|.
\]

This is the declared finite closed-distinction monotonicity, with exact rational support parts retained separately in the records.


97. Entropy successor closure and exact finite completion

For one deterministic microstate, the exact partition is one class whose native normalized support part is One and whose unresolved ledger is structural empty One. Adding a fresh state preserves every prior image and appends the new observation in source order. Its assigned class appends precisely that state; every other class retains its member trace. The pair successor preserves the original nested order and contains exactly the old class pairs together with one pair from each prior same-class member to the fresh state. Each returned class part satisfies the exact member-count/new-support-count ratio. The changed total is retained in the weights of all classes.

The general successor derivation, exact weighted class ledger and refinement containment/count results complete the original finite entropy statement. All 512 candidate kernels and all five operational witnesses are retained; fresh native program output matches the pinned census, actual lookup decisions, registration, closure, controls and digest fields. The retained 9805 independent input comparisons cover partition/closed pairs, raw unresolved pairs, refinement and normalized Fraction records, including the original invalid and duplicate input domains. Entropy and Uncertainty is complete at V3's declared finite boundary. Conventional scalar Shannon summaries remain subject to the separately declared source correspondence map.


For a fresh state x∉S, let S'=S++[x] and O'=O++[(x,v)]. Last-row lookup gives f_{O'}(x)=v. Induction over the observation rows, using x≠s for every old state s, gives f_{O'}(s)=f_O(s). Consequently the ordered observation and fibre equations are

\[
 \operatorname{observedValues}(O',S')=\operatorname{observedValues}(O,S)\mathbin{++}[v],
 \qquad F'_l=F_l\mathbin{++}\begin{cases}[x],&v=l,\\ \lbrack\rbrack,&v\ne l.\end{cases}
\]

Freshness preserves distinct support and distinct observation keys. The old key coverage extends in both directions by the same appended key x, so partition admission is preserved. The assigned fibre appends x; the others retain their complete ordered traces, including when v is a newly occurring label.

The ordered successor pairs are described by E_x([])=[] and

\[
 E_x(y::R)=\bigl([(y,z):z\in R]\mathbin{++}[(y,x)]\bigr)\mathbin{++}E_x(R).
\]

Expanding the positional-pair recurrence and distributing the head map over R++[x] proves P(M++[x])=E_x(M) by induction. Splitting each concatenation in that induction also proves

\[
 p\in E_x(M)\iff p\in P(M)\ \lor\ \exists y\in M,\ p=(y,x).
\]

Thus each new pair appears at its original nested-loop position, alongside the preserved prior pairs. This formula applies directly to the changed fibre's unresolved-pair contents.

For any returned successor record of label l with normalized part (a'_l,b'_l), the exact-part identity and the fibre equation give

\[
 \bigl(|F_l|+\mathbf1_{v=l}\bigr)b'_l=a'_l(|S|+1).
\]

All class weights therefore use the new total, including unchanged fibres. At the base S=[x], O=[(x,v)], key coverage and distinctness hold directly, and the partition is [(v,[x])]. Normalization of (1,1) gives numerator and denominator both 1, while P([x])=[] gives structural empty-One uncertainty. These base and successor equations retain the exact finite construction.


98. Exact compression descriptions and reconstruction provenance

A dictionary description is admitted precisely when its dictionary and token trace are nonempty, dictionary keys are unique and every retained token has a dictionary entry. The executable source allows empty dictionary blocks and empty string labels; the translation retains these domains. Reconstruction concatenates each referenced block in token order. The native lookup theorem connects each selected block to its unique retained dictionary row, and the provenance theorem identifies the supplying token and block for every reconstructed cell.

Losslessness is equivalent to a nonempty source message and exact equality with the reconstructed message. An empty source message returns false without attempting reconstruction, preserving the source short circuit. Malformed descriptions retain the original reconstruction error, while a valid unequal reconstruction gives the original redundancy error. The redundancy trace retains a token/cell pair for each produced occurrence; projecting its cells recovers the exact source message, and its length equals the source length.

The resource record retains the dictionary rows in order, every token cell in order, the reconstructed trace and their complete resource concatenation. Native count identities retain every dictionary row and token occurrence. One retained token reconstructs exactly its registered block; appending any valid token appends precisely its block to the message and one cell to the resource trace. These results establish the source token-successor clause. Independent comparisons cover 1720 reconstruction/resource cases and 6880 lossless/redundancy cases, including duplicate keys, missing tokens, empty blocks, repeated tokens and empty-message behavior. Prefix parsing and the complete fixed/short support boundary remain distinct components of the Compression and Redundancy entry.


Write D for the ordered dictionary, T for the token list and B_D(t) for first-key lookup's block. Description admission is exactly

\[
 V(D,T)\iff D\ne[]\land\operatorname{Distinct}(\operatorname{keys}(D))
 \land T\ne[]\land\forall t\in T,\ t\in\operatorname{keys}(D).
\]

Neither nonempty blocks nor nonempty string labels are added to this predicate. With C_D(T)=concat_{t∈T}B_D(t), reconstruction obeys

\[
 \operatorname{reconstruct}(D,T)=\operatorname{ok}(W)
 \iff V(D,T)\land W=C_D(T).
\]

Admission supplies a dictionary row for each token; distinct keys make its first lookup equal that row's block. Membership in concatenation then proves both directions of the provenance statement

\[
 c\in W\iff\exists t,U,\ t\in T\land(t,U)\in D\land c\in U.
\]

The forward direction selects a supplying token block; the reverse direction uses unique-key lookup to insert that block's cell into the concatenation. Repeated tokens retain repeated output occurrences.

The lossless result is true precisely when M≠[] and reconstruct(D,T)=ok(M). For M=[], the result is false immediately, including for an invalid description. For a nonempty message, reconstruction errors propagate; successful unequal reconstruction yields false. Redundancy returns the original exact-reconstruction error for a false result and propagates reconstruction errors otherwise.

Define the ordered occurrence ledger

\[
 Q_D(T)=\operatorname{concat}_{t\in T}[(t,c):c\in B_D(t)].
\]

Projection commutes with concatenation, and projecting each block's pairs returns that block. Induction over T therefore proves map(second,Q_D(T))=C_D(T). For a lossless description this is M, and length preservation under mapping yields |Q_D(T)|=|M|. The ledger retains occurrences rather than merely the set of supplying tokens.

The resource traces are

\[
 A(D)=[\,[\text{dictionary},t]\mathbin{++}U:(t,U)\in D\,],\qquad
 H(T)=[\,[\text{token},t]:t\in T\,],\qquad R(D,T)=A(D)\mathbin{++}H(T).
\]

Their outer list lengths satisfy |A(D)|=|D|, |H(T)|=|T| and |R(D,T)|=|D|+|T|. These count retained resource rows, not character lengths. For a valid description and a dictionary row (t,U), appending t preserves admission and gives

\[
 C_D(T\mathbin{++}[t])=C_D(T)\mathbin{++}U,\qquad
 R(D,T\mathbin{++}[t])=R(D,T)\mathbin{++}[[\text{token},t]].
\]

The decoding equality follows by distributing concatenation over appended token lists and using unique-key lookup. For the singleton token list [t], the same argument gives reconstruct(D,[t])=ok(U), including U=[]. This establishes the base and token-successor clauses without changing the executable domain.


99. Prefix parsing and the complete-support shortening boundary

The prefix-family predicate rejects empty families, empty words, repeated words and any distinct pair for which the shorter word is the initial segment of the longer. A native common-initial-segment argument shows that two parses of the same stream must begin with the same admitted word. Cancelling that word and repeating over the finite traces proves unique parsing for arbitrary admitted concatenations. Explicit dictionary token boundaries are retained by the reconstruction representation.

Fixed support retains the complete ordered Cartesian product at the specified width. The shorter support concatenates all products at positive widths below that width, in increasing width order. Native membership and count proofs establish the complete original enumeration and its strict multiplicity-count inequality whenever the raw helper accepts. The pigeonhole argument rules out a pairwise-distinct shorter assignment to every generated occurrence. For set-injective coding of distinct messages, the distinct-label requirement is inherited from the registered Information Quantity complete_word_support domain, rather than added to Compression's executable guard. A native bridge proves that this canonical support is exactly the Compression product and establishes the no-injective-short-code theorem on that domain. The broader raw helper accepts repeated labels; its list-count result is preserved, without treating repeated equal words as distinct messages. The native raw helper therefore preserves repeated alphabet labels and their output multiplicities without an additional distinctness guard.

Compression and Redundancy retains all 1,024 kernels and all five operational witnesses. Fresh native execution matches every original program field, including actual candidate lookups, controls and digests. The cumulative 11,626 source-expression comparisons cover exact descriptions, errors, prefix families and complete fixed/short supports. These results complete the entry on its registered canonical support boundary, without a universal best compressor, fitted distribution or real-valued asymptotic rate.


For a family W of words, the source tests proper prefixes by

\[
 \operatorname{properPrefix}(u,v)\iff |u|<|v|\land\operatorname{take}_{|u|}(v)=u.
\]

Prefix admission requires W≠[], every word nonempty, distinct words, and no proper-prefix relation between distinct members. Suppose two admitted parses begin with u and v and have equal concatenations, u++A=v++B. Cancelling equal leading cells recursively proves that either v=u++r or u=v++r. If r is nonempty, the shorter word is a proper prefix of the longer, contradicting admission; if r is empty, u=v. Cancel this common first word and apply induction to the remaining parses. A nonempty parse cannot equal an empty parse because its first word is nonempty. Thus for any two finite parses L,R over W,

\[
 \operatorname{concat}(L)=\operatorname{concat}(R)\ \Longrightarrow\ L=R.
\]

For an ordered label list A, define the complete word list recursively by

\[
 W_0=[\,[]\,],\qquad W_{n+1}=\operatorname{concat}_{a\in A}[a::w:w\in W_n].
\]

The raw fixed-support helper admits exactly |A|≥2 and a nonempty position list P, and returns W_{|P|}. It does not test distinctness of A. Every list occurrence is retained. With q=|A|, distributing list length over the outer concatenation gives |W_{n+1}|=q|W_n| and hence |W_n|=q^n. Membership is exactly word length n with every cell drawn from A.

Define T_0=[] and T_{n+1}=T_n++W_{n+1}. Then

\[
 w\in T_n\iff 0<|w|\le n\land\forall c\in w,\ c\in A,
 \qquad |T_n|=\sum_{j=1}^{n}q^j.
\]

The shorter nonempty support for a width-N source is T_{N-1}, preserving increasing width order. For q≥2, its strict count bound follows without omitting any generated word. At n=0, |T_0|=0<q=|W_1|. If |T_n|<|W_{n+1}|, then

\[
 |T_{n+1}|=|T_n|+|W_{n+1}|
 <2|W_{n+1}|\le q|W_{n+1}|=|W_{n+2}|.
\]

Therefore every admitted raw fixed support satisfies |T_{N-1}|<|W_N|, including when repeated labels produce repeated equal words.

For any duplicate-free list C contained in a finite list T, the removal induction gives |C|≤|T|: remove the head of C from T, use the head's membership to make T strictly shorter, and use C's distinctness to keep its tail contained in the remainder. If a proposed code f maps every generated full-support word into T_{N-1} and the full output list map(f,W_N) is duplicate-free, this yields

\[
 |W_N|=|\operatorname{map}(f,W_N)|\le|T_{N-1}|<|W_N|,
\]

a contradiction. This establishes the occurrence-list result on the raw helper's exact domain.

For the registered complete_word_support domain, admission already supplies distinct alphabet labels. Induction through the Cartesian-product construction makes W_N duplicate-free: different leading labels separate blocks, and the tail induction separates words within each block. Injectivity of f on W_N then makes map(f,W_N) duplicate-free. The canonical bridge returns the identical W_N from the Compression helper, so the preceding contradiction proves the no-injective-short-code result on that inherited domain. The bridge adds no condition to the raw executable helper and preserves both source domains.


100. Finite channel admission and exact confusability

An admitted finite channel retains nonempty duplicate-free input and output carriers and a duplicate-free relation within those carriers. Every input has at least one image; unused output-carrier entries remain permitted. The native constructor preserves the original order of carrier, repeated-cell, endpoint and totality checks, including their distinct errors. Empty string labels remain allowed.

Output support filters the retained relation by the queried source and preserves every corresponding target in relation order. Native theorems establish exact cell membership, membership within the output carrier, nonemptiness for admitted inputs and absence of repeated targets. A query outside the input carrier retains the original error. Confusability is equivalent to the existence of a retained target shared by both inputs. The overlap is symmetric, and every admitted input is self-confusable because its support is nonempty. No probability or error threshold enters these predicates.

The distinguishable-code predicate retains its original nonempty, unique-input and input-coverage guards and tests every positional input pair. Its native characterization establishes that admitted codes have pairwise disjoint output supports. Every admitted single input forms a distinguishable singleton code. Independent source-expression comparisons pass 3,913 constructor cases, 600 output-support cases, 2,400 confusability cases and 6,000 code cases, covering malformed and empty carriers, duplicate cells, missing transport, empty labels, repeated held inputs and invalid queries. Complete capacity selection, composition and the fresh-carrier successor are established below.


Write a channel as (I,O,R), where I and O are ordered carriers and R is the ordered list of transport cells. Constructor success is equivalent to

\[
 I\ne[]\land O\ne[]\land\operatorname{Distinct}(I)\land\operatorname{Distinct}(O)
 \land\operatorname{Distinct}(R)\land R\subseteq I\times O
 \land\forall x\in I,\ \exists y,\ (x,y)\in R.
\]

The constructor checks carrier validity first, then repeated cells, then endpoint containment, then totality; the first failed condition returns its corresponding source error. Success retains I,O,R exactly. No output-surjectivity requirement is imposed.

For an input x, define the ordered support

\[
 S_R(x)=\operatorname{map}(\operatorname{second},\operatorname{filter}_{\operatorname{first}=x}R).
\]

Unpacking filter and map membership proves y∈S_R(x) iff (x,y)∈R. Totality therefore supplies a support member for every admitted x, while endpoint containment places every support member in O. Distinctness follows because two filtered cells have the same first coordinate x, so equality of their second coordinates implies equality of the cells. A query succeeds exactly for x∈I and returns this ordered support; other queries return the outside-input error.

The overlap predicate has the exact characterization

\[
 \operatorname{overlap}_R(x,z)\iff\exists y,\ (x,y)\in R\land(z,y)\in R.
\]

Swapping the two conjuncts proves symmetry. Totality supplies the same witness twice to prove self-confusability for admitted inputs. The public confusability operation checks the left input's support, then the right input's support, preserving that error order, and returns overlap when both queries succeed.

Let P(H) be the positional-pair list of a held input list H. Its code predicate is

\[
 \operatorname{Code}(H)\iff H\ne[]\land\operatorname{Distinct}(H)\land H\subseteq I
 \land\forall(x,z)\in P(H),\ \neg\operatorname{overlap}_R(x,z).
\]

A shared support target would witness overlap and contradict the final clause. Conversely, absence of any shared target makes that Boolean overlap false for every tested pair. For H=[x] with x∈I, the pair list is empty, so all code conditions hold directly. These are exact finite relations and guards, without probability weights.


101. Complete finite capacity families and relation composition

Channel candidate generation enumerates each positive selection size in increasing order and, within that size, every input combination in source-index order. A native selection relation characterizes precisely these retained subsequences. The enumeration theorem establishes exact length, carrier membership and complete generation; distinct admitted inputs yield duplicate-free selections and candidates. Filtering retains exactly the original distinguishable codes. Every admitted input supplies a singleton candidate, so the admitted candidate family is nonempty.

The capacity computation takes the greatest candidate length and retains every candidate attaining it, in the original enumeration order. Native proofs establish the maximum bound, attainment and equivalence between returned membership and being a distinguishable canonical selection at least as large as every other such selection. No tie rule removes an alternative. Admitted capacity is nonempty and contains no repeated records. A one-input channel returns exactly its singleton code family.

Composition requires equality of the ordered interface carriers. It traverses the first relation and then each matching second relation row, keeping the first occurrence of each resulting endpoint cell. Native proofs establish that composite membership is equivalent to a retained two-edge path through the interface, and that admitted channels compose to a total admitted channel. If two inputs share an intermediate output, totality of the second channel supplies a common final output; composition therefore cannot restore that merged distinction.

Independent source-expression comparisons pass 186 combination enumerations, 399 complete candidate/capacity families and 6,272 compositions, covering all retained ties, repeated raw combination labels, relation-row permutations, first-occurrence duplicates and ordered-interface mismatch errors. The fresh-input successor and complete candidate program complete the Channel Capacity entry as established below.


A canonical selection is an order-preserving subsequence of the carrier, generated by the empty, skip and take constructors. The complete size-k enumeration obeys

\[
 B_0(I)=[\,[]\,],\qquad B_{k+1}([])=[],\qquad
 B_{k+1}(x::I)=\operatorname{map}(x::\cdot,B_k(I))\mathbin{++}B_{k+1}(I).
\]

Induction on the carrier matches the first branch to take and the second to skip, proving H∈B_k(I) iff |H|=k and H is a canonical selection of I. With T_0(I)=[] and T_{k+1}(I)=T_k(I)++B_{k+1}(I), this gives

\[
 H\in T_n(I)\iff 0<|H|\le n\land\operatorname{Chosen}(H,I).
\]

Every chosen selection has length at most |I|. Filtering T_{|I|}(I) by Code therefore gives exactly all canonical distinguishable codes. For distinct I, take and skip branches cannot share a selection: a take selection contains the head x, whereas x is absent from the remaining carrier. Induction proves distinctness within each size; unequal lengths separate different sizes. Filtering preserves that distinctness. Any admitted input supplies a singleton candidate, establishing nonemptiness.

For the candidate list C, the maximum recurrence is

\[
 M([])=0,\qquad M(H::C)=\begin{cases}M(C),&|H|<M(C),\\|H|,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
\]

Induction proves both |H|≤M(C) for every H∈C and M(C)≤b whenever every candidate has length at most b. On a nonempty list, either the head attains the recurrence value or the tail induction supplies a maximizer. Consequently the retained family K=[H∈C:|H|=M(C)] satisfies

\[
 H\in K\iff H\in C\land\forall G\in C,\ |G|\le|H|.
\]

Every tie is retained in the original enumeration order. Nonempty C gives nonempty K; distinct C gives distinct K. The raw empty-candidate case retains the source max-empty-sequence error. For a one-input carrier [x], the candidate list and capacity family are both [[x]].

For composition, the ordered output carrier of the first channel must equal the ordered input carrier of the second. Let

\[
 P(R_1,R_2)=\operatorname{concat}_{(x,y)\in R_1}[(x,z):(y,z)\in R_2],
 \qquad R_{21}=\operatorname{firstCells}(P(R_1,R_2)).
\]

The nested enumeration retains relation order; firstCells skips cells already seen and otherwise emits the cell before continuing with it added to the seen list. Induction proves that a cell survives this scan iff it occurs in the input and was absent from the initial seen list, and that the output is duplicate-free. Starting with no seen cells preserves membership, so

\[
 (x,z)\in R_{21}\iff\exists y,\ (x,y)\in R_1\land(y,z)\in R_2.
\]

Endpoint containment follows from the two component relations. For each first-channel input x, its totality supplies y; interface equality makes y an admitted second-channel input, whose totality supplies z. This proves composite totality and hence admission with the retained endpoint carriers.

Finally, if x and x' share y in R_1, second-channel totality supplies one z with (y,z)∈R_2. The path characterization gives both (x,z) and (x',z) in R_{21}. Therefore

\[
 \operatorname{overlap}_{R_1}(x,x')\ \Longrightarrow\operatorname{overlap}_{R_{21}}(x,x').
\]

Thus the no-restored-distinction statement follows from the actual finite relation composition and its totality, with no replacement transport rule.


102. Fresh-input closure of exact channel capacity

Appending a fresh input and its complete nonempty, duplicate-free support within the held output carrier preserves channel admission. Output support for every prior input is unchanged, while the new input has exactly the appended support in its original order. Every prior confusability decision remains unchanged. The new decision with each prior input is precisely the overlap of its retained support with the new support.

Every prior distinguishable code remains valid. Its extension by the fresh input is distinguishable exactly when each prior member has disjoint support from the fresh input. The native exhaustive successor theorem establishes that every generated code is an old code, the fresh singleton, or such a permitted extension of an old code, and conversely every one of those alternatives is generated. This closes arbitrary finite carrier growth while retaining the full candidate family used by the greatest-cardinality selection.

All 1,024 channel kernels and all five original operational witnesses are retained. Fresh native execution matches every original registration, census, actual candidate lookup, closure, control and digest field. The cumulative 20,505 source-expression comparisons include 735 fresh-input support/code checks alongside constructor, support, confusability, capacity and composition cases. Channel Capacity is complete at the source finite zero-error boundary; probabilistic error rates and asymptotic coding claims remain outside that original boundary.


Let C=(I,O,R) be admitted, let x∉I be fresh, and let T be a nonempty distinct list contained in O. The source extension is

\[
 I'=I\mathbin{++}[x],\qquad O'=O,\qquad
 R'=R\mathbin{++}[(x,t):t\in T].
\]

Distinctness of I' follows from freshness. The new cells are distinct because their second coordinates are distinct; no new cell can coincide with an old cell, since endpoint containment would put x in I. Old endpoints remain inside the extended carriers, and new endpoints lie there by T⊆O. Old inputs retain a transport witness, while nonempty T supplies one for x. These four facts establish the extended constructor's admission conditions.

Filtering distributes over relation concatenation. For the fresh rows alone,

\[
 S_{[(x,t):t\in T]}(s)=\begin{cases}T,&s=x,\\\lbrack\rbrack,&s\ne x.\end{cases}
\]

Hence S_{R'}(s)=S_R(s) for every old s. No old cell begins at x, so S_R(x)=[] and S_{R'}(x)=T. The preserved support equations imply

\[
 \operatorname{overlap}_{R'}(s,u)=\operatorname{overlap}_{R}(s,u)\quad(s,u\in I),
 \qquad\operatorname{overlap}_{R'}(s,x)\iff\exists t\in T,\ (s,t)\in R.
\]

For any held list H contained in I, its nonemptiness, distinctness and old pair decisions are unchanged, proving Code_{C'}(H) iff Code_C(H). If H is already a code, freshness preserves distinctness of H++[x]. Its positional pairs consist exactly of old pairs and pairs (s,x) for s∈H, in the established interleaved source order. Therefore

\[
 \operatorname{Code}_{C'}(H\mathbin{++}[x])
 \iff\forall s\in H,\ \neg\operatorname{overlap}_{R'}(s,x).
\]

For the converse restriction used below, a nonempty H contained in I with Code_{C'}(H++[x]) inherits distinctness and all old pair tests from that longer code. The old overlap identity then proves Code_C(H).

Write K(C) for the complete candidate list, before selecting maximum cardinality. Induction through the take/skip constructors shows that any canonical selection of I++[x] either omits the final x and is chosen from I, or equals H++[x] for H chosen from I. In the latter case H=[] yields the fresh singleton; otherwise the restriction argument supplies an old code and the extension test supplies separation. This proves the exhaustive membership equivalence

\[
 L\in K(C')\iff L\in K(C)\ \lor\ L=[x]\ \lor
 \exists H\in K(C),\ L=H\mathbin{++}[x]
 \land\forall s\in H,\ \neg\operatorname{overlap}_{R'}(s,x).
\]

Conversely, an old selection remains chosen in the appended carrier; [x] is a singleton code; and appending x to a chosen old code remains a canonical selection and passes exactly the stated separation tests. Thus every listed alternative is generated, and none is missing. This is a membership characterization of the complete existing enumeration, not a replacement ordering for that enumeration. The maximum-family construction continues to act on the full ordered candidate list.


103. Registered action relations and exact correction

An error relation retains nonempty duplicate-free source and received word carriers, duplicate-free source/image/action triples, and at least one image for every source. Every triple lies within the held carriers and has a nonempty action label. Empty words and unused received entries remain permitted. Distinct actions may connect the same source and image. The native constructor retains the original guard order and errors.

Image and predecessor extraction filters the original action relation, then removes repeated words in first-occurrence order. Native membership equivalences establish that a word is retained exactly when a corresponding action cell exists. Images of an admitted source are nonempty and lie in the received carrier; every retained predecessor lies in the source carrier. Both lists are duplicate-free. An unused received entry has an empty predecessor class, preserving the original domain.

Correction is unique exactly when the complete registered predecessor list is a singleton. The unique record retains that source; every other list, including the empty list, is returned intact as a closed source class. Native proofs establish that a uniquely corrected source has a registered action cell and that any registered predecessor of that received word equals the corrected source. No likelihood, distance rule or inferred predecessor enters the construction. Independent source-expression comparisons pass 7,693 constructor cases, 738 image extractions, 738 predecessor extractions and 1,476 correction decisions/records.


Let S and V be source and received word lists, and R the ordered list of triples (s,v,a). Admission requires nonempty distinct S,V, distinct R, and

\[
 \forall(s,v,a)\in R,\ s\in S\land v\in V\land a\ne\text{empty string},
 \qquad\forall s\in S,\ \exists v,a,\ (s,v,a)\in R.
\]

The checks occur in this order: carriers, repeated triples, endpoint/action conditions, totality. Success returns the original lists. Empty words remain allowed; different actions on the same source/image pair remain distinct triples.

Let F be the first-occurrence word filter. Its scan skips a word already in the seen list, otherwise emits it and adds it to that list. Induction proves membership in the scan output iff membership in its input and absence from its initial seen list. Emitted words cannot recur because each is thereafter seen; hence F preserves membership and produces a distinct ordered list. Define

\[
 I_R(s)=F([v:(s,v,a)\in R]),\qquad P_R(v)=F([s:(s,v,a)\in R]).
\]

Filter and projection membership, followed by membership preservation of F, give

\[
 v\in I_R(s)\iff\exists a,\ (s,v,a)\in R
 \iff s\in P_R(v).
\]

Thus totality makes each admitted source's image list nonempty, and endpoint containment places images in V and predecessors in S. Querying an image list requires s∈S; querying predecessors requires v∈V. An unused admitted v has P_R(v)=[], whereas a query outside V retains its error.

The correction record is defined by the complete predecessor list:

\[
 \operatorname{record}(P)=\begin{cases}\operatorname{uniqueSource}(s),&P=[s],\\
 \operatorname{closedSourceClass}(P),&|P|\ne1.\end{cases}
\]

Splitting a list into empty, singleton, and at-least-two cases proves |P|=1 iff P=[s] for some s, and record(P)=uniqueSource(s) iff P=[s]. Therefore

\[
 \operatorname{correction}(v)=\operatorname{ok}(\operatorname{uniqueSource}(s))
 \iff v\in V\land P_R(v)=[s].
\]

Membership of s in that singleton supplies a registered action (s,v,a). Any other registered predecessor t belongs to P_R(v) by the same membership equivalence and must equal s. If |P_R(v)|≠1, the record returns the entire P_R(v), including the empty list, with no selected replacement predecessor.


104. Complete position traces, detection and action succession

Word comparison requires equal generated position carriers and retains the original error for unequal widths. Complete ordered position pairs are filtered by label inequality, retaining each changed position and both its source and received labels. Positions are rendered with the original one-based position labels. Native indexed membership proves exact correspondence at every position of arbitrary finite words. The resulting trace is structural empty One exactly when the words are identical; otherwise it is the complete ordered changed-position trace.

The detection function retains identity as true. For nonidentical equal-width forms, detection is exactly received-word nonmembership in the valid codebook. No source-membership guard, probability threshold or distance measure is added. Unequal-width errors propagate unchanged.

The identity-action base admits a single source and yields its singleton predecessor class, unique correction and empty-One trace. At each finite successor, appended registered action cells extend the received support by their exact images. Native admission preservation retains the original carrier and cell conditions. Complete image and predecessor membership after extension is equivalent to prior membership or a new matching action cell. First-occurrence order equations retain the original traversal and deduplication behavior. These results cover arbitrary finite action additions, including shared images, without selecting among ambiguous predecessors.

Noise and Error retains all 1,024 kernels, all five operational witnesses and both admitted witness relations. Fresh native execution matches every original registration, census, actual candidate lookup, closure, control, hash and unknown-identifier result. The cumulative 18,867 source-expression comparisons include 1,600 trace, 6,400 detection and 222 successor comparisons. The entry is complete at its finite structural boundary; stochastic frequencies, physical noise mechanisms and asymptotic reliability remain outside that original claim.


At an initial index k, the position-pair recursion emits (k,s_0,v_0) and continues at k+1 on the two tails; it stops when either word ends. Its indexed membership theorem is

\[
 (i,a,b)\in Q_k(s,v)\iff\exists j,\ i=k+j\land s[j]=\operatorname{Some}(a)\land v[j]=\operatorname{Some}(b).
\]

The head case has j=0; the tail case increments j and uses k+1+j=k+(j+1). For equal-width words, induction also gives |Q_k(s,v)|=|s|. Filtering Q_k by a≠b gives exactly the changed positions, retaining order and both labels. Presentation maps (i,a,b) to ("position-" followed by the decimal representation of i+1,a,b).

For equal widths, the changed list is empty iff s=v: identical heads are removed and induction applies to the tails; unequal heads would be retained and contradict emptiness. Conversely, identical words have no unequal head at any step. Consequently

\[
 \operatorname{errorTrace}(s,v)=\operatorname{ok}(\mathrm{emptyOne})\iff s=v.
\]

For equal-width unequal words, the trace is the complete mapped changed list. Unequal widths return the common-position-carrier error before presentation. For any supplied valid-word list B, detection therefore satisfies

\[
 \operatorname{detectable}(B,s,v)=\operatorname{ok}
 \left(\begin{cases}\mathrm{true},&s=v,\\v\notin B,&s\ne v\end{cases}\right)
 \quad\text{when }|s|=|v|.
\]

The unequal-width error propagates unchanged; no membership guard on s is introduced.

For appended action cells A, the exact extension is

\[
 S'=S,\qquad V'=F(V\mathbin{++}[v:(s,v,a)\in A]),\qquad R'=R\mathbin{++}A.
\]

Assume the original relation is admitted, A is distinct and disjoint from R, and every added cell has its source in S and a nonempty action label. Concatenation preserves distinct triples; first-occurrence filtering preserves a nonempty distinct received carrier. Old endpoints remain admitted, new images enter V' by construction, and every old totality witness remains in R'. These facts prove extension admission.

Distributing filter and map over concatenation gives the exact order equations

\[
 P_{R'}(v)=F([s:(s,v,a)\in R]\mathbin{++}[s:(s,v,a)\in A]),
\]
\[
 I_{R'}(s)=F([v:(s,v,a)\in R]\mathbin{++}[v:(s,v,a)\in A]).
\]

Membership is consequently old membership or existence of a matching added action cell, in both directions. Shared images retain every predecessor through this same construction. At the identity base, S=V=[s] and R=[(s,s,"identity")]. The guards hold directly, P_R(s)=[s], correction returns uniqueSource(s), and the unchanged word has empty-One trace. This proves the base and finite action-successor clauses on the registered domain.


105. Complete coding support and held codebooks

Coding support enumerates the full ordered Cartesian product of at least two distinct labels over a nonempty position list. Its native membership characterization retains exactly every word of the held width whose labels belong to the alphabet. The cardinality is the alphabet size raised to that width, and distinct alphabet labels give duplicate-free support. The original position-list domain is preserved, including repeated position names.

A codebook returns the original held selection, in order, when it is nonempty, duplicate-free and contained in the supplied support. Native admission and membership proofs show that selecting from complete coding support gives every retained codeword the same position width and allowed labels. No additional guard is imposed on the supplied support itself.

Codeword difference retains the complete indexed changed-position trace and both labels at each change. Equality gives empty One; unequal widths retain Coding's original common-carrier error. The general position correspondence follows the established exact Noise and Error derivation.

The repetition constructor maps each supplied label to its constant word over the full position list. Its original helper accepts repeated labels, preserving output multiplicity and order. Under complete support's distinct-label condition, nonempty width makes the repetition map injective and its words distinct; each word belongs to the generated support. Independent source-expression comparisons pass 600 complete-support cases, 600 repetition cases, 1,785 held-codebook cases and 1,600 difference cases.


For an alphabet list A and position list P, coding support succeeds precisely when

\[
 |A|\ge2\land\operatorname{Distinct}(A)\land P\ne[].
\]

Put n=|P|. The output is the complete ordered word list W_n(A), with W_0(A)=[[]] and W_{n+1}(A)=concat_{a∈A}[a::w:w∈W_n(A)]. The head/tail decomposition proves by induction

\[
 w\in W_n(A)\iff |w|=n\land\forall a\in w,\ a\in A,
 \qquad |W_n(A)|=|A|^n.
\]

For distinct A, different leading labels separate the generated blocks and the tail induction excludes repeats within each block. Thus the complete support is distinct. Position names enter this constructor through their list length; no distinct-position-name condition is added.

For an arbitrary supplied support U and held list H, the codebook result has the exact characterization

\[
 \operatorname{codebook}(U,H)=\operatorname{ok}(K)
 \iff H\ne[]\land\operatorname{Distinct}(H)\land H\subseteq U\land K=H.
\]

The operation therefore retains the exact order and contents of H. If U=W_n(A) is admitted complete support, every w∈K belongs to U, so the preceding membership theorem gives |w|=n and every cell in A. For two retained words u,v, their two width equalities imply |u|=|v|. No guard on U beyond held-list membership is introduced by codebook itself.

For equal-width u,v, differenceTrace uses exactly the Noise and Error changed-position construction. Consequently

\[
 \operatorname{differenceTrace}(u,v)=\operatorname{ok}(\mathrm{emptyOne})\iff u=v,
\]

and its changes result consists exactly of the one-based printed positions where u and v differ, with both labels retained. On unequal widths it returns Coding's own common-position-carrier error, rather than substituting Noise and Error's error text.

Define the constant word r_P(a)=map(λp.a,P). Repetition admission requires only |A|≥2 and P≠[], and returns [r_P(a):a∈A] in alphabet-list order. Induction on P gives

\[
 |r_P(a)|=|P|,\qquad b\in r_P(a)\Longrightarrow b=a.
\]

Thus any emitted word from a label a∈A belongs to admitted W_n(A). Since P is nonempty, equality r_P(a)=r_P(b) forces equality of their first cells, a=b. The map is therefore injective, and distinct A yields a distinct repetition list. Repeated labels in the broader repetition helper remain repeated output words; this proof uses the distinctness already supplied by complete-support admission and does not change that helper's guard.


106. Complete single-label error images and exact decoding

Single-label image generation begins with the unchanged source word, then visits every retained position and every supplied label in order, skipping only labels equal to the held label at that position. Native membership is equivalent to identity or to a decomposition into an unchanged prefix, one different supplied replacement label and an unchanged suffix. This equivalence includes both completeness and exclusion of unregistered replacements. Every image has the source width, and generated changes are nonidentity. A source in complete coding support has all its images in that support. The raw helper retains repeated supplied labels and their repeated outputs.

The complete error family retains each source occurrence with its full image list. Detection holds exactly when each image is either its source or outside the codebook. Correction holds exactly when every positional pair of source image families has no common received word. Native equivalences establish these conditions over the full generated lists, including raw duplicate source occurrences.

Decoding filters source occurrences in their original order by exact received-image membership. A singleton gives the original unique-source record; any other length gives the entire closed source class. The native correction theorem proves that disjoint source families yield an empty or singleton filtered list. Consequently, every registered received image of a correctable code decodes to its source. Conversely, every unique decoded source is a retained codeword with that received image in its registered family. No distance or prior is used. Independent source-expression comparisons pass 1,600 image lists, 465 complete families, 465 detection decisions, 465 correction decisions and 2,790 decoding records.


For the supplied label list A, the complete changed-image recursion is

\[
 D_A([])=[],\qquad D_A(h::t)=[a::t:a\in A,\ a\ne h]\mathbin{++}[h::v:v\in D_A(t)].
\]

The image list is E_A(w)=[w]++D_A(w). The first branch changes the first position; the second retains it and recursively changes a later position. Induction, splitting on whether the unchanged prefix is empty, proves the exact equivalence

\[
 v\in D_A(w)\iff\exists p,h,t,a,\ w=p\mathbin{++}(h::t)
 \land a\in A\land a\ne h\land v=p\mathbin{++}(a::t).
\]

The forward proof extracts the replacement from the head filter or prepends the unchanged head to the tail induction's prefix. Conversely, an empty prefix selects the head branch; a nonempty prefix selects the recursive branch. Thus E_A(w) contains precisely identity and every registered single-label replacement. Mapping and filtering retain the original label multiplicities and visit order.

Each branch preserves length, giving |v|=|w| for every v∈E_A(w). A changed image cannot equal w: cancelling the unchanged prefix would give a=h, contradicting the replacement guard. If all labels of w lie in A, each changed image also uses only A, since its unchanged cells retain that property and its replacement was selected from A. Together with preserved width, complete-support membership proves closure of all images inside the admitted support.

For a code list C, the family [(w,E_A(w)):w∈C] has exactly |C| rows, one per source occurrence. Expanding the Boolean all/any tests yields

\[
 \operatorname{Detect}(C,A)\iff\forall w\in C,\forall v\in E_A(w),\ v=w\lor v\notin C,
\]
\[
 \operatorname{Correct}(C,A)\iff\forall(u,w)\in P(C),\
 \neg\exists v,\ v\in E_A(u)\land v\in E_A(w),
\]

where P(C) is the full positional pair list. The pair condition retains raw source occurrences rather than silently deduplicating C.

The received source class is the ordered filter

\[
 H_{C,A}(v)=[w\in C:v\in E_A(w)],\qquad
 w\in H_{C,A}(v)\iff w\in C\land v\in E_A(w).
\]

Decoding applies the singleton/closed-class record constructor to this exact list. Hence decode(v)=uniqueSource(w) iff H_{C,A}(v)=[w]; every nonsingleton result retains H_{C,A}(v) intact.

To prove correct decoding, induct on C. If its head has no image v, the filtered list equals the tail's class and correction restricts to the tail's positional pairs. If the head does have image v, every tail occurrence must reject v: accepting it would give a shared image for that head–tail positional pair, contradicting Correct. The filtered tail is therefore empty and the whole class is the head singleton. Thus

\[
 \operatorname{Correct}(C,A)\Longrightarrow
 H_{C,A}(v)=[]\ \lor\ \exists w,\ H_{C,A}(v)=[w].
\]

When w∈C and v∈E_A(w), class membership excludes the empty case and identifies the singleton as w, proving decode(v)=uniqueSource(w). Conversely, a unique-source record gives that singleton class; its member must be a retained source with v in its generated family. This proves both recovery and provenance using the full registered image enumeration.


107. General coding action succession and retained source provenance

A registered error family assigns its complete finite image list to each retained codeword. The general native detection, correction and decoding loops are definitionally the original single-label loops when instantiated with those original images. The singleton-action base supplies one image per codeword; correction then holds exactly when every positional pair has different action images.

Appending an action's registered images preserves the old image list and its order. The detection successor theorem is an equivalence: extended detection holds exactly when both prior and added images satisfy the original codebook-membership rule. The correction successor theorem checks every old–old, old–new, new–old and new–new pair of image supports. Their joint disjointness is equivalent to correction of the extended family. The received source class extends by exactly the sources whose new image lists contain that form. These proofs apply to arbitrary finite registered actions and preserve raw list multiplicity.

The stated total source/codeword relation retains one codeword for each registered source. Native recovery proves that every received image of that codeword in a correctable family decodes to the held codeword while preserving the source row. The same recovery result follows after an action addition precisely when the complete successor disjointness conditions hold. No injective message assignment beyond the stated source-map constraint is imposed.

The original width-three repetition example is a held codebook and its complete single-label image families are disjoint. The one-position uncoded control fails correction; a width-two repetition family also fails, giving the exact finite separation witness for the retained width. Coding includes all 2,048 original kernels and all six original operational witnesses. The cumulative 19,745 source-expression comparisons include 9,375 action-successor detection, correction and decoding outputs. Coding remains within the original finite classical boundary, without a stochastic error model, metric threshold, imported asymptotic rate or quantum-code claim.

Fresh native execution of the complete Coding program matches all 2,048 original candidate lookups together with every registration, census, closure, control and digest field and the unknown-identifier rejection. These results accompany the native source derivations and complete the Coding entry on its original finite classical domain.


Let F(w) be the ordered image list registered for codeword w. For action cells R, its registered-image constructor is [v:(w,v,a)∈R], retaining all matching occurrences. Membership is equivalent to existence of a matching action a. The general detection, correction and source-class definitions are exactly those of section 106 with E_A(w) replaced by F(w); substituting E_A recovers the original single-label operations by definition.

For a singleton action f, put F_f(w)=[f(w)]. Two such lists overlap iff their sole values agree, so

\[
 \operatorname{Correct}(C,F_f)\iff\forall(u,w)\in P(C),\ f(u)\ne f(w).
\]

For old and added families F,G, define (F⊕G)(w)=F(w)++G(w). Membership splits into the old or added list. Applying that split to the universal detection condition proves both directions of

\[
 \operatorname{Detect}(C,F\oplus G)\iff
 \operatorname{Detect}(C,F)\land\operatorname{Detect}(C,G).
\]

Define X_C(F,G) to mean that, for every positional pair (u,w)∈P(C), no received word belongs to both F(u) and G(w). Expanding the two append memberships yields precisely four cases:

\[
 \operatorname{Correct}(C,F\oplus G)\iff
 \operatorname{Correct}(C,F)\land X_C(F,G)\land X_C(G,F)\land\operatorname{Correct}(C,G).
\]

For the forward direction, each alleged shared image in one case embeds into the two extended lists and contradicts their disjointness. Conversely, an overlap in the extended lists belongs to one of old–old, old–new, new–old or new–new, and the corresponding conjunct excludes it. Both cross orientations are retained because P(C) uses source order.

The extended source class remains the single ordered filter

\[
 H_{C,F\oplus G}(v)=[w\in C:v\in F(w)\lor v\in G(w)].
\]

Each source occurrence is tested once against the disjunction; the two separate filtered lists are not concatenated. This preserves the actual source-list multiplicity even when both families contain v for the same source.

For registered source labels S, code list C and row list E, the source-map condition is

\[
 \forall s\in S,\ \exists c\in C,\ (s,c)\in E
 \land\forall d,\ (s,d)\in E\Rightarrow d=c.
\]

Select that retained c for a source s. Under Correct(C,F), the general source-class induction proves that every v∈F(c) decodes to uniqueSource(c). The row (s,c) is retained alongside this recovery statement. Different source labels are not required by this condition to have different codewords. Under the four successor conditions, apply the same result to F⊕G; this establishes recovery after the action addition without strengthening the source-map domain.

For the concrete alphabet [a,b] and width three, the complete lists are

\[
 E(aaa)=[aaa,baa,aba,aab],\qquad E(bbb)=[bbb,abb,bab,bba].
\]

They are disjoint; every changed word lies outside [aaa,bbb], and baa occurs only in the aaa family. The complete support has 2^3=8 distinct words, while aaa and bbb differ at all three positions. These computations give the retained support, difference, detection, correction and decoding witnesses. For width two,

\[
 E(aa)=[aa,ba,ab],\qquad E(bb)=[bb,ab,ba],
\]

so ab and ba witness failure of correction. At width one, E(a)=[a,b] and E(b)=[b,a] overlap as well. These full finite lists establish the protected and unprotected witness results directly.


108. Exact observation incidence and conditional class restriction

Mutual and conditional information begins with nonempty duplicate-free support and an observation assigning exactly one label to every support form. The native validator retains the original support-first error order, observation-key uniqueness and exact support coverage. Admitted dictionary rows preserve insertion order, while class generation visits support order and retains each observed label on its first occurrence. Empty labels and literal empty-One source names remain in the original raw domain.

Native validation equivalence connects this admission to the existing exact observation partition. Each class contains exactly the support forms mapped to its label; classes are nonempty, cover the support and are disjoint for different labels. The complete marginal-label product retains every pair in left-major/right-minor order. Each joint member belongs precisely to the held support and both corresponding observation classes.

Conditional membership first restricts to a given class, then filters by the target observation. Native coverage and disjointness prove that these target restrictions partition every given class, retaining source order and exact provenance. Independent source-expression comparisons pass 637 validations, 637 class outputs, 5,832 joint-member filters, 5,832 conditional-member filters and 648 complete ordered label products.


Let S be the ordered support and O the observation rows. Validation succeeds exactly when

\[
 S\ne[]\land\operatorname{Distinct}(S)\land\operatorname{Distinct}(\operatorname{keys}(O))
 \land\operatorname{keys}(O)\subseteq S\land S\subseteq\operatorname{keys}(O).
\]

The support conditions are tested before the map conditions, and success returns O unchanged. These are precisely the exact-partition admission conditions: splitting or recombining the conjunction proves the bridge in both directions. Write f_O(s) for lookup and F_O(a)=[s∈S:f_O(s)=Some(a)]. Observed labels L_O are retained on first occurrence while scanning S. Then a∈L_O iff some s∈S has image a, and the emitted classes are [(a,F_O(a)):a∈L_O]. The witnessing s makes each emitted fibre nonempty; total lookup supplies a fibre for every s; deterministic lookup makes two fibres sharing a member have equal labels.

For observations L,R, the full pair enumeration is concat_{a∈L_L}[(a,b):b∈L_R]. Unpacking map and concatenation membership proves that (a,b) occurs iff both marginal labels occur. Define

\[
 J_{ab}=[s\in S:f_L(s)=\operatorname{Some}(a)\land f_R(s)=\operatorname{Some}(b)].
\]

Filter membership proves exactly these three conditions: source membership and the two image equalities. Conditional restriction, with given observation G and target T, is

\[
 C_{ab}=\operatorname{filter}_{f_T=\operatorname{Some}(b)}F_G(a),\qquad
 s\in C_{ab}\iff s\in S\land f_G(s)=\operatorname{Some}(a)\land f_T(s)=\operatorname{Some}(b).
\]

For any s∈F_G(a), target-map coverage supplies b and places s in C_{ab}; this b occurs in L_T. If s also belongs to C_{ac}, equality of its target lookup forces b=c. Thus the restrictions cover the given fibre and different target labels are disjoint, with all filters preserving source order.


109. Exact joint fractions and marginal-product comparison

The joint ledger visits every pair of observed labels in left-major/right-minor order. Each row retains the exact support-ordered joint member list, its normalized part of the whole support, both normalized marginal parts, their normalized product and the original comparison label. An empty joint cell retains the original empty-One presentation and empty-One-below-product relation. Literal empty-One source labels remain admitted.

Native proofs establish positive marginal counts, exact count-ratio values of every present joint cell and both marginals, the product fraction value, and the four comparison cases through exact integer cross-products. Full label projection proves that every marginal-label pair is retained in source order; admitted ledger evaluation preserves left-then-right validation. Independent comparisons pass 1,360 complete ledgers, 144 guard-order cases and 252 rational comparison cases, bringing the observation and joint-ledger comparison total to 15,342.


Put N=|S|, l_a=|F_L(a)|, r_b=|F_R(b)| and j_{ab}=|J_{ab}|. Validation gives N>0; each emitted marginal label has a witnessing state, giving l_a,r_b>0. Normalize the marginal ratios to positive reduced pairs (u_a,v_a) and (x_b,y_b). Their exact value equations are

\[
 l_a v_a=u_aN,\qquad r_b y_b=x_bN.
\]

The normalized product (p,q) satisfies (u_a x_b)q=p(v_a y_b), with p,q positive. If J_{ab} is empty, its count part is structural empty One and its displayed member list is ["empty-One"]. Otherwise its part (h,k) is the normalized positive count ratio, with

\[
 j_{ab}k=hN,\qquad h>0,\quad k>0,\quad\gcd(h,k)=1.
\]

Nonempty member lists are displayed unchanged, so a literal source name "empty-One" remains present. Comparison is defined by the complete four-way case split

\[
 \operatorname{relation}(J_{ab},p/q)=
 \begin{cases}
 \text{empty-One-below-product},&J_{ab}=[],\\
 \text{equal},&J_{ab}\ne[]\land hq=pk,\\
 \text{joint-below-product},&J_{ab}\ne[]\land hq<pk,\\
 \text{joint-above-product},&J_{ab}\ne[]\land pk<hq.
 \end{cases}
\]

The empty constructor is distinct from every positive fraction. For a fraction, equality is tested first, then less-than; when both fail, natural-number order gives the reverse strict inequality. These cases establish the comparison labels without rounding.

Each row is constructed from its two labels, presented members, joint part, both marginal parts, their product and this comparison. Induction over the complete label-pair list proves that projecting the row labels returns the input pair list exactly. Hence no absent joint cell or marginal-label combination is dropped. The ledger validates the left observation first and the right observation second, retaining the first error encountered; successful evaluation returns those complete rows.


110. Conditional fractions, resolution and retained dependence

The conditional ledger enumerates every target label inside every given label in the original given-major order. Each row retains the full intersection and its exact normalized ratio to the given class. Native proofs establish the empty-cell criterion, positive intersection count and fraction value, complete ordered label-pair coverage and admitted evaluation. These rows use the previously established conditional class partition.

The executable resolution rule counts, within each given class, precisely the presented member lists unequal to the source empty-One sentinel and requires one such row together with a nonempty target dictionary. This exact raw comparison is retained even for a source form literally named empty-One. Native equivalences characterize that count rule without adding a domain restriction.

Factorization is exactly the conjunction of joint-cell presence and equality with the marginal product across every ledger row. Dependence retains, in original order, all and only the rows whose comparison is unequal, returning the source sentinel when none remain. Native all-cell and retained-cell equivalences accompany 1,504 independent comparisons for each of the conditional ledger, resolution, factorization and complete dependence ledger. The cumulative observation and ledger comparison total is 21,358.


For given label a and target label b, write g_a=|F_G(a)| and c_{ab}=|C_{ab}|. Every emitted given label has g_a>0. An empty C_{ab} receives structural empty One; a nonempty cell has a normalized positive part (u,v) satisfying

\[
 c_{ab}v=u g_a,\qquad c_{ab}>0.
\]

Conditional rows enumerate L_G×L_T in given-major order, retaining the presented intersection and this part. Induction on the row constructor proves that the label projection is exactly that full product. Validation nevertheless follows the executable argument order: target first, then given. Both this error order and the different row-enumeration order are preserved.

Let e=["empty-One"] be the source's literal list sentinel, and let R be the presented conditional rows. The exact counted-row predicate and resolution rule are

\[
 B_a(r)\iff r.\mathrm{givenLabel}=a\land r.\mathrm{members}\ne e,
\]
\[
 \operatorname{Resolved}\iff
 \left(\forall(a,F_G(a))\text{ in the given classes},\ |[r\in R:B_a(r)]|=1\right)
 \land\text{target dictionary}\ne[].
\]

Expanding the Boolean conjunction, filter and all predicates proves this characterization. It deliberately compares the presented member list: a real singleton source list ["empty-One"] is excluded by the same equality test, even if its exact fraction is positive. No extra source-name guard changes that behavior.

For joint rows Q, the factorization predicate is

\[
 \operatorname{Factorizes}\iff\forall r\in Q,
 \left(\exists p,\ r.\mathrm{jointPart}=\mathrm{part}(p)\right)
 \land r.\mathrm{relation}=\text{equal}.
\]

An empty joint constructor returns false; a positive constructor tests comparison equality. Induction over Q establishes the all-cell equivalence. The responsible list is D=[r∈Q:r.relation≠equal], so filter membership proves that it contains all and only unequal rows in their existing order. Dependence is structural empty One exactly when D=[], and otherwise retains D in full. Errors from joint-ledger construction propagate through both decisions.


111. Fresh-source joint and conditional succession

For a fresh source form, each observation extends by its one assigned label. Native admission preservation keeps total observation coverage and duplicate-free support. The joint member list after extension is exactly the old list followed by the fresh source when both assigned labels match the cell, and exactly the old list otherwise. Thus precisely the designated joint cell receives the new source. Conditional intersections satisfy the same ordered successor equation with given and target labels in their original roles.

The exact joint fraction is recomputed against the extended support count. The conditional fraction is recomputed against the extended given-class count, using the retained intersection plus its possible new member. Native ratio equations establish these updates for arbitrary finite support. The singleton base has one joint member and one conditional member with normalized whole part. Marginal class and label succession use the established observation-extension derivation.

All five original operational witnesses pass native proof: half-parts conditioned on the first coordinate, exact factorization of the complete grid, empty grid dependence, resolution by the copied observation, and nonfactorization with retained dependence cells for the nontrivial copy. Independent fresh-source comparisons pass 1,344 each of conditional ledgers, resolution, factorization and full dependence ledgers, bringing the cumulative source-expression total to 26,734.


Append a fresh x∉S with assigned left and right labels l,r. Freshness preserves support/key distinctness, and appending the same source key to each observation preserves total coverage. Old lookups remain unchanged; lookup of x gives its appended label. Induction through the joint filter therefore gives

\[
 J'_{ab}=J_{ab}\mathbin{++}
 \begin{cases}[x],&l=a\land r=b,\\\lbrack\rbrack,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
\]

The base case evaluates the two label equalities at x. In the induction step, old lookup equality preserves the head's keep/drop decision and the tail induction supplies the appended term. Thus only the designated cell gains x. Conditional restriction equals the joint filter with given and target in that order; substituting their assigned labels g,t yields

\[
 C'_{ab}=C_{ab}\mathbin{++}
 \begin{cases}[x],&g=a\land t=b,\\\lbrack\rbrack,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
\]

Write δ_{ab}=1 when both labels match and 0 otherwise. For any positive successor joint part (u',v'), the prior joint-value theorem and the exact list equation give

\[
 (|J_{ab}|+\delta_{ab})v'=u'(|S|+1).
\]

For a positive successor conditional part (h',k'), given-fibre succession supplies the changed denominator as well:

\[
 (|C_{ab}|+\mathbf1_{g=a\land t=b})k'
 =h'(|F_G(a)|+\mathbf1_{g=a}).
\]

Empty cells retain the empty constructor until they gain a member. In the singleton base, both joint and conditional lists are [x]; normalization of (1,1) yields numerator and denominator 1. These equations cover the complete finite update, including newly occurring labels.

For the witness support [aa,ab,ba,bb], the first- and second-coordinate marginal classes each have size 2, and every coordinate joint cell has size 1. Thus each joint part is 1/4=(1/2)(1/2), every conditional part is 1/2, factorization holds and the dependence list is empty. Comparing the first coordinate with its copy gives diagonal counts 2 and off-diagonal counts 0. The diagonal parts 1/2 exceed the product 1/4; off-diagonal cells are empty-below-product. Hence factorization fails and all four unequal rows remain. Each given copy-class has exactly one nonsentinel presented target cell, establishing the resolution witness.

 \operatorname{accept}(c)\iff\bigwedge_{i=1}^{10}(c_i=\mathrm{admitted})

Any rejected field contradicts the corresponding projection of that equality and therefore rejects the candidate.
