113. Exact conservation and loss of source distinctions

A transformation assigns exactly one image to each member of complete duplicate-free source support. The native validator preserves the original source-first and map-second guard order and errors. Image names remain unrestricted strings. Complete source pairs visit each source form followed by every later source form in the original order.

The transformation ledger classifies each pair by exact image equality. Different images retain the distinction; equal images close it. Native membership equivalences prove that these two ledgers disjointly exhaust the complete source-pair list. Empty ledgers have the original empty-One presentation. The full record retains source support, first-occurrence image support, both pair ledgers and exact predecessor fibres.

For an admitted transformation, a closed pair lies in a common image fibre. Conversely, every source pair whose two members lie in a common image fibre is closed. These statements retain the exact source provenance of loss. Independent comparisons pass 813 validations, 121 complete ordered pair outputs and 813 full transformation records, including empty-string and literal empty-One labels, reversed mappings and invalid source/map cases.


For source list S and transformation rows T, admission requires S≠[], distinct S, distinct keys(T), and key-set equality with S. Support is checked before the map, and success retains T unchanged. Let f_T(s) denote its admitted image. The complete pair recursion is

\[
 P([])=[],\qquad P(x::R)=[(x,y):y\in R]\mathbin{++}P(R).
\]

The two ordered subledgers are

\[
 R_T=[(x,y)\in P(S):f_T(x)\ne f_T(y)],\qquad
 C_T=[(x,y)\in P(S):f_T(x)=f_T(y)].
\]

Filter membership proves each defining equivalence. Decidable equality places every pair in one of the two lists; simultaneous membership would assert both equality and its negation. Thus these ledgers exhaust the original pairs without overlap, retaining their source traversal order. Empty lists are presented as structural empty One, and nonempty lists are retained intact.

The full record is (S,L_T,present(R_T),present(C_T),[(a,F_T(a)):a∈L_T]), where L_T is the first-occurrence image-label list and F_T(a)=[s∈S:f_T(s)=a]. For a closed pair, total lookup supplies its first endpoint's label a; equality gives that same label for the second endpoint, placing both in F_T(a). Conversely, two endpoints of a source pair in F_T(a) have equal images and the pair is closed. This proves the correspondence between closed distinctions and their exact predecessor fibres.


114. Composition and downstream retention

Transformation composition first validates the source map, derives its middle support in source-order first occurrence, and validates the second map on that complete middle support. Its output retains one row per original source form in the original order. Native proofs establish exact successive lookup and preservation of transformation admission.

When two source forms have equal first images, their composed images are equal. Every closed upstream pair therefore remains closed. Since the retained and closed ledgers are disjoint and exhaustive, each retained composite pair must have been retained upstream. The native downstream-retention theorem proves this subset relation for arbitrary finite admitted compositions, and establishes that the original executable law returns true. No external record or newly created distinction is introduced. Independent comparisons pass 648 complete compositions and 648 downstream results, including the original validation errors and reordered maps; the cumulative comparison total is 3,043.


Composition validates T on S, forms its complete first-occurrence image support L_T, and validates U on L_T. The emitted rows are

\[
 V=[(s,f_U(f_T(s))):s\in S].
\]

The implementation's lookup default is unreachable on these admitted queries: totality supplies f_T(s), its occurrence puts it in L_T, and U is total on L_T. Induction on the row construction gives keys(V)=S and image_V(s)=Some(f_U(f_T(s))) for s∈S. Therefore V preserves distinct keys, coverage and source order and is an admitted transformation.

If (x,y)∈C_T, then f_T(x)=f_T(y). Applying f_U yields f_U(f_T(x))=f_U(f_T(y)), so

\[
 C_T\subseteq C_V.
\]

Suppose a retained composite pair were not retained upstream. Its source-pair membership and exhaustive equality split would place it in C_T, hence C_V, contradicting disjointness of R_V and C_V. Consequently

\[
 R_V\subseteq R_T.
\]

The executable downstream law removes only the presentation constructor and tests membership in R_T for every pair of R_V. The subset result supplies every test, so the all predicate returns true for admitted compositions. Validation errors retain their original order through the ledger, composition and final-ledger calls.


115. Exact predecessor records and recovery

The reverse record visits the predecessor fibres in their original order. A singleton fibre returns its source label directly; every other fibre returns the original held-predecessor-labels record with its complete ordered member list. Native equivalences establish precisely when each form is produced. Every source with the recorded image equals the unique predecessor in a singleton record. For a held record, membership is exactly source membership together with the recorded image. Two distinct retained predecessors exclude a unique record.

The executable reversibility decision retains V3's comparison of the number of distinct admitted dictionary images with the number of source forms. Independent comparisons pass 813 such decisions and 813 complete reverse records, including original errors and unrestricted string labels. The cumulative transformation comparison total is 4,669. The native count-decision equivalence with singleton fibres is established below.


For a predecessor member list M, the exact record cases are

\[
 B(M)=\begin{cases}\operatorname{unique}(s),&M=[s],\\
 \operatorname{heldLabels}(M),&|M|\ne1.\end{cases}
\]

Splitting M into empty, singleton and longer lists proves B(M)=unique(s) iff M=[s], and B(M)=heldLabels(H) iff H=M and |M|≠1. Reverse rows map (a,F_T(a)) to (a,B(F_T(a))) without reordering fibres. Thus a unique record for a supplies F_T(a)=[s]. Any source t with image a belongs to that fibre and hence equals s. A held record satisfies

\[
 t\in H\iff t\in S\land f_T(t)=a.
\]

If two different predecessors lie in M, uniqueness would force both to equal its alleged singleton member and contradict their difference. No member is selected from a merged fibre.

Let D=map(second,T) be the dictionary-value list, and let L(D) retain its first occurrences. After validation, the executable reversibility result is exactly

\[
 \operatorname{isReversible}(S,T)=\operatorname{ok}(|L(D)|=|S|).
\]

This is the original count expression; the next derivation relates it to the fibre-based recovery records.


116. Reversibility is equivalent to singleton fibres

For every transformation admitted by the original validator, the executable distinct-image count test returns true exactly when every observed predecessor fibre contains one source form. The native proof retains the original dictionary-value count. First-occurrence deduplication preserves list length exactly when the value list has no repetitions. Unique source keys establish exact correspondence between dictionary values and the images obtained by traversing the admitted source support. Their distinct-image counts are equal, regardless of dictionary row order.

Distinct mapped source values are equivalent to injectivity on the held source support. Injectivity makes each nonempty image fibre a singleton; conversely, singleton fibres identify any two sources with the same image. These implications prove both directions of V3's reversibility statement on arbitrary finite admitted supports, without a new guard or an assumed inverse. The established reverse-record recovery theorems then give direct recovery for singleton fibres and complete retained predecessor alternatives for merged fibres.


A first-occurrence scan can never lengthen a list. If it skips a head already seen, its tail output has length at most the tail length and therefore strictly less than the original list length. Hence equality of input and output lengths forces every head to be retained and, recursively, absent from the later list. Conversely, a distinct list with no initially seen member is retained unchanged. Starting with an empty seen list gives

\[
 |L(X)|=|X|\iff\operatorname{Distinct}(X).
\]

Let D=map(second,T) and V=[f_T(s):s∈S]. A value in D has a dictionary row; unique keys make lookup return that row's value, and coverage puts its key in S, so the value occurs in V. Conversely, lookup of any admitted s yields a retained dictionary row and thus a value of D. Therefore D and V have identical value membership. Their first-occurrence lists are distinct and mutually contained; finite distinct-list length bounds in both directions give |L(D)|=|L(V)|. Since |V|=|S|,

\[
 |L(D)|=|S|\iff|L(V)|=|V|\iff\operatorname{Distinct}(V).
\]

On distinct S, injectivity of f_T makes V distinct: a repeated head value would identify its source with a later source, contradicting distinctness, and the tail follows by induction. Conversely, if V is distinct, equal mapped values cannot occur at two different source positions; splitting source membership into head and tail proves injectivity. Thus the original count test is equivalent to injectivity on S.

Each observed fibre is nonempty and distinct. Under injectivity, choose one of its members s; every other member has the same image and must equal s, so the fibre is [s]. Conversely, two sources with equal images lie in the same observed fibre, and singleton fibres make those sources equal. Combining the equivalences proves

\[
 \operatorname{isReversible}(S,T)=\operatorname{ok}(\mathrm{true})
 \iff f_T\text{ is injective on }S
 \iff\forall a\in L_T,\ \exists s,\ F_T(a)=[s].
\]

The argument uses membership and distinct counts, so arbitrary admitted dictionary-row order is preserved. Recovery follows from the existing reverse records, without postulating an inverse function.


117. Source succession and conservation of pair accounting

The singleton transformation has one source, one held image, one singleton predecessor fibre and empty retained and closed pair ledgers. Native evaluation proves the complete original record. Adding a fresh source and one image row preserves the admitted transformation conditions. Every image fibre retains its old members and appends the new source exactly when its label matches the added image.

The complete pair list after extension retains the original source traversal order, adding one pair between each prior source and the fresh source. A retained successor pair is exactly a previously retained pair or a new old/fresh pair with unequal images. A closed successor pair is exactly a previously closed pair or a new old/fresh pair with equal images. These native equivalences establish exhaustive successor accounting for arbitrary finite support.

All five original operational witnesses pass native proof: identity conservation, exact prefix loss, the identity/prefix reversibility boundary, complete held predecessor records and downstream erasure. Fresh-source comparisons pass 1,395 validations, 15 complete ordered pair lists and 1,395 full ledgers. The cumulative source-expression comparison total is 7,474.


For fresh x∉S and appended row (x,v), put S'=S++[x] and T'=T++[(x,v)]. The established observation successor preserves admission, retains all old images, and gives image_{T'}(x)=Some(v). Its exact fibre update is

\[
 F_{T'}(a)=F_T(a)\mathbin{++}\begin{cases}[x],&v=a,\\\lbrack\rbrack,&v\ne a.\end{cases}
\]

The pair list retains its nested source order. Explicitly, E_x([])=[] and E_x(y::R)=([(y,z):z∈R]++[(y,x)])++E_x(R), and P(S++[x])=E_x(S). Membership is exactly old pair membership or a new pair (s,x) with s∈S. Substituting the old and fresh image equations into the two filters yields

\[
 p\in R_{T'}\iff p\in R_T\lor\exists s\in S,\ p=(s,x)\land f_T(s)\ne v,
\]
\[
 p\in C_{T'}\iff p\in C_T\lor\exists s\in S,\ p=(s,x)\land f_T(s)=v.
\]

Both directions follow from the pair split: old endpoints keep their images; a new pair compares exactly its old image with v. The formulas classify the complete ordered successor list, without replacing it by a differently ordered concatenation of pair classes.

At the singleton base, S=[s], T=[(s,a)], the ledger is ([s],[a],emptyOne,emptyOne,[(a,[s])]). For the witness S=[aa,ab,ba,bb], P(S) has six pairs. Identity retains all six and closes none. Prefix projection closes exactly (aa,ab) and (ba,bb), retaining the other four; its fibres are [aa,ab] and [ba,bb]. Identity has four distinct images and is reversible, whereas prefix projection has two and is not. Both prefix reverse rows retain their two predecessor labels. Composing prefix projection with a↦one,b↦one closes all six pairs and leaves no retained pair, establishing the downstream-erasure witness.

Fresh native execution matches all 1,024 original candidate lookups, registration, census, closure, controls, hashes and unknown-identifier rejection. Projecting this equality onto any rejected field gives a contradiction, proving rejection for each failed criterion. This complete finite classification accompanies the source-pair, composition, fibre, recovery and successor derivations above.


119. Classical state, observation classes and deterministic realization

A classical information state retains one member of the complete nonempty duplicate-free support together with that support as provenance. A total observation partitions the same support by its labels in first-occurrence order. Each probability row retains its entire ordered microstate class and the reduced positive fraction of complete support occupied by that class. Native proofs identify the exact class membership, retained order and fraction count equation. Deterministic pushforward constructs corresponding image rows with complete predecessor classes and exact parts.

Observation first requires exactly one row for its observed label. A singleton member class returns its held classical state; every other retained class returns all its members. The native singleton equivalence and unresolved-member identity preserve this boundary. Deterministic realization visits the probability rows in order, pairing every original microstate with the deterministic-member tag while retaining the class label and its exact support part. Source-expression comparisons cover 6,296 support, ledger, resolution and realization outputs and error cases, including empty labels and original validation order. Exact-whole closure and the source microstate-successor construction are established below.


Canonical support admission is S≠[] and Distinct(S). A classical-state request succeeds exactly when this holds and h∈S, returning (heldState=h,supportProvenance=S). For observation O, probability validation checks the support first, then distinct keys and complete key coverage. Pushforward validation has the same successful domain but checks duplicate map keys before support validation, retaining the original short-circuit errors.

For each first-occurrence label a, let F_a=[s∈S:f_O(s)=a], m_a=|F_a| and N=|S|. The probability row is (a,F_a,(u_a,v_a)), with the positive reduced part satisfying

\[
 m_a v_a=u_aN,\qquad u_a>0,\quad v_a>0,\quad\gcd(u_a,v_a)=1.
\]

The row maps from the entropy ledger preserve its label, members and part exactly. Induction on those maps proves that projecting label/member pairs returns the original ordered partition. Pushforward uses the same record with image/predecessor field names, so its source provenance and fraction equation are identical.

Resolution first forms H=[r in rows:r.label=a]. It returns the outside-observation error unless H=[r] is exactly a singleton. For that sole row,

\[
 \operatorname{resolveMembers}(M)=\operatorname{classicalState}(s)\iff M=[s].
\]

The empty and longer-list cases return unresolvedClass(M) with M unchanged. Deterministic realization maps each row to (a,[(s,"deterministic-member"):s∈F_a],(u_a,v_a)). Projecting first coordinates recovers F_a by list induction, while the label and fraction are unchanged by construction. The realization therefore exposes every original member in order.


120. Complete observation-class cardinality

For every total observation of a finite support, the sum of the full fibre cardinalities over the first-occurrence observed labels equals the support cardinality. The proof counts each source occurrence against distinct observed labels: exactly its own image contributes once. Induction over the support then reconstructs the complete count. No equal-class-size assumption is used.

The raw exact-whole helper retains its Fraction input domain, including signed and zero fractions. Integer cross-multiplication accumulates the exact numerator and denominator; the nonempty ledger is whole precisely when those values are equal. Generated probability and pushforward rows supply their original positive reduced fractions to this calculation. 3,614 independent source-expression comparisons pass, including 2,801 raw fraction lists and 813 generated-ledger or original-error cases. The universal reduced-fraction bridge and exact-whole result are established below.


Let L be the distinct list of observed labels, and define

\[
 H_L(a)=\sum_{b\in L}\mathbf1_{b=a},\qquad
 K(S,O,L)=\sum_{a\in L}|F_O(a)|.
\]

If a∉L, every summand in H_L(a) is zero. If a∈L and L is distinct, its unique matching position contributes one and every other position contributes zero, so H_L(a)=1. For f_O(s)=a, expanding each fibre filter at the head gives

\[
 K(s::S,O,L)=H_L(a)+K(S,O,L).
\]

Total observation coverage supplies such an a∈L for each source. Induction over S, starting from K([],O,L)=0, therefore yields K(S,O,L)=|S|. This counts every source occurrence exactly once without assuming equal fibre sizes.

The raw exact-sum helper begins with (n,d)=(0,1) and, for each rational p/q, updates

\[
 (n,d)\longmapsto(nq+pd,dq).
\]

Here n,p are integers and d,q are natural denominators; zero and negative input fractions remain admitted. Cross-multiplication gives (nq+pd)/(dq)=n/d+p/q, so induction represents the exact rational sum. The executable whole test is false on an empty input list; on a nonempty list it tests n=d after all updates. Generated positive reduced parts enter this same helper through their exact numerator and denominator, without changing its broader raw domain.


121. Exact-whole closure and microstate succession

Every generated probability ledger and deterministic pushforward ledger reconstructs the exact whole. The proof tracks the numerator and denominator of the original exact sum through each retained class fraction. Its invariant relates the accumulated numerator to the count of represented microstates over the fixed support count. Reduced class fractions satisfy this relation by their native count equations. Complete class coverage gives the full support count, and cancellation of that positive count establishes equality of the accumulated numerator and denominator. Thus the executable exact-whole test returns true for every admitted ledger.

The singleton base retains its sole classical state with complete provenance, one observation class and one exact whole part. Extending admitted support by one fresh microstate preserves admission. Its assigned class gains exactly that state in source order, every other class retains its members, and each reduced fraction satisfies the updated membership-to-support ratio. The successor ledger again reconstructs the exact whole. These statements apply at arbitrary finite depth and preserve deterministic membership throughout. Full candidate enumeration and complete program correspondence are verified below.


For generated rows, let N>0 be the support size, m_i each row's microstate count and a_i/b_i its reduced part. The native row equation is a_iN=m_i b_i. At an intermediate sum with numerator n, denominator d and represented count u, use the invariant nN=ud. Adding one row gives

\[
 (nb_i+a_id)N
 =(nN)b_i+(a_iN)d
 =u d b_i+m_i b_i d
 =(u+m_i)(db_i).
\]

Thus the same invariant holds after each exact update. Starting from (0,1,0), induction over all rows gives n_final N=(Σ_i m_i)d_final. The class-count theorem supplies Σ_i m_i=N; cancellation of positive N yields n_final=d_final. The rows cannot be empty because their member count is N>0. Hence the executable exact-whole predicate returns true.

The natural-number accumulator for these positive rows agrees with the signed raw helper after embedding its numerator into the integers: their recurrences have identical multiplication and addition updates. Probability and pushforward row maps preserve the part list exactly. Consequently the preceding argument proves exact-whole closure for both generated ledger types, not only for unreduced count fractions.

For fresh x∉S with assigned label v, validation remains admitted after S'=S++[x], O'=O++[(x,v)]. Old lookup values persist, and the assigned fibre gains x:

\[
 F'_a=F_a\mathbin{++}\begin{cases}[x],&a=v,\\\lbrack\rbrack,&a\ne v.\end{cases}
\]

Every successor row with part a'_i/b'_i therefore satisfies

\[
 (|F_i|+\mathbf1_{i=v})b'_i=a'_i(N+1).
\]

Applying the universal closure result to the newly admitted ledger proves that it again reconstructs the whole. At the singleton base S=[s], O=[(s,a)], the state is (s,[s]), the projected ledger is [(a,[s])], and its part is 1/1. The same base and successor construction preserves deterministic-member provenance at every finite stage.


122. Classical and probabilistic operational witnesses

All six original operational witnesses pass native proof: held-state provenance, two equal half classes, unequal three-quarter and one-quarter parts, singleton versus unresolved-class resolution, deterministic prefix pushforward reconstructing the whole, and exposed deterministic-member traces. Complete 1,024-candidate program correspondence is verified below.


For the original witness support S=[aa,ab,ba,bb], requesting held state aa returns (aa,S). Prefix observation gives the ordered classes F_a=[aa,ab] and F_b=[ba,bb], so both reduced fractions are 2/4=1/2. The unequal observation instead gives [aa,ab,ba] and [bb], with parts 3/4 and 1/4. These compute the held-state, half-class and unequal-class witnesses directly.

Fine observation labels each microstate by itself, so the aa row has members [aa] and resolves to classicalState(aa). Under prefix observation, label a has members [aa,ab] and returns that exact unresolved class. Prefix pushforward retains the same two predecessor lists and its exact sum is

\[
 (0,1)\xrightarrow{1/2}(1,2)\xrightarrow{1/2}(4,4),
\]

so the whole test succeeds. The unequal example similarly gives (0,1)→(3,4)→(16,16). Finally, realization returns [(aa,deterministic-member),(ab,deterministic-member)] in the first prefix row and the corresponding ba,bb pairs in the second. Every emitted member has the required tag while its source label, row label and fraction are preserved. These are the six complete operational witness evaluations.


123. Complete classical and probabilistic correspondence

All 1,024 original candidates and six operational witnesses are retained. Empirical frequencies retain their separately registered blind-data boundary.


The grammar enumerates ten binary choices: support, classical state, observation, parts, meaning, resolution, transformation, normalization, generality and addition. Each axis retains its rejected and admitted alternatives in source order, so

\[
 |\mathcal G|=2^{10}=1{,}024.
\]

Membership in each two-element axis list constructs membership in the complete nested product. Constructor case analysis proves that the source identifier decision equals the conjunction of all ten admitted-field decisions; their conjunction identifies exactly the all-admitted candidate:

\[
 \operatorname{accept}(c)\iff\bigwedge_{i=1}^{10}c_i=\mathrm{admitted}
 \iff c=(\mathrm{admitted},\ldots,\mathrm{admitted}).
\]

Each rejected field contradicts the corresponding projection of this equality. The full candidate classification accompanies the ledger, resolution, realization, exact-whole and arbitrary finite successor derivations in this block.


124. Quantum-information branch support and provenance

The support correspondence generates every held-label word across a nonempty canonical position trace. Its original validator requires at least two distinct labels and distinct positions. Native correspondence with the information-quantity construction preserves the full Cartesian order and proves exhaustive word membership and duplicate freedom. Empty string labels remain allowed.

The superposition-equivalent record retains the complete branch family and pairs each word with its full position-label trace. Projecting these records recovers the original branch family in order; projecting each trace recovers both its original word and every canonical position. The amplitude field retains the original not-admitted-at-support-boundary marker. 3,200 independent comparisons cover full support/provenance outputs and original errors. Information-unit traces, basis records, joint support and observation/reconstruction are established below; the canonical basis-name correspondence is established below.


Write L for the ordered label list and P for the ordered position list, with n=|P|. Admission is precisely |L|≥2, Distinct(L), n>0 and Distinct(P). The recursive complete support is

\[
 W_0(L)=[[]],\qquad W_{n+1}(L)=\mathop{\mathrm{concat}}_{\ell\in L}[\ell::w:w\in W_n(L)].
\]

Induction on n proves w∈W_n(L) if and only if |w|=n and every label of w belongs to L: the successor case removes the first label and applies the induction hypothesis to the tail. Distinct labels and distinct tails make each block duplicate-free, and different first labels separate different blocks. This establishes duplicate freedom of the complete support.

For each admitted w, define T_P(w)=zip(P,w). The returned branch record is (W_n,[(w,T_P(w)):w∈W_n],not-admitted-at-support-boundary). Equal lengths permit simultaneous induction on P and w, giving

\[
 \pi_1(T_P(w))=P,\qquad\pi_2(T_P(w))=w.
\]

Projecting the word field of the outer record likewise returns W_n in its original order. Thus the complete alternative family and each branch's full origin are retained by the construction itself.


125. Basis records and joint support

The basis correspondence retains each original word together with its source colon-delimited basis name. Projecting the retained words reconstructs complete input support exactly, and distinct words yield distinct word/name records. The name field alone is not injective over the original unrestricted string-label domain: labels a and a:a at two canonical positions generate the distinct words (a,a:a) and (a:a,a), both named basis:a:a:a. The accepted support, distinct words and identical names are established by native proofs. Consequently, retained-record correspondence and name-only uniqueness are separate propositions; the latter does not hold over the complete executable domain.

Joint support requires two nonempty duplicate-free word carriers and enumerates every left/right pair in source order, retaining both coordinates and their concatenation. Native membership equivalence establishes complete pair coverage; coordinate recovery preserves both source words even when concatenated values coincide. 993 independent output comparisons cover exact basis and joint records, errors, empty words and delimiter-containing labels.


For a word w=[w_1,…,w_n], the executable name is B(w)=“basis:”++intercalate(“:”,w), and the basis record list is [(w,B(w)):w∈S]. Projection onto its first component gives S. Equality of two records immediately gives equality of their words, establishing injectivity of the retained-record map without any condition on delimiters inside labels. The raw example satisfies

\[
 B([\text{a},\text{a:a}])=\text{basis:a:a:a}=B([\text{a:a},\text{a}]),
\]

while the two first coordinates differ. The fixed inherited Fold labels used in the registered derivation have the separate name-injectivity proof below.

For admitted left and right carriers A and C, the joint construction is

\[
 J(A,C)=\mathop{\mathrm{concat}}_{a\in A}[(a,c,a\mathbin{++}c):c\in C].
\]

Expanding the nested list maps proves (a,c,z)∈J(A,C) exactly when a∈A, c∈C and z=a++c. Equality of two emitted triples forces equality of both first and second coordinates by projection. Hence the joint correspondence retains the original pair even if concatenation alone would erase its split.


126. Independently realized information-unit traces

Each canonical position retains a four-field record: its position name, the one-Fold-information-distinction tag, the all-first-label baseline word and the word differing only at that position by the second held label. Native proofs place both words in the full generated support, identify the baseline with repeated first labels and establish distinctness of the alternatives for distinct labels. The admitted trace has exactly one row per position and preserves the entire position order at arbitrary finite depth. 1,600 independent complete-record and original-error comparisons pass, including empty strings and delimiter-containing labels.


Let a and b be the first two distinct admitted labels. For zero-based position i<n, the baseline and its independently changed alternative are

\[
 u_i=a^i\mathbin{++}[a]\mathbin{++}a^{n-i-1}=a^n,\qquad
 v_i=a^i\mathbin{++}[b]\mathbin{++}a^{n-i-1}.
\]

Here a^k means the list of k copies of a. Both words have length n and every component belongs to L, so complete-support membership puts both in W_n(L). If u_i=v_i, cancelling the common prefix of i copies gives equal heads a=b, contradicting distinctness. The four-field row is (P_i,one-Fold-information-distinction,u_i,v_i).

The recursive implementation emits the head-position row and increments i before processing the remaining positions. Its invariant is i+|remaining positions|=n. Induction preserves this invariant, proves membership of both words in every row, and gives exactly n rows whose position projection is P. Thus the construction establishes an independent distinction at every position for arbitrary finite depth.


127. Exact support observation and reconstruction

Observation retains every branch with the selected image in source order, together with its complete complement and a record of every original branch and image. An empty complement retains the original empty-One marker. Native membership proofs establish disjoint and exhaustive accounting of retained and closed alternatives. Projecting the complete reconstruction record recovers the original support in order. On canonical nonempty support, reconstruction accepts this record and returns exactly the pre-observation support.

The raw observation helper preserves its original domain, including repeated support words when the observation keys cover that support set exactly. Reconstruction separately rejects repeated branch keys, as in the source. 4,660 independent comparisons cover full observation records, reconstruction outputs and original errors, including empty words, empty labels, repeated support, reversed mapping order and literal empty-One branch labels.


Let O have distinct word keys whose set equals the support set, and write f_O(w) for lookup of the image of w. For selected image y the emitted lists are

\[
 R_y=[w\in S:f_O(w)=y],\qquad C_y=[w\in S:w\notin R_y],\qquad
 E=[(w,f_O(w)):w\in S].
\]

Observation rejects an empty R_y. Otherwise it returns y, R_y, the presented C_y (using the empty-One marker when empty), and E. Filter membership gives w∈R_y iff w∈S and f_O(w)=y; the complementary filter gives w∈C_y iff w∈S and w∉R_y. These equivalences prove disjointness and exhaustive accounting of S while preserving each filtered order.

List induction gives π₁(E)=S. Reconstruction first requires E nonempty and Distinct(π₁(E)), then returns π₁(E). Consequently canonical nonempty duplicate-free S is recovered exactly. The raw observation guard checks distinct observation keys and coverage in both directions; it does not require distinct S. Repeated support occurrences therefore remain present in E, and the separate reconstruction guard rejects them exactly as specified by the source.


128. Canonical position succession

Adding one fresh canonical position preserves support admission and extends every prior branch by every held label in the original Cartesian order. The extended support remains duplicate-free. Each branch provenance is exactly its previous position-label trace followed by the fresh position and chosen label; a complete information-unit trace exists for the extended support. The first-position construction retains all held labels, with the original two-label instance producing its two branches. All six original operational witnesses pass native proof on their fixed source inputs. Name-only bijection holds throughout the registered fixed-label Fold support, as established below.


For fresh p∉P, admission persists because P++[p] is nonempty and duplicate-free. Define the ordered extension E_L(S)=concat_{w∈S}[w++[ℓ]:ℓ∈L]. Distribution over concatenated blocks, followed by induction on depth, proves the ordered identity

\[
 W_{n+1}(L)=E_L(W_n(L)).
\]

The base is W_1(L)=[[ℓ]:ℓ∈L]. In the successor proof, each existing leading-label block is extended internally, preserving both its leading label and the source's order. For |w|=|P|, simultaneous list induction also gives

\[
 \operatorname{zip}(P\mathbin{++}[p],w\mathbin{++}[\ell])
 =\operatorname{zip}(P,w)\mathbin{++}[(p,\ell)].
\]

The support-admission theorem then supplies duplicate freedom of the extended support, and the information-unit construction supplies its full position trace. At the original two-label base, P=[p1] produces [[held-a],[held-b]]. These base and successor results establish the construction at each finite canonical depth.


129. Quantum support program correspondence

Fresh native execution reproduces all 1,024 original candidate lookups and all registration, census, closure, control, hash and unknown-identifier outputs. The registered Fold Assembly generator fixes its labels to held-a and held-b, and the quantum grammar refers to the two held Fold labels. Native proofs establish name-only injectivity and the unique inverse on this generated domain at every finite depth; the arbitrary-label collision is outside that registered grammar and remains faithfully represented in the raw helper behavior.


The complete grammar has ten binary axes: unit, support, basis, provenance, superposition, composition, observation, record, generality and addition. Each is enumerated in rejected/admitted order. The nested product therefore contains 2¹⁰=1,024 candidates, and membership of a constructor in each binary axis proves membership of every candidate in this product. Exhaustive constructor analysis establishes

\[
 \operatorname{survives}(c)=\operatorname{criteriaDecision}(c),\qquad
 \operatorname{survives}(c)=\mathrm{true}\iff\bigwedge_{i=1}^{10}(c_i=\mathrm{admitted}).
\]

The conjunction fixes the unique all-admitted survivor. Its support, basis and provenance fields refer to the complete inherited Fold construction proved in the surrounding sections.

130. Canonical Fold basis bijection

The registered Fold Assembly generator supplies precisely held-a and held-b. Quantum support with these inherited labels contains exactly the words in Fold Assembly at the same finite depth. The original colon-joined basis names uniquely identify these words: the native proof establishes exact character decoding, cancels the basis prefix and recovers the held-label sequence. Hence all emitted names are distinct and every emitted name has one and only one source word. This result holds at arbitrary finite depth and preserves the original executable functions on their broader raw input domains. Together with the unit, provenance, pair-support, observation, reconstruction and succession proofs and the complete 1,024-candidate program correspondence, it completes the registered quantum-information support entry.


Put H=[held-a,held-b]. Complete-support membership and the inherited Fold generator give the same condition,

\[
 w\in W_n(H)\iff |w|=n\ \land\ (\forall x\in w,\ x=\text{held-a}\ \lor\ x=\text{held-b}).
\]

To prove name injectivity, decode the name's bytes into characters. Each character of the fixed prefix, delimiter and held labels belongs to the explicit finite alphabet {h,e,l,d,-,a,b,:,s,i}; character-list induction proves exact decoding on that alphabet. Equality B(u)=B(v) therefore yields equality of their character lists. Cancelling the fixed “basis:” prefix leaves equality of the colon-joined label sequences.

For nonempty sequences, prefixing a colon produces blocks “:held-a” or “:held-b”, each of length seven. Their seventh characters distinguish the alternatives. Equality forces the same first label; cancelling the common seven-character block reduces to equality of the remaining blocks. Induction recovers the entire label sequence. The empty case is separated by the presence or absence of a first block. Hence

\[
 u,v\in W_n(H),\ B(u)=B(v)\ \Longrightarrow\ u=v.
\]

For any emitted name z, membership in the mapped name list supplies a word w with B(w)=z. The preceding implication proves that any other such source word equals w. Thus every emitted name has exactly one preimage, and the complete basis-name list is duplicate-free at every admitted finite depth.


131. Exact generated transition systems

A transition system retains the ordered generated state carrier, held action labels and source/action/target rows. Construction first checks state uniqueness, then action uniqueness and nonempty carriers; it then checks each row in order for carrier membership and repeated source/action coordinates. The relation may be partial. A requested step succeeds exactly when its matching target list is a singleton, preserving the original missing-transition boundary.

Execution first checks that the initial state belongs to the carrier, then retains that initial state and every successive generated target, propagating the first failed step. Native proofs identify successful execution exactly with an inductive chain of the original step results. Every admitted trace begins at its registered initial state, has one position for every action plus the initial position, and remains entirely within the state carrier. Constructor admission is equivalent to the exact original carrier and ordered-row conditions. 3,397 independent comparisons cover complete outputs and original errors, including duplicate states/actions, empty strings, missing transitions, repeated coordinates, escaped rows and failure order.


Write a system as (S,A,R), where each retained row is (s,a,t). Row admission requires s,t∈S and a∈A. Scanning the relation maintains the list K of previously seen (source,action) coordinates: an empty remainder succeeds; a head row succeeds only if it is carrier-bound and (s,a)∉K, after which validation continues with (s,a)::K. Together with distinct, nonempty S and A, this is exactly constructor admission, including its ordered failure behavior.

Define T_R(s,a)=[t:(s,a,t) occurs in R]. The executable step satisfies

\[
 \operatorname{step}(s,a)=\operatorname{ok}(t)\iff T_R(s,a)=[t].
\]

The retained execution relation has the two clauses

\[
 E(s,[],[s]),\qquad
 \frac{\operatorname{step}(s,a)=\operatorname{ok}(t)\quad E(t,u,\tau)}
 {E(s,a::u,s::\tau)}.
\]

Induction on the requested action list proves runFrom(s,u)=ok(τ) iff E(s,u,τ), by matching each successful step and recursive result; either error branch propagates unchanged. The outer run additionally requires s∈S. Induction on E gives head(τ)=s and |τ|=|u|+1. Each successful step selects a retained row, whose validation gives its target in S. Starting with s∈S, the same induction therefore puts every trace state in S.


132. Retained execution composition and extension

Every successful execution admits an exact decomposition into its retained predecessor positions and terminal state. Two such paths with a common terminal/initial interface compose by concatenating their action traces and predecessor records, retaining the shared state exactly once. An empty action trace is the identity path; appending one generated action retains the complete previous trace and appends its generated target. These statements hold for arbitrary finite executions.

For every admitted system, a requested transition succeeds if and only if its exact source/action/target row belongs to the retained relation. Original constructor validation excludes any second target row at the same coordinate. Consequently, an admitted extension that retains the earlier state carrier and transition rows preserves every earlier successful execution with its exact trace. This applies to appended carriers and rows and to both components embedded in a common admitted assembly. Compatibility is decided by the original constructor; no additional transition or scheduling rule is imposed.


Make the terminal interface explicit by writing P(s,u,b,t) for a retained path with actions u, predecessor list b and terminal t. Its identity clause is P(s,[],[],s); its successor clause prepends the current state and action after an exact successful step. Induction in both directions establishes

\[
 E(s,u,\tau)\iff\exists b,t,\ \tau=b\mathbin{++}[t]\ \land\ P(s,u,b,t).
\]

If P(s,u,b,m) and P(m,v,c,t), induction on the first path gives

\[
 P(s,u\mathbin{++}v,b\mathbin{++}c,t),\qquad
 \operatorname{runFrom}(s,u\mathbin{++}v)=\operatorname{ok}((b\mathbin{++}c)\mathbin{++}[t]).
\]

The identity case returns the second path. The successor case retains the first edge and applies the induction hypothesis to its remainder. Taking the second path to be the single edge m→t yields the old complete trace b++[m] followed by [t], with no repeated interface inserted.

For an admitted relation, the seen-coordinate invariant excludes any later row at a previously encountered coordinate. Induction through validation therefore gives T_R(s,a)=[t] whenever (s,a,t)∈R. Together with step membership, this proves step(s,a)=ok(t) iff (s,a,t)∈R. If an admitted extension retains S and every row of R, each old step still succeeds with the same target. Induction on the original execution preserves its exact trace, and retained initial-state membership preserves the outer run. Appended carriers and common admitted assemblies are direct instances of these two inclusions.


133. Generated words and exact coordinate rewrite

The generated-word operation retains the original empty-One marker and then every complete word layer up to the given finite position depth, preserving both layer order and Cartesian order. Native induction identifies the exact frontier at every depth and proves that one new position appends the entire next layer. The original unique-alphabet, unique-position and nonempty-alphabet conditions remain unchanged; the literal empty-One symbol remains allowed.

Exact rewriting validates the unique position trace, equal word/position lengths and selected held position. The output retains the full position trace, changes the selected coordinate to its replacement and preserves every other coordinate and total length. 2,952 independent full-output/error comparisons and all three original formal-computation witnesses pass. Fresh native execution matches every original 256-candidate lookup, registration, census, closure, control and digest field. These results complete Exact State and Transition Law on its registered generated-finite boundary; no language, machine model or resource bound is introduced.


Let F_n be the current word frontier and G_n the retained cumulative output. The source recurrence is

\[
 F_0=[[]],\quad G_0=[[\text{empty-One}]],\qquad
 F_{n+1}=\mathop{\mathrm{concat}}_{w\in F_n}[w\mathbin{++}[a]:a\in A],\quad
 G_{n+1}=G_n\mathbin{++}F_{n+1}.
\]

The ordered word-extension identity proves F_n=W_n(A). Substitution into the second recurrence gives G_n=[[empty-One]]++W_1(A)++⋯++W_n(A), retaining every layer and its order. Admission requires distinct A and P and nonempty A; the depth n=|P| may be zero. Membership is exactly the marker word or a word of positive length at most n whose symbols all belong to A. The host empty frontier [] and the one-symbol marker word [empty-One] remain different objects.

For an admitted rewrite, P is distinct, |w|=|P| and the selected position h lies in P. The output is the coordinatewise map

\[
 R_{h,r}(w)_i=\begin{cases}r,&P_i=h,\\w_i,&P_i\ne h.\end{cases}
\]

Simultaneous induction over P and w proves

\[
 \operatorname{zip}(P,R_{h,r}(w))=[(p,\text{if }p=h\text{ then }r\text{ else }x):(p,x)\in\operatorname{zip}(P,w)].
\]

The empty case is immediate; each successor retains its position and emits exactly the selected replacement or original symbol before the inductive tail. The same induction gives |R_{h,r}(w)|=|w|. Thus the displayed trace proves the selected-coordinate change and every unchanged coordinate together.


134. Finite language derivation certificates

The language formation rules retain the empty construction frontier and append a held alphabet symbol at each finite successor. Native derivation trees are sound and complete for the exact generated word layer: a word belongs to that layer if and only if it has the corresponding finite derivation certificate. The certificate fixes its depth, admits only symbols in the generated alphabet and retains a recoverable predecessor at each successor. Concatenating two derivations produces the concatenated word at the summed construction depth.

For the complete cumulative language output, membership is exactly the original empty-One marker or a positive-depth derivation bounded by the registered position trace. The marker and the empty host frontier remain distinct, and literal marker-valued alphabet symbols retain their original behavior. The shared native operation proofs and all three claim-specific witnesses combine with full 256-candidate program/output/hash agreement to complete the registered Fold Formal-Language and Grammar Law. No completed infinite language or conventional grammar model is introduced.


The native finite certificate D_A(n,w) has precisely the source's seed and append clauses:

\[
 D_A(0,[]),\qquad
 \frac{D_A(n,w)\quad a\in A}{D_A(n+1,w\mathbin{++}[a])}.
\]

Soundness follows by induction on the certificate: the seed belongs to W_0(A), and ordered extension inserts each appended held symbol into W_{n+1}(A). Conversely, membership in W_{n+1}(A) decomposes into a predecessor w∈W_n(A) and a∈A with result w++[a]. Induction on n constructs the corresponding append certificate. Hence

\[
 w\in W_n(A)\iff D_A(n,w),\qquad
 D_A(n,w)\Longrightarrow |w|=n\ \land\ \forall a\in w,\ a\in A.
\]

Every successor certificate therefore has a recoverable predecessor and final held symbol. Substituting this equivalence into cumulative-output membership gives w∈G_N iff w=[empty-One] or there exists 0<n≤N with D_A(n,w). This disjunction preserves literal marker-valued symbols in ordinary generated words as well as the distinguished initial marker.

For D_A(m,u) and D_A(n,v), induction on the second certificate proves D_A(m+n,u++v). The seed uses u++[]=u. Its append case applies one append to the induction hypothesis and reassociates (u++v)++[a]=u++(v++[a]). Thus concatenation retains the complete finite construction certificate at the summed depth.


135. Finite recognition with retained accepting classes

The finite-recognition kernel retains its generated states, input alphabet, original transition relation, initial state, accepting class and complete run. Its accepting-class statement is represented by a native predicate requiring the original successful execution and a terminal state held in that class. This predicate is proved equivalent to the retained-path representation; it does not introduce a new executable source method.

Accepted traces preserve the initial state, full trace length and admitted input alphabet. The identity trace accepts exactly when its state belongs to the accepting class; an empty class admits no accepted trace. Matching paths compose with every trace position retained, and an admitted carrier/transition extension preserves earlier accepted traces. A one-symbol successor retains its prior trace and uses the original generated target. All results hold at arbitrary finite depth. Full 256-candidate program, witness, control and hash correspondence completes the registered Fold Finite Automaton Law without an equivalence-to-all-models or universality assertion.


For accepting class F, the retained acceptance statement is

\[
 \operatorname{Accepted}(s,u,F,\tau)\iff s\in S\ \land\
 \exists b,t,\ \tau=b\mathbin{++}[t]\ \land\ P(s,u,b,t)\ \land\ t\in F.
\]

The terminal-state recursion returns t on b++[t], by induction on b. Applying the execution/path correspondence in each direction therefore proves

\[
 \operatorname{Accepted}(s,u,F,\tau)\iff
 \operatorname{run}(s,u)=\operatorname{ok}(\tau)\ \land\
 \exists t,\ \operatorname{terminalState}(\tau)=\operatorname{some}(t)\ \land\ t\in F.
\]

This immediately gives rejection for F=[] and, for s∈S, identity acceptance iff s∈F. The execution theorem gives head(τ)=s and |τ|=|u|+1. In an admitted system every edge has its action in A; induction over the path places every requested symbol in A. If F is held in S, the retained accepting terminal also lies in S.

Composition uses the path equation of Section 132 and membership of its final t in F. For a one-symbol extension, a successful step from the previous terminal m to t∈F gives the accepted trace (b++[m])++[t] on input u++[a]. Finally, an admitted carrier/row extension preserves the exact run and leaves its terminal and accepting membership unchanged; the displayed equivalence transfers acceptance directly. These deductions retain all intermediate states at arbitrary finite depth.


136. Retained held-position rewrite traces

The exact coordinate rewrite retains its original position validation and output behavior. Replacing the same held position twice has precisely the effect of the second replacement; repeating an identical replacement is idempotent. Replacements at distinct held positions commute. Native proofs establish these equations for arbitrary finite words with the original matching position trace. An admitted rewrite permits another replacement at the same position without changing its admission conditions.

Retained step records associate rule identity, source word, held replacement position, replacement symbol and successor word. Replaying a record uses the unchanged exact rewrite operation. Each valid record preserves word length and carries the complete selected/unchanged coordinate correspondence. Finite chains compose at their exact successor/source interface, preserve length and retain a replay proof for every recorded step. These typed records express the declared provenance fields; they do not add an executable operation to V3.


For a fixed matching position trace P, write R_{h,a}(w) for the coordinate rewrite. At each coordinate p the overwrite equation reduces to

\[
 \text{if }p=h\text{ then }b\text{ else }(\text{if }p=h\text{ then }a\text{ else }x)
 =\text{if }p=h\text{ then }b\text{ else }x.
\]

Induction on the paired position/word lists therefore gives R_{h,b}(R_{h,a}(w))=R_{h,b}(w). Setting b=a proves idempotence. For h≠k, the three cases p=h, p=k and neither give the same value in either order, proving

\[
 R_{k,b}(R_{h,a}(w))=R_{h,a}(R_{k,b}(w)).
\]

Length preservation leaves the rewrite admission conditions unchanged after the first replacement: P remains distinct, the word still has length |P|, and h is still held in P. Thus overwrite also holds for the admitted executable results.

A record r=(ruleIdentity,h,a,w,v) replays precisely when exactRewrite(w,h,P,a)=ok(v). Its coordinate theorem gives the entire retained map from (p,x) to (p,if p=h then a else x), and |v|=|w|. Define T(w,[],w) as the identity trace; prepend r to T(v,rs,t) only when r replays from w to v. Induction over this trace gives |t|=|w| and replay validity for every member of the record list. Induction over a first trace ending at m, with a second beginning at m, proves T(w,rs++ss,t). Every recorded rewrite and its rule identity remain in that concatenation.


137. Registered rewrite boundaries and trace classes

The finite relation of registered rewrite applications retains complete records, including rule identity, source word, selected position, replacement and successor. Admitted applications belong to this retained relation, have both endpoints in the generated carrier and replay the original exact rewrite operation. Every admitted application has an exact source-symbol occurrence at its held position, a source-word decomposition around that occurrence and the replacement at that same coordinate. Missing registrations and endpoints outside the generated carrier are excluded by the declared boundary; the raw executable helper keeps its original guards.

A terminal word has no admitted outgoing application. Consequently its retained traces are empty and it cannot begin a cyclic or branching trace. A cyclic trace retains a nonempty return path, which can be repeated by exact composition. A branching trace retains two admitted applications from the same word with distinct successors; each yields its own complete one-step trace. Identity, serial composition and one-record succession preserve the retained relation. Extending the registered application relation and generated carrier preserves all previous traces. These predicates formalise the declared finite trace classes; no claim of universal termination or confluence is introduced. Full 256-candidate native program agreement and the original three operational witnesses complete the registered Rewriting entry.

\[
 w=u\mathbin{++}(x::v),\qquad (h,a)\in\operatorname{zip}(P,\operatorname{successor}(r)).
\]

Here a is the record's replacement. The coordinate theorem supplies the second statement at the same held position. Failure of record registration or either carrier membership directly contradicts the corresponding conjunct of A(r).

This excludes both a nonempty return cycle and the two outgoing records required for branching.

Let f(v,p) be the supplied exact step on list-valued v, and let V(b,P) retain all values. Its defining equations are

\[

The same recursion gives head(V)=b and |V(b,P)|=|P|+1.

Let t(b,P) be the recursively computed terminal and B(b,P) the predecessor list, omitting that terminal. Induction gives

\[
 V(b,P)=B(b,P)\mathbin{++}[t(b,P)],
\]

\[
 t(b,L\mathbin{++}R)=t(t(b,L),R),\qquad
 V(b,L\mathbin{++}R)=B(b,L)\mathbin{++}V(t(b,L),R).
\]

The empty-left case is immediate. In the successor case, retain b, replace the recursive base by f(b,p), and apply the induction hypothesis to the remaining left positions. Taking R=[p] yields

\[

\]

The executable composition and successor equations require precisely the original distinctness check on the combined position trace. For every pair (p,v) in zip(P,B(b,P)), induction also places f(v,p) in V(b,P): the head pair yields the next base, and each tail pair uses the recursive proof. This retains the exact step result for every successor, including empty list-valued bases.

Encoding alone does not introduce an answer-preservation premise.

Composition retains the intermediate input and both input translations. The final answer decodes through the second reduction and then the first, retaining both intermediate and source answers. Native proofs establish both directions of this correspondence and preserve both generated-domain witnesses. The identity law preserves both the exact input and the decoded answer; finite successor laws retain the original table-validation boundary.

All 256 original candidates and all complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3. These results complete the registered Fold Computability-Reduction Law.


Let I(x,y) and D(b,a) be the supplied input and answer translations, each defined by finite encoding lookup followed by exact decoding. Totality on source S and target T means ∀x∈S, ∃y∈T, I(x,y). The registered answer correspondence is the explicit equivalence

\[
 A_S(x,a)\iff\exists b,\ A_T(y,b)\land D(b,a)\qquad(x\in S,\ I(x,y)).
\]

Given A_S(x,a), totality supplies y and the forward implication supplies b. A target procedure answer retains a selected request and halt certificate, so replay gives its full trace before++[b] and normal terminal b. Expanding D(b,a) retains the actual code word, encoding lookup and answer decoder result. Conversely, a target answer and its decoding yield A_S(x,a) by the reverse implication.

For successive reductions with intermediate answer a₁ and final answer a₂, substitute the second correspondence into the first:

\[
 A_S(x,a)\iff\exists a_2,a_1,\ A_T(z,a_2)\land D_2(a_2,a_1)\land D_1(a_1,a).
\]

Totality supplies and retains both intermediate input y and final input z in their respective domains. The two reverse implications prove the reverse direction in the opposite order. With the same validated table and its exact inverse, translation uniqueness forces output=input; applying this to both input and held answer proves identity preservation. The domain successor law separates the new head input's translated witness from totality on the old tail, by the two cases of list membership.


152. Finite relative computability classes

Registered problems and their certified reductions form an original generated finite graph. Its exact reachability operation supplies complete retained paths. Every reduction step on such a path retains its finite encoders, decoders and bidirectional correctness certificate. Native path composition supplies transitive closure without replacing the intermediate interfaces.

Mutual reachability is reflexive on registered problems, symmetric and transitive. A degree class retains exactly the mutually reachable members of the original ordered problem carrier. Each representative belongs to its class; mutually reducible representatives give the same members, and overlapping classes imply mutual reducibility. These statements require no quotient axiom.

Strict one-way reachability is transitive and excludes mutual equivalence. A finite incomparability certificate excludes complete paths in both directions. Strict order and incomparability are independent of the chosen equivalent representatives. The singleton One supplies the base class, and retained nodes and edges preserve every prior mutual reachability witness. Degrees remain finite generated relation classes, with no completed transfinite hierarchy.

All 256 original candidates and every complete native program field and hash agree with pinned V3. These results complete the registered Fold Degrees-of-Computability Law.


Write x↝y for true reachability in the admitted finite problem graph. Define x∼y iff x↝y and y↝x, and retain the ordered class [y∈nodes:x∼y]. Filter membership proves that this list contains exactly the held mutually reachable nodes. Identity paths give x∼x, swapping the two paths gives symmetry, and path composition in both directions gives transitivity. Hence x∼x′ transports class membership in both directions. If two classes share z, composing x↝z↝y and y↝z↝x proves x∼y.

Strict reachability is x≺y iff both nodes are held, x↝y and not y↝x. If x≺y and y≺z, composition gives x↝z. A reverse z↝x would compose with y↝z to contradict not y↝x. Thus strict reachability is transitive and excludes mutuality. Incomparability requires both reachability tests false; any complete path in either direction would force its test true.

For equivalent replacements x∼x′ and y∼y′, transport is the path composition

\[
 x'\rightsquigarrow x\rightsquigarrow y\rightsquigarrow y'.
\]

Applying the same construction in reverse proves transport of absence by contradiction. Therefore strictness and incomparability preserve equivalent representatives. Retaining graph nodes and edges retains each original path and thus every mutual witness; the singleton graph [One] with no edges has class [One]. Finally, complete reachability reconstruction gives an ordered path whose every adjacent pair is a registered edge. The supplied edge certificate then recovers each reduction's total input map and bidirectional answer map along that path.

\[
 I(w)=\operatorname{some}(o)\iff

\]

Both directions follow by expanding interpretation and using the successful decoder and registry lookup. Conversely, a successful interpreted trace reconstructs its path; supplying the stated normal-terminal boundary completes the halt certificate. A decision retains t=accept or t=reject. 154. Generated diagonal contradiction

The registered undecidability construction applies on languages supporting exact self-encoding and complementation. Correctness would equate the proposed answer with that observed complementary answer, contradicting the proved absence of a complement fixed point. Thus a total correct internal decider is excluded on the explicitly registered self-encoding/complement domain.

\[
 q_v=(M_v,c(v),[]),\qquad

Correctness for this same code requires v=c(v), contradicting the complement's no-fixed-point theorem. This instantiation proves exclusion of a total correct internal decider on the stated self-supported domain. 155. Computational proof-system boundary

For initial proposition list A and proof steps L, the checker accepts the empty trace. \[
 \forall p\in F(A,L),\quad \operatorname{denied}(p)\notin F(A,L).
\]

Thus a retained proposition and its denied form directly contradict a checked record. Identity requires precisely consistency of A. Induction on a first accepted trace proves serial acceptance at the actual intermediate list, while induction on its step list gives

\[
 F(A,L\mathbin{++}R)=F(F(A,L),R).
\]

The second checked record's consistency therefore proves consistency of the composite final state. Finite positional reconstruction supplies each proof's exact numbered name; successful registry lookup supplies its original step list.

For an answer-correct predictor on the retained self-supported domain, choose the specific decoded self-code from SelfSupport. Hence that code admits no held answer, and totality would contradict this explicit missing decision. Over a nonempty finite family, induction compares the head cost with an attained maximum of the tail, selects an actual member, and proves every cost is bounded by the selected cost. Instantiating verification at that member yields a verified upper witness.

For a lower witness, let x,y be held inputs with observe(x)=observe(y) but required(x)≠required(y). Any correct response based on that observation would satisfy

\[
 required(x)=respond(observe(x))=respond(observe(y))=required(y),
\]

a contradiction. A lower certificate supplies such a pair for each declared model whose measured cost is below B. Instantiation at any allegedly correct below-bound model excludes it, proving correctness implies not(cost<B) within that comparison family. Appending inputs preserves both held witnesses; composing comparison families splits model membership into the original two cases and retains the corresponding witness. The singleton upper bound is reflexive.


166. Resource-preserving reductions and completeness

A verified reduction retains its complete source domain, total encoded input translation and bidirectional answer decoder. Every source input has a registered executable overhead record satisfying the frozen resource law. Native reconstruction recovers its original observation history, occupied support and exact counts. Across stages, every trace is retained and the sum of verified observation costs equals the length of the full concatenated trace.

Reduction chains preserve each original mapping, intermediate description and overhead witness under the same resource law. Hardness supplies a retained chain from every member of the frozen problem inventory; completeness additionally requires membership of the target. Composition transports hardness while retaining the full chain. Inventory extension requires the new certificate and preserves all prior certificates; the identity chain supplies the singleton base. Completeness remains relative to the frozen problem class and resource law.

All 256 original candidates, resource witnesses and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Reduction and Completeness Law.


VerifiedReduction conjoins total input translation, bidirectional answer preservation, and—for every held source input x—a selected verified overhead record r satisfying the frozen resource law. Instantiation supplies its target input, actual overhead lookup and complete answer equivalence. Resource reconstruction gives the overhead row's observation trace, occupied support and their exact counts.

For stage records R, define trace([])=[], trace(r::R)=r.observed++trace(R), cost([])=0 and cost(r::R)=r.timeCount+cost(R). Induction substitutes each verified r.timeCount=|r.observed| and obtains

\[
 cost(R)=|trace(R)|,\quad
 trace(L\mathbin{++}R)=trace(L)\mathbin{++}trace(R),\quad
 cost(L\mathbin{++}R)=cost(L)+cost(R).
\]

A reduction chain is identity or a verified first reduction followed by another chain. Induction on the first chain composes it with a second while retaining every map, intermediate problem and overhead law. Hard(I,t) means every p∈I has such a chain to t; Complete(I,t) additionally requires t∈I. Composing each supplied chain transports hardness to a subsequent target. Membership in fresh::I splits the obligation into Chain(fresh,t) and Hard(I,t). Identity therefore supplies completeness for the singleton inventory.


167. Complete instance-family resource profiles

Each generated support cell retains its instance assignment and original resource record. Lookup membership reconstructs both provenance rows. Map-length preservation gives |map(record,cells)|=|support|, including repeated instances represented by different cells.

Let m(r) select time or space count. Define C([])=0 and C(r::R)=m(r)+C(R). A verified record has positive m because the original ledger requires nonempty observation and occupied support. Thus a nonempty verified family has positive total. Normalizing C(R)/|R| to positive fraction p/q preserves the exact equation

\[
 p\,|R|=C(R)\,q.
\]

For complete cells replace |R| by |support| using the count identity. The original exact-event weighting supplies support parts and propagates its original admission errors.

For an attained peak k, the greatest class is [r∈R:m(r)=m(k)]. Filter membership proves this condition. If r bounds every member, compare it with the peak; the peak also bounds r, so antisymmetry gives equal costs. Hence this class retains all and only tied maxima. Induction gives C(L++R)=C(L)+C(R) and, when every m(r)≤B, C(R)≤|R|B. Taking a singleton right list gives the exact successor aggregate. The empirical alternative retains preSeal=false, mutation=false, allRows=true and identified=true from the original measurement predicate.


168. Exact approximation and retained objective distinction

The approximation construction retains the registered optimization carrier, preference relation and positive generated objective-cell traces. All 256 original candidates, resource witnesses, controls and native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Approximation-Complexity Law.


Complete objective data supplies each feasible candidate x with a held positive cell list C_x. The registered objective order is (x,y)∈preferred iff |C_x|<|C_y|. A retained optimum o has no incoming better preference row. If |C_x|<|C_o|, the order equivalence supplies precisely the excluded row (x,o). Therefore |C_o|≤|C_x|. Applying this bound in both directions to two optima proves their objective lengths equal.

Put b=|C_o| and c=|C_x|. Completeness supplies b>0 and the optimum argument gives b≤c. The source approximation operation returns the normalized exact part p/q for b/c and proves

\[
 bq=pc.
\]

A missing objective lookup contradicts completeness at that held candidate. With b>0 but not b≤c, the original approximation operation returns its invalid-lower-optimum error. A verified computation ending in x retains run(request)=ok(before++[x]), the full numbered observation projection and unchanged occupied support. Appending feasible candidates retains o whenever the original no-better-row condition still holds. The singleton with empty preference relation is its own optimum.

\[
 extract(F,k,x)=lookup(lookup(F,x),k).
\]

A successful extraction yields both actual rows (x,fields) and (k,coordinate). Two successes on the same lookup have equal coordinates. Define Family(c)=[x∈inputs:extract(F,k,x)=some(c)]. Filter membership gives this exact condition; total extraction places every held input in its obtained class, while uniqueness makes classes with distinct coordinates disjoint. A missing extraction at a held input contradicts totality.

170. Shortest exact descriptions at declared finite depth

The frozen alphabet generates every nonempty description through the declared depth. Filtering by the original decoding relation retains exactly those descriptions that reconstruct the target object. The original boundary remains: unrestricted shortest-description values are uncomputable, and finite censuses state their depth explicitly. The finite minimum procedure does not assert unrestricted completeness. All 256 original candidates and complete native program records and hashes match pinned V3.


The finite census is the original nonempty word layers through depth d filtered by exact decoding to object x. Thus

\[
 w\in Census_d(x)\iff
 (\exists n,\ 0<n\le d\land w\in W_n)\land decode(w)=ok(x).
\]

On a nonempty census, shortestFrom compares the next length with the current best and retains the shorter, choosing the next on a tie. Induction proves its result is a census member and has length no greater than every member. Filtering by equality to this attained minimum retains exactly all tied minima: the reverse direction follows by antisymmetry between a proposed minimum and the attained member. Every retained minimum therefore decodes to x; the selected registry request then reconstructs its unchanged execution by the interpretation equation.

If d≤e, the same width witness remains admitted at depth e. If |w|>d, word-length characterization contradicts the census width bound, excluding w. The singleton minimum is itself. A retained translation supplies its actual encoding row and decoder result; if a translated census member satisfies |translated|≤|source|+|overhead|, the target minimum satisfies that same upper bound by transitivity. Concatenation length is exactly additive. These are finite-depth minimum and explicit-overhead results.


171. Exact search on registered carriers

The translated search operation preserves the original admission order: repeated carrier identities are rejected before target membership is checked. A held target yields its original position label and exact identity. Native induction proves finite exhaustion, reconstructs the carrier as the unmatched prefix followed by the target and remaining suffix, and establishes that every earlier comparison differs from the target. Appending further carrier rows retains an already found position; the singleton base returns position-1. These search results combine with the exact ordering construction and complete native entry-program comparison presented below.


The scan equations are scan(t,[])=none and

\[
 scan(t,x::R)=
 \begin{cases}some(0),&x=t,\\map(succ,scan(t,R)),&x\ne t.\end{cases}
\]

For held t, induction on the carrier finds an index: the head match returns zero, otherwise membership lies in the tail and the inductive index is incremented. Induction on a successful scan also reconstructs

\[
 carrier=B\mathbin{++}(t::A),\qquad |B|=index,\qquad
 \forall x\in B,\ x\ne t.
\]

The matching head gives B=[]; a nonmatching head is prepended to the tail's unmatched prefix. Positional lookup at that index consequently returns t. With distinct carrier and held target, the executable result is (position-(index+1),t). Its guard order first rejects duplicate carriers, then rejects absence, retaining the original errors. A successful scan in L remains at the same index in L++R by induction on L, so admitted extensions preserve the found position. The singleton returns (position-1,t).


172. Exact ordering and complete recursion traces

The registered finite carrier generates its full arrangement census. All three original search and recursion witnesses pass. The ordering predicate on x::R requires every pair (x,y), y∈R, in the registered relation and recursively requires ordering of R. Its empty case is true. Induction equates this Boolean with the corresponding empty/next invariant. Filtering the complete arrangement census therefore gives, for a nonempty distinct carrier C,

\[
 r\in orders(C,\rho)\iff Perm(r,C)\land Invariant(\rho,r).
\]

Permutation preserves carrier membership and length. Combining membership with the class ledger gives x in class a of r iff x∈C and classify(x)=a, retaining distinct identities in each duplicate-value class. To append fresh, require (x,fresh)∈ρ for every old x. Induction on the invariant splits each comparison into an old element or fresh and preserves the earlier order.

For successor positions P and exact step f, recursion has V(b,[])=[b] and V(b,p::P)=b::V(f(b,p),P). Induction constructs the corresponding retained step certificate and proves |V(b,P)|=|P|+1. Distinct successor identities make the original wrapper return this exact trace; duplicate identities retain its rejection.

Arithmetic Execution(a,ops,rows,b) has identity Execution(a,[],[],a). A successor requires execute(op,a)=ok(c), then a certificate from c through the remaining operations, and prepends row(op,stepInputs(op,a),c). Induction on ops in the forward direction and on the certificate in reverse proves

\[
 recordFrom(a,ops)=ok(rows,b)\iff Execution(a,ops,rows,b).
\]

The same successor structure gives |rows|=|ops| and map(operation,rows)=ops. Each constructed row binds outputHash=partIdentity(output). Its Part value carries 0<n≤d and Coprime(n,d). Original positive normalization additionally supplies n·d′=n′·d, preserving the exact fraction.

The existing generated-trace replay theorem applied to this correspondence gives replayFrom(a,rows)=ok(b). 174. Finite sequence matches and position reconstruction

For the registered subsequence alternative, complete finite scanning retains every skip/take choice that reconstructs the target sequence. Each match carries the source positions of its held symbols. For starting offset k, target x::N and source y::R, the complete match generator first emits all skip matches at k+1, then—only when y=x—prepends k to every take match for N in R. An empty target emits the single empty position list; a nonempty target in an empty source emits none. Induction establishes the equivalence

\[
 positions\in matchesFrom(k,N,S)\iff Match(k,N,S,positions),
\]

where Match has exactly done, skip and take constructors. The forward direction splits membership between skip and take blocks; the reverse direction inserts each constructor into its corresponding generated block. Induction gives |positions|=|N| and reconstructs N as a source-order selection.

For each (i,x)∈zip(positions,N), the same induction gives S=B++x::A and i=k+|B|. A skip prepends its source head to B; a take at the current head uses B=[]; later takes apply the tail induction. Thus k≤i<k+|S|. Repeated symbols retain their distinct offsets. Extending the source appends to the unused suffix in every constructor, preserving each prior position list. A singleton matching symbol has the singleton position list [k].


175. Finite graph traversal certificates

The traversal certificate retains the complete frontier and visited ledger at each original search step. Each traversal snapshot retains unseen support U, visited list V and complete frontier Q. Its branches, in original order, are: Q=[] returns false; a head equal to the target returns true; a different already-visited head is skipped; otherwise the fresh head h is expanded. Expansion sets

\[
 V'=V\mathbin{++}[h],\qquad Q'=rest\mathbin{++}outgoingUnvisited(edges,h,V').
\]

The frontier invariant places every frontier node in U or V. A fresh h therefore belongs to U and its removal strictly reduces |U|. Skipping keeps U and shortens Q. Both branches decrease the lexicographic measure (|U|,|Q|), so well-founded induction constructs a finite snapshot trace without discarding frontier rows.

Induction on that trace applies the matching source search equation at each constructor, proving searchLoop(initialSnapshot)=recordedAnswer. For distinct origin and target, this is the original reachability result. Under graph admission, true reconstructs a complete path by reachability/path equivalence; false excludes any such path because that path would force true. The same-origin case retains the original test of node membership. The empirical translation condition remains preSeal=false, mutation=false, allRows=true and identified=true.

and yields |U|=|T|+|tasks| and map(key,U)=map(key,T)++map(key,tasks). Thus distinctness of this combined identity sequence transfers to the final table. Missing dependency values or a non-singleton recurrence match halt the construction.

178. Complete optimization traversal

The traversal retains every feasible candidate in source order and every exact incoming dominance row. Its terminal class is proved equal to the original complete undominated selection. For candidate x, eliminators(P,x) filters every preference row whose target is x. Keep(x) means no preference row targets x; advance appends x to the incumbent precisely in that case. Induction gives

\[
 scan(P,I,F)=I\mathbin{++}retained(F,P).
\]

The kept branch appends its candidate and reassociates concatenation; the eliminated branch leaves the incumbent unchanged. With I=[], terminal membership is exactly x∈F and no incoming dominance row. Under preference admission, each eliminated feasible x has a distinct held dominator recorded in eliminators(P,x). Ties and incomparable survivors are retained; classification is unique exactly when the complete survivor list is a singleton.

History begins with I and recurses after each advance, giving |F|+1 incumbent states. Serial scan first computes scan(P,I,L), then uses that exact incumbent for R. Its history equation removes the first history's last state before adjoining the second complete history, retaining the shared state once. With support F++G and preference P++Q, survival requires membership in either support and absence of both old and new dominators. If an old survivor disappears, its elimination witness cannot be in P and must therefore be a new row in Q.

Every registered schedule executes the same held deterministic kernel and initial form. For each registered (name,actions), branches retains the same machine and initial form with that action list.
