Outcome classes from Section 162 cover every branch and are disjoint for different outcomes.

Success names are the identity projection of the target-outcome class. Map/filter membership gives

\[
 name\in Success(o)\iff\exists b\in Schedule,\ b.name=name\land outcome(b)=o.
\]

Consequently success support is contained in the whole identity support. Induction on filtering preserves identity distinctness, so canonical schedules admit the exact event operation. For a positive success fraction p/q, the original weight theorem gives

\[
 |Success(o)|q=p\,|ScheduleNames|,\qquad p\le q.
\]

An empty success list retains the original empty-One weight marker. 180. Causally independent algorithm layers

Every task in an admitted ready layer reads the same prior retained result table. The dependency graph places each prerequisite before the layer and excludes dependencies between simultaneous tasks. Complete synchronization rows retain the corresponding prerequisite identities. Native layer execution and its typed trace imply one another, retaining each task identity and one exact recurrence result per task. Local invariants propagate from prior dependency results, and every generated output reconstructs through the original finite decomposition and recurrence rows.

Successive layers append their complete result rows to the shared table. The combined result identities equal the initial identities followed by the complete flattened layer trace. Complete task coverage therefore transfers directly to the work ledger, while the existing exhaustive path construction supplies the span certificate. Composition and successor preserve previous records and their shared interfaces. No physical speedup is claimed without an implementation experiment. All 256 original candidates, witnesses and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Parallel-Algorithm Law.


Let \(B\) be the retained result table and \(L=[(k_i,d_i)]_{i=1}^m\) a layer of task identities and dependency lists. The source execution reads every dependency list against the same \(B\):
\[
 \operatorname{layerRun}(R,B,[]) = \operatorname{some}[],
 \qquad
 \operatorname{layerRun}(R,B,(k,d)::L)
 =\operatorname{some}((k,v)::W)
\]
precisely when \(\operatorname{inputs}(B,d)=\operatorname{some}u\), the selected recurrence gives \(\operatorname{result}(R,k,u)=\operatorname{some}v\), and \(\operatorname{layerRun}(R,B,L)=\operatorname{some}W\). A failed read or recurrence produces failure. Thus earlier rows in the same layer never become inputs to its later tasks. The native reconstruction and identity statements are
\[
 \operatorname{layerRun}(R,B,L)=\operatorname{some}W
 \iff \operatorname{LayerExecution}(R,B,L,W),\qquad
 \operatorname{ids}(W)=\operatorname{ids}(L),\quad |W|=|L|.
\]
For an admitted ready layer, every graph prerequisite belongs to \(\operatorname{ids}(B)\), and no edge joins two tasks in that layer. Under the retained local-rule hypothesis, validated dependency values imply a validated recurrence result; induction over the layer then validates every row of \(W\). Instantiating this implication with the source derivation property reconstructs each result's derivation.

Writing \(E(B,\mathcal L)\) for execution of successive layers, its equations are
\[
 E(B,[])=\operatorname{some}B,\qquad
 E(B,L::\mathcal L)=E(B\mathbin{+\!\!+}W,\mathcal L)
 \quad\text{when }\operatorname{layerRun}(R,B,L)=\operatorname{some}W.
\]
The scheduled trace retains readiness at each intermediate table. Induction gives
\[
 \operatorname{ids}(B_{\rm final})
 =\operatorname{ids}(B_{\rm initial})
   \mathbin{+\!\!+}\operatorname{flatten}([\operatorname{ids}(L):L\in\mathcal L]).
\]
With an empty initial table, coverage of final node identities therefore yields coverage by the flattened work trace. The same layer induction propagates complete derivations, while concatenation of executions uses their shared retained table.


181. Global replay of retained local process events

A retained event identifies its process, prior local state, action and resulting local state. The replay checker resolves the registered process kernel and verifies the unchanged local transition before updating that process in the global state table. Native certificates and successful replay imply one another for every finite event trace. Process identities are preserved, and every process absent from the trace retains its original local state. Serial composition and successor extension reconstruct through the shared intermediate state. These results establish the local-state replay component; the message and causal/fault certificates below complete its connection to distributed communication.


Write an event as \(e=(\iota,p,b,a,c)\), with identity \(\iota\), process \(p\), prior state \(b\), action \(a\), and resulting state \(c\). For registry \(K\) and store \(S\), the exact successful replay condition is
\[
 \operatorname{replayEvent}(K,S,e)=\operatorname{some}S'
 \iff
 \exists M,\ 
 \operatorname{lookupFirst}(K,p)=\operatorname{some}M
 \land\operatorname{lookupFirst}(S,p)=\operatorname{some}b
 \land\operatorname{step}(M,b,a)=\operatorname{ok}c
 \land S'=\operatorname{update}(S,p,c).
\]
Here update preserves the store's list structure and replaces the value of every row whose process identity equals \(p\); lookup retains the original first-match convention. A missing process, wrong prior state, transition error, or mismatched resulting state rejects the event.

The full trace is generated by identity and successor rules:
\[
 \operatorname{GlobalTrace}(K,S,[],S),\qquad
 \frac{\operatorname{LocalStep}(K,S,e)\quad
       \operatorname{GlobalTrace}(K,\operatorname{update}(S,p,c),T,F)}
      {\operatorname{GlobalTrace}(K,S,e::T,F)}.
\]
Induction in both directions establishes
\[
 \operatorname{replay}(K,S,T)=\operatorname{some}F
 \iff\operatorname{GlobalTrace}(K,S,T,F),\qquad
 \operatorname{ids}(F)=\operatorname{ids}(S).
\]
If \(q\ne e.p\) for every event \(e\in T\), each update preserves its lookup, hence
\[
 \operatorname{lookupFirst}(F,q)=\operatorname{lookupFirst}(S,q).
\]
Two traces from \(S\) to \(M\) and from \(M\) to \(F\) compose into their concatenation from \(S\) to \(F\); the successor case is the corresponding one-event extension.


182. Exact distributed messages and causal reconstruction

Separate send and receive ledgers retain each message identity, payload, process and event identity. Every record binds to the registered alphabet and an actual process event. Every declared receipt has a retained send with the same message identity and payload at an earlier event position. Sends without a declared receipt remain visible; delivery is not assumed. Canonical event identities and exact positions make the causal relation transitive and irreflexive, excluding causal return cycles.

An original local transition error prevents global replay from continuing to a result. A completed certificate combines the full local-state trace, canonical event identities and verified message ledger, reconstructing the terminal global state while preserving all process identities. Trace extension retains prior port bindings and causal decisions; ledger composition retains both complete record collections. System implementation and physical network claims remain outside the formal theorem. All 256 original candidates, witnesses and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Distributed-Algorithm Law.


For a port \(r=(m,\iota,p,v)\), let \(A\) be the payload alphabet and \(T\) the event list. The binding and causal predicates have the exact meanings
\[
 \operatorname{bound}(A,T,r)
 \iff v\in A\land\exists e\in T,\ e.\iota=\iota\land e.p=p,
\]
\[
 \operatorname{before}_T(x,y)
 \iff \exists i,j,\ 
 \operatorname{scan}(x,\operatorname{ids}(T))=\operatorname{some}i
 \land\operatorname{scan}(y,\operatorname{ids}(T))=\operatorname{some}j
 \land i<j.
\]
Scan uses the source's first matching position. For send ledger \(S\) and receipt ledger \(R\), validity is exactly
\[
 \bigl(\forall s\in S,\operatorname{bound}(A,T,s)\bigr)
 \land\bigl(\forall r\in R,\operatorname{bound}(A,T,r)\bigr)
 \land\bigl(\forall r\in R,\exists s\in S,\
 s.m=r.m\land s.v=r.v\land\operatorname{before}_T(s.\iota,r.\iota)\bigr).
\]
This quantifies over every receipt without requiring every send to have a receipt. An unpaired receipt contradicts the final conjunct. Determinacy of scan identifies the middle index in two causal comparisons, so \(i<j\) and \(j<k\) give \(i<k\); \(i<i\) is impossible. These establish transitivity, absence of self-cause, and absence of causal return.

The complete certificate conjoins distinct event identities, the global local-state trace, and this message-ledger validity:
\[
 C=\operatorname{Distinct}(\operatorname{ids}(T))
 \land\operatorname{GlobalTrace}(K,I,T,F)
 \land\operatorname{valid}(A,T,S,R).
\]
Consequently \(C\) implies \(\operatorname{replay}(K,I,T)=\operatorname{some}F\) and \(\operatorname{ids}(F)=\operatorname{ids}(I)\). Under the registered-machine and current-state premises, a local transition error yields a failed event replay, which yields failed replay for the trace beginning with that event. Appending events preserves existing bindings and successful scan positions; therefore it preserves this ledger's validity. Two valid send/receipt ledgers over the same event list compose by list concatenation, retaining witnesses from their respective original ledgers.


183. Finite-prefix streaming and retained distinctions

Every generated input has a complete ordered prefix family, including its identity prefix. Native proofs establish both directions between prefix membership and exact input decomposition, giving one result record per prefix and a total of input length plus one records. Each successful result reconstructs its complete original execution path and terminal state summary. The original transition relation determines the next summary, and serial continuation uses the retained intermediate state.

The full ordered pair ledger retains exactly distinct supported inputs whose state summaries coincide. Inputs with different summaries are excluded from that loss ledger. Input extension preserves every earlier prefix, output and lost-distinction record. Each resource certificate retains the complete observation trace and supplied occupied support with their exact counts. Every result names a finite generated prefix boundary; no completed infinite stream is admitted. All 256 original candidates, witnesses and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Online and Streaming Algorithm Law.


For a finite input \(x\), the complete ordered prefix list is defined recursively:
\[
 P([])=[[]],\qquad P(a::x)=[]::[a::p:p\in P(x)].
\]
Induction proves both its exact boundary and its count:
\[
 p\in P(x)\iff\exists s,\ x=p\mathbin{+\!\!+}s,\qquad |P(x)|=|x|+1.
\]
The output ledger is \(O(x)=[(p,\operatorname{run}(M,i,p)):p\in P(x)]\), so it retains exactly the original run for each prefix and has \(|x|+1\) rows. Its summary is
\[
 \sigma(p)=
 \begin{cases}
 \operatorname{failed}(r),&\operatorname{run}(M,i,p)=\operatorname{error}r,\\
 \operatorname{returned}(\operatorname{endLabel}(t)),
       &\operatorname{run}(M,i,p)=\operatorname{ok}t.
 \end{cases}
\]
For every successful prefix, native path reconstruction gives \(x=p\mathbin{+\!\!+}s\), \(t=b\mathbin{+\!\!+}[q]\), the original path from \(i\) through \(p\) to \(q\), and \(\sigma(p)=\operatorname{returned}(\operatorname{some}q)\). If that path extends by \(\operatorname{step}(M,q,a)=\operatorname{ok}q'\), with the original initial-state admissibility, then
\[
 \sigma(p\mathbin{+\!\!+}[a])=\operatorname{returned}(\operatorname{some}q').
\]
Serial path composition gives the corresponding statement for an arbitrary admitted continuation.

For supplied finite support \(U\), form the full ordered product list \(U\times U\), retaining its source ordering and multiplicities, then filter it:
\[
 L(U)=\operatorname{filter}_{u\ne v\ \land\ \sigma(u)=\sigma(v)}(U\times U).
\]
The exact membership theorem is
\[
 (u,v)\in L(U)\iff
 u\in U\land v\in U\land u\ne v\land\sigma(u)=\sigma(v).
\]
Different summaries therefore exclude a pair. Since every old prefix remains a prefix after appending input, old output rows and pairs in \(L(P(x))\) remain in the extended ledgers. Finally, for a successful run and its accepted resource record with supplied occupied support \(H\),
\[
 \operatorname{map}(\operatorname{snd},\ell.\operatorname{timeTrace})=t,\quad
 \ell.\operatorname{spaceSupport}=H,\quad
 \ell.\operatorname{timeCount}=|p|+1,\quad
 \ell.\operatorname{spaceCount}=|H|.
\]
These equations retain the full finite observation trace as well as the exact support supplied to the recorder.


184. Exact oriented numerical-error records

For equal nonempty finite supports of exact positive fractions, the numerical error ledger retains one ordered cell record per paired input. Equal values carry the structural One marker and the equal orientation; unequal values carry their exact positive difference and the held-below or held-above orientation. Native arithmetic proofs identify each normalized difference by its cross-multiplied rational value. Nonpositive values and unequal or empty supports retain the original rejection behavior. These results establish the error-ledger component of the numerical formalisation.


For reference \(x=n_x/d_x>0\) and approximation \(y=n_y/d_y>0\), define the exact cross-products
\[
 A=n_y d_x,\qquad E=n_x d_y,\qquad D=d_xd_y>0.
\]
The source error operation is the following oriented positive-part record:
\[
 \operatorname{err}(x,y)=
 \begin{cases}
 (1,\text{equal}),&A=E,\\
 (\operatorname{normalizePositive}(E-A,D),\text{held-below}),&A<E,\\
 (\operatorname{normalizePositive}(A-E,D),\text{held-above}),&E<A.
 \end{cases}
\]
The equal case deliberately retains the structural One marker. For either unequal branch, with positive difference numerator \(\Delta\) and normalized result \(n/d\), preservation of exact value is proved by
\[
 \Delta d=nD,\qquad n>0.
\]
Thus normalization retains the exact rational difference and the orientation separately. The ordered row operation starts at index \(1\), emits \((\text{cell-}i,\operatorname{err}(x_i,y_i))\), and recurses at \(i+1\). Its outer admission guard is
\[
 |X|=|Y|\ \land\ X\ne[],
\]
while each paired element must have the positive constructor. A zero or negative constructor rejects the scientific value; a length mismatch or empty reference rejects the support. These are distinct original error branches, not numerical tolerances.


185. Complete numerical error-ledger reconstruction

Successful numerical error-ledger execution is equivalent to a complete ordered certificate pairing each reference value with its approximation and exact oriented row. The certificate proves positivity of both input supports and every error value, equal support lengths, and one row per pair without omission. Conversely, every equal-length nonempty pair of positive supports constructs a successful ledger. Serial composition and successor extension preserve earlier rows while advancing the original cell labels. Independent finite expression comparisons exercise all 1,849 pairs of supports of widths up to two over six exact signed/zero/positive test values; their output and rejection records agree with native Lean execution.


Let \(\mathcal R_i(X,Y,L)\) denote the retained ordered row certificate. Its constructors are
\[
 \mathcal R_i([],[],[]),\qquad
 \frac{\mathcal R_{i+1}(X,Y,L)}
 {\mathcal R_i(x::X,y::Y,(\text{cell-}i,\operatorname{err}(x,y))::L)}
 \quad(x,y\text{ positive}).
\]
Structural induction on these constructors reproduces the executable row operation. Conversely, inspection of each successful executable branch constructs the corresponding certificate. Therefore
\[
 \operatorname{rowsFrom}(i,X,Y)=\operatorname{ok}L
 \iff\mathcal R_i(X,Y,L),
\]
\[
 \operatorname{exactErrorLedger}(X,Y)=\operatorname{ok}L
 \iff X\ne[]\land\mathcal R_1(X,Y,L).
\]
Induction gives positivity of both supports and every error numerator, together with
\[
 |L|=|X|=|Y|.
\]
Each row retains its paired positive values, index label, and exact error operation. Conversely, the recursively generated positive-row list provides a certificate for any two equal-length positive supports; nonemptiness then gives outer ledger admission.

For concatenated supports, the second certificate begins at the advanced cell index:
\[
 \mathcal R_i(X,Y,L)\land
 \mathcal R_{i+|X|}(X',Y',L')
 \ \Longrightarrow\
 \mathcal R_i(X\mathbin{+\!\!+}X',Y\mathbin{+\!\!+}Y',L\mathbin{+\!\!+}L').
\]
Taking the second pair of supports to be singletons proves successor extension with label \(\text{cell-}(i+|X|)\). The finite comparison family uses \(1+6+6^2=43\) supports, including the empty support, so its complete ordered pair family has \(43^2=1,849\) cases. Rejected supports and signed or zero elements remain part of that comparison.


186. Retained numerical update observations

Every state in a generated update trace resolves to its registered numerical carrier and a complete exact error ledger. The observation checker and the retained certificate imply one another; the ordered projection reproduces the entire state trace, and every numerical value is traced to its registered carrier row. Connecting these records to the original transition execution gives exactly one observation for each action plus the initial state. Composition retains all intermediate records. An admitted successor step appends its exact observation while preserving the earlier trace. Missing carrier values or rejected error ledgers cannot produce an accepted observation trace.


Let \(T\) be the registered state-to-value table and \(X\) the fixed reference support. The numerical observation recursion is
\[
 N(T,X,[])=\operatorname{some}[],
\]
\[
 N(T,X,s::S)=\operatorname{some}((s,Y,L)::Q)
\]
exactly when
\[
 \operatorname{lookupFirst}(T,s)=\operatorname{some}Y,\qquad
 \operatorname{exactErrorLedger}(X,Y)=\operatorname{ok}L,\qquad
 N(T,X,S)=\operatorname{some}Q.
\]
Missing lookups, rejected ledgers, and failed suffix observations propagate failure. The corresponding inductive certificate retains these three witnesses at every row. Reconstruction and replay prove
\[
 N(T,X,S)=\operatorname{some}Q
 \iff\operatorname{Records}(T,X,S,Q).
\]
Induction then yields
\[
 \operatorname{map}(\operatorname{fst},Q)=S,\qquad |Q|=|S|,
\]
and for every \((s,Y,L)\in Q\), the registered row \((s,Y)\in T\), nonempty reference, and full certificate \(\mathcal R_1(X,Y,L)\).

If the original state execution satisfies \(\operatorname{run}(M,i,a)=\operatorname{ok}S\), its path theorem gives \(|S|=|a|+1\). Combining this equality with the observation count gives
\[
 |Q|=|a|+1.
\]
An admitted transition from the retained terminal state to \(s'\), its registered value \(Y'\), and accepted error ledger \(L'\) extend both executions:
\[
 S'=S\mathbin{+\!\!+}[s'],\qquad
 Q'=Q\mathbin{+\!\!+}[(s',Y',L')].
\]
The earlier records remain intact. Concatenation of observation certificates follows directly by induction over the first certificate.


187. Finite numerical boundary certificates

The numerical carrier uses exact positive parts on finite generated cells. Its retained state updates reconstruct every oriented error row. Output enclosure is the complete held-order selection between registered bounds: every selected form belongs to the carrier and has both endpoint relations. Original exhaustive lower and upper bound results enclose each held output. A terminal certificate retains original state-constructor and order admission, the actual finite execution, all intermediate observations, its explicit target and its enclosure. The final numerical value and complete error ledger reconstruct from that certificate.

Projecting each original transition to its source and target connects executed paths to the admitted dynamics relation. The registered stability check holds exactly when every registered perturbation has a retained path to the target. Complete perturbation path families establish that check, and the target retains both enclosure relations. Successor construction requires the exact next transition, numerical record and enclosure evidence, retaining all earlier observations. This formalises the declared finite certificate boundary; it does not assert that every numerical procedure converges or import an ungenerated infinite limit.

The complete 256-candidate numerical program, its original operational witnesses, controls and all recorded hashes agree with pinned V3 under fresh native execution. Together with the carrier, update and boundary derivations, these results complete the Fold Numerical-Algorithm Law.


For generated carrier \(C\), held order relation \(\preceq\), and bounds \(l,u\), the enclosure is the complete filtered list
\[
 I(C,\preceq,l,u)=[v\in C:l\preceq v\land v\preceq u].
\]
Its membership theorem is exactly
\[
 v\in I(C,\preceq,l,u)
 \iff v\in C\land l\preceq v\land v\preceq u.
\]
If the original lower- and upper-bound operations return \(L\) and \(U\) for held support \(H\), then \(l\in L\), \(u\in U\), and \(v\in H\cap C\) imply this enclosure: the lower-bound witness supplies \(l\preceq v\), and the upper-bound witness supplies \(v\preceq u\).

A terminal certificate retains all six conditions
\[
 \operatorname{create}(M)=\operatorname{ok}M,\quad
 \operatorname{isPartialOrder}(C,\preceq)=\operatorname{true},\quad
 \operatorname{run}(M,i,a)=\operatorname{ok}S,\quad
 N(T,X,S)=\operatorname{some}Q,
\]
\[
 S=B\mathbin{+\!\!+}[t]\text{ for some }B,\qquad
 t\in I(C,\preceq,l,u).
\]
Induction over the observation trace ending at \(t\) produces
\[
 Q=Q_0\mathbin{+\!\!+}[(t,Y,L)],\qquad
 \operatorname{lookupFirst}(T,t)=\operatorname{some}Y,\qquad
 X\ne[]\land\mathcal R_1(X,Y,L).
\]
The terminal bounds are \(l\preceq t\preceq u\), and the complete observation count remains \(|Q|=|a|+1\).

Project each original transition \((s,a,s')\) to \((s,s')\), obtaining \(D_M\). Induction over an executed path gives reachability in \(D_M\). The registered stability equivalence is
\[
 \operatorname{stableUnderRegisteredPerturbations}(D_M,t,P)=\operatorname{true}
 \iff\forall p\in P,\operatorname{Reaches}(D_M,p,t).
\]
Thus an explicit execution path from every registered perturbation constructs stability, and the retained terminal enclosure supplies the same two endpoint relations. The identity certificate uses the admitted initial state, its numerical record and enclosure. A successor certificate requires the actual next transition, accepted next record and next enclosure, and appends them to the prior trace; its derivation does not replace those required witnesses with a convergence assumption.


188. Exact symbolic transformations within the frozen grammar

The generated expression grammar contains positive exact values, held names and binary joins. Native evaluation preserves original binding uniqueness, literal admission and left-to-right error behavior. A successful result is equivalent to a complete evaluation derivation. Bound names retain their original raw Fraction values; the scientific normalization certificate separately carries the positive-binding boundary. Exact signed arithmetic is represented constructively, including cancellation and normalized positive differences.

A held tree occurrence is identified by its generated left/right address and surrounding context. Native proofs establish both directions of that representation and reconstruct the complete expression. Equivalence of successful exact values is preserved inside every context. Each rewrite record retains a frozen registered rule, its held occurrence and its equivalence proof. All intermediate and final values are preserved along a complete trace; composition and successor retain earlier records. A normalization certificate includes the terminal absence of an admitted rewrite. Its final expression and exact positive scientific result reconstruct from the retained trace. No external algebraic rewrite theory or unconditional termination or confluence assertion is required by these results.

All 256 original symbolic kernels, witnesses, controls and complete program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3. Independent evaluation of the source expressions agrees with Lean in all 7007 cases spanning 143 expressions and 49 environments, including invalid literals, missing and duplicate names, signed values and nested joins. These results complete the Fold Symbolic-Computation Law on its frozen grammar boundary.

Let \(\rho\) be the original binding list. Successful evaluation has the exact characterization
\[
 \operatorname{eval}(\rho,e)=\operatorname{ok}v
 \iff \operatorname{Distinct}(\operatorname{names}(\rho))
       \land\operatorname{Evaluation}(\rho,e,v).
\]
The evaluation derivation has positive-literal, registered-name and binary-join rules:
\[
 \frac{}{\rho\vdash\operatorname{value}(p)\Downarrow p}\ (p>0),\qquad
 \frac{\operatorname{lookupFirst}(\rho,n)=\operatorname{some}v}
      {\rho\vdash\operatorname{name}(n)\Downarrow v},\qquad
 \frac{\rho\vdash a\Downarrow u\quad\rho\vdash b\Downarrow v}
      {\rho\vdash\operatorname{join}(a,b)\Downarrow\operatorname{add}(u,v)}.
\]
The executable operation checks binding uniqueness first and evaluates a join's left child before its right child; it propagates the first encountered error. The name rule retains its bound value without imposing the positive-literal restriction on that value.

For a generated context \(C\), plugging and addressing satisfy
\[
 \operatorname{focus}(\operatorname{plug}(C,e),\operatorname{address}(C))
 =\operatorname{some}e.
\]
Conversely, successful focus supplies a context with that address and reconstructs the entire original expression by plugging. Define successful-value equivalence by
\[
 e\sim_\rho f\iff
 \forall v,\ \operatorname{eval}(\rho,e)=\operatorname{ok}v
 \iff\operatorname{eval}(\rho,f)=\operatorname{ok}v.
\]
Induction on contexts, using the two join evaluations, proves
\[
 e\sim_\rho f\Longrightarrow
 \operatorname{plug}(C,e)\sim_\rho\operatorname{plug}(C,f).
\]
Each admitted rewrite retains a registered rule, generated pattern and replacement, a generated context and the pattern/replacement equivalence. Contextual equivalence therefore proves each rewrite preserves successful values; induction on the complete rewrite trace preserves them at every source, target and terminal expression.

Normality is the generated-expression condition together with absence of any admitted rewrite whose source is that expression. A normalization certificate is a retained trace ending at such a normal expression. Hence
\[
 \operatorname{Normalized}(\rho,R,e,T,f)
 \land\operatorname{eval}(\rho,e)=\operatorname{ok}v
 \Longrightarrow\operatorname{eval}(\rho,f)=\operatorname{ok}v.
\]
With the separately retained positive-binding condition, evaluation induction also gives \(v=\operatorname{positive}(p)\) for some exact positive fraction \(p\). Normality is evidence in this certificate; it is not inferred from an unproved termination assertion.

189. Exact approximation-bound admission and source rows

The inherited approximation operation is accepted exactly when the optimum cell count is positive and no greater than the candidate cell count. This equivalence preserves the original signed-integer input domain and its rejection behavior. Each successful part reconstructs the exact optimum-to-candidate ratio by cross multiplication; its numerator is positive and bounded by its denominator. A retained bound record identifies both original objective rows. Extending those rows preserves the candidate, optimum and exact part without changing the objective or introducing a tolerance. These results establish the bound component of the approximation formalisation.

For the original signed integer inputs \(c,o\), successful approximation is characterized without restricting its input domain:
\[
 \operatorname{exactApproximation}(c,o)=\operatorname{ok}p
 \iff \exists C,O\in\mathbb N,\ 
 c=C\land o=O\land0<O\le C
 \land p=\operatorname{normalizePart}(O,C).
\]
Here natural values are embedded in the original integer type, and the normalization carries the displayed positivity and bound witnesses. Negative inputs, zero optimum, or optimum greater than candidate retain the original rejection. For accepted \(p=n/d\), normalization proves
\[
 Od=nC,\qquad 0<n\le d,\qquad C>0.
\]
Thus the accepted part is exactly the optimum-to-candidate ratio.

A bound record additionally retains objective rows \((k,C)\) and \((k_*,O)\), their membership in the original row list \(J\), and the accepted approximation result. These witnesses give
\[
 (k,C)\in J,\quad(k_*,O)\in J,\quad0<O\le C,\quad Od=nC.
\]
Appending rows to \(J\) transports these membership witnesses while leaving \(k,k_*,C,O,p\) unchanged. This construction preserves the original objective values and ratio.

190. Approximation with complete objective and execution ledgers

The exact objective observation binds the feasible candidate and held optimum to their original cell counts. Approximation partitions every original objective distinction into retained or lost records; the partition is exhaustive and disjoint. Loss requires unequal original values and equal approximate values. Identity loses no original distinction, and an admitted downstream composition cannot restore a distinction it has closed. Preserving the original row images preserves every earlier decision under extension.

The same candidate and optimum counts generate the original exact oriented error row. A result certificate retains original feasibility and preference admission, complete objective rows, the actual state path, every registered numerical observation and the exact terminal lookup. Native reconstruction proves that its error belongs to that candidate and reference. The resource ledger reproduces the entire state trace, supplied occupied support and exact counts, including the initial observation. Identity, serial continuation and successor preserve the recorded interfaces; the successor may carry a different candidate with the same reference. These finite certificates introduce no tolerance, metric or conventional approximation algorithm. All 256 original candidates, witnesses, controls and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Approximation-Algorithm Law.

Let \(D\) be the original ledger of objective distinctions, and let \(f\) and \(g\) be the exact and approximate observations. The two filtered ledgers are
\[
 L=[(a,b)\in D:g(a)=g(b)],\qquad
 K=[(a,b)\in D:g(a)\ne g(b)].
\]
Every member of \(D\) belongs to \(L\) or \(K\), and no pair belongs to both. Since \(D\) already records exact differences,
\[
 (a,b)\in L\Longrightarrow f(a)\ne f(b)\land g(a)=g(b).
\]
Taking \(g=f\) gives no loss. Equality of the approximate images remains equality under the admitted downstream observation composition, so lost distinctions remain lost; consequently any distinction kept after composition was kept before it. Extension preserves either decision when the original distinction remains present and both approximate images remain unchanged.

For a retained bound record with optimum \(O\) and candidate \(C\), define exact positive reference and candidate values by normalizing \(O/1\) and \(C/1\). Their singleton numerical ledger is
\[
 \operatorname{exactErrorLedger}([O],[C])
 =\operatorname{ok}[(\text{cell-1},\operatorname{err}(O,C))],
\]
where the displayed numbers denote those normalized positive constructors. The result certificate retains feasibility and preference admission, objective coverage and domain, the uneliminated optimum, both admitted observations, the original state path and all numerical records. In particular, its exact observation satisfies
\[
 f(k)=\operatorname{some}(\operatorname{toString}C),\qquad
 f(k_*)=\operatorname{some}(\operatorname{toString}O).
\]
If its state trace is \(B\mathbin{+\!\!+}[t]\), terminal lookup appends precisely the candidate's value and error record to the prior observations. Therefore
\[
 \operatorname{map}(\operatorname{fst},Q)=B\mathbin{+\!\!+}[t],\qquad
 |Q|=|a|+1.
\]
Its accepted resource ledger gives
\[
 \operatorname{map}(\operatorname{snd},\ell.\operatorname{timeTrace})
 =B\mathbin{+\!\!+}[t],\quad
 \ell.\operatorname{spaceSupport}=H,\quad
 \ell.\operatorname{timeCount}=|a|+1,\quad
 \ell.\operatorname{spaceCount}=|H|.
\]
Identity and concatenation follow from the original path and observation constructors. A changed-candidate successor additionally carries equality of the old and new reference values, the actual next transition, and the new terminal lookup; these witnesses give the new error row while retaining all previous observations.

191. Generated program syntax and complete occurrence records

The formation checker accepts exactly the generated value, name and binary join trees. Positive exact literal payloads and held names remain explicit. Rejection is equivalent to a constructive malformed derivation identifying an invalid literal, unsupported constructor or malformed child. Syntax membership imposes no binding environment: a well-formed name may remain unbound under execution.

The tree ledger retains the root and every child occurrence with its complete left/right address and exact subtree payload. Membership is equivalent to successful focus at that address, in both directions; one address identifies one subtree. Every occurrence of an accepted tree is formed and has a registered constructor. An invalid occurrence rejects the entire tree. The literal base has its identity record. Joining trees preserves both complete ledgers with their address prefixes, and generated contexts compose by retaining the held subtree. All 256 original candidates, the three exact semantics witnesses, controls and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Program-Syntax Law.

The formation recursion is
\[
 F(\operatorname{value}(p))=\operatorname{true}\ (p>0),\qquad
 F(\operatorname{name}(n))=\operatorname{true},\qquad
 F(\operatorname{join}(a,b))=F(a)\land F(b).
\]
Foreign, zero or negative literals and unsupported constructors return false. Induction on the expression and on the corresponding constructors proves both exact characterizations
\[
 F(e)=\operatorname{true}\iff\operatorname{Generated}(e),\qquad
 F(e)=\operatorname{false}\iff\operatorname{Malformed}(e).
\]
A malformed join retains a malformed left or right child witness. Formation is independent of evaluation bindings: \(F(\operatorname{name}(n))=\operatorname{true}\), while evaluating that name in the empty environment returns the original unbound-name error.

For a leaf \(e\), the occurrence ledger is \([([],e)]\). At a join it is
\[
 T(\operatorname{join}(a,b))
 =[([],\operatorname{join}(a,b))]
 \mathbin{+\!\!+}\operatorname{lift}_{L}(T(a))
 \mathbin{+\!\!+}\operatorname{lift}_{R}(T(b)),
\]
where each lift prefixes the corresponding direction to every address. Induction proves the complete occurrence equivalence
\[
 (p,s)\in T(e)\iff\operatorname{focus}(e,p)=\operatorname{some}s.
\]
Determinacy of focus implies that two rows with the same address have the same subtree. For a formed expression, contextual decomposition makes every retained subtree formed; its constructor is therefore value, name or join. Conversely, a retained malformed subtree contradicts whole-tree formation and yields rejection. The literal base and both lifted child ledgers remain explicit, and plugging a formed expression into a generated context preserves formation.

192. Complete name occurrences and registered scope

Every name occurrence in the registered expression tree has a retained structural address, exact label and original environment lookup. Ledger membership is equivalent to a native occurrence derivation. The ledger contains exactly as many records as the tree contains names; free and bound records form an exhaustive disjoint partition. A bound occurrence reconstructs its exact environment row, and a free occurrence has no registered lookup. Preserving lookup results preserves the entire occurrence ledger.

Every successful evaluation resolves every name occurrence to its original value. Conversely, resolving every occurrence in a generated expression constructs its evaluation derivation. The original unique-binding admission remains part of executable evaluation. These results establish the name-occurrence and scope component of the binding formalisation without assigning a new binding grammar.

For environment \(\rho\), address \(p\), and expression \(e\), the name ledger is generated by
\[
 N_\rho(p,\operatorname{value}(v))=[],\qquad
 N_\rho(p,\operatorname{name}(x))=[(p,x,\operatorname{lookupFirst}(\rho,x))],
\]
\[
 N_\rho(p,\operatorname{join}(a,b))
 =N_\rho(p\mathbin{+\!\!+}[L],a)
  \mathbin{+\!\!+}N_\rho(p\mathbin{+\!\!+}[R],b).
\]
Unsupported constructors contribute no name rows. The inductive occurrence judgment has precisely the name, left-child and right-child constructors, giving
\[
 r\in N_\rho(p,e)\iff\operatorname{Located}(\rho,p,e,r),\qquad
 r.\operatorname{value}=\operatorname{lookupFirst}(\rho,r.\operatorname{label}).
\]
Structural induction also gives \(|N_\rho(p,e)|=\operatorname{nameCount}(e)\). Filtering by whether the lookup is some value or none partitions every occurrence into bound or free rows; the two conditions cannot both hold. A successful lookup supplies the original environment row, while a free row has lookup none. Pointwise equality of lookups in two environments gives equality of their entire occurrence ledgers.

Induction over successful evaluation proves every located name has a value. Conversely, induction over generated syntax, resolving each located name, constructs
\[
 \bigl(\operatorname{Generated}(e)\land
       \forall p,r,\operatorname{Located}(\rho,p,e,r)
           \Rightarrow\exists v,\ r.\operatorname{value}=\operatorname{some}v\bigr)
 \Longrightarrow\exists v,\operatorname{Evaluation}(\rho,e,v).
\]
The distinct-binding guard is additionally required when replaying this derivation through the executable evaluator.

193. Binding-extension formation and exact substitution behavior

The SEMX extension supplies value, name, join, let and four-branch same term shapes. Its formation decision is equivalent to a native derivation retaining the original bound-name list; formed terms have no free name relative to that list. Free-name set membership preserves the original initializer and body scopes. Value payloads are carried unchanged. Whole-tree renaming retains the source operation, and identity renaming preserves the complete term.

The original fresh-name search examines u, v, w and z in order, excluding exactly the free names of the body and replacement and the substituted name. Every selected name has that provenance and those exclusions. Exhaustion holds exactly when all four candidates are unavailable.

The translated substitution preserves literal values, exact name matches, ordered join and same recursion, the held-binder stop in the body, substitution into the initializer and conflicting-binder renaming before descent. Accumulated name mappings are proved equivalent to the source whole-body rename operation for arbitrary generated terms. Fresh-search failure precedes initializer substitution as in the source. The original let-y/name-x witness reconstructs its u-bound result. These are exact behavioral correspondence results; a general capture-preservation result is not inferred from the witness.

For bound-name list \(B\), the SEMX formation equations include
\[
 W_B(\operatorname{value}(v))=\operatorname{true},\quad
 W_B(\operatorname{name}(x))=[x\in B],\quad
 W_B(\operatorname{join}(a,b))=W_B(a)\land W_B(b),
\]
\[
 W_B(\operatorname{let}(x,a,b))
 =W_B(a)\land W_{B\mathbin{+\!\!+}[x]}(b),\qquad
 W_B(\operatorname{same}(a,b,c,d))
 =((W_B(a)\land W_B(b))\land W_B(c))\land W_B(d).
\]
Formation constructors mirror these equations, yielding \(W_B(t)=\operatorname{true}\iff\operatorname{Formation}(B,t)\). The free-name recursion uses the same initializer/body scopes, with disjunction instead of conjunction; induction on formation gives \(\operatorname{free}(B,x,t)=\operatorname{false}\) for every \(x\).

Fresh selection scans \([u,v,w,z]\) in order with predicate
\[
 \operatorname{available}(b,r,x,c)
 \iff \neg\operatorname{free}([],c,b)
       \land\neg\operatorname{free}([],c,r)\land c\ne x.
\]
A selected name belongs to this list and satisfies all three exclusions. The search returns the original exhaustion error exactly when every candidate fails availability.

Write \(S_x^r(t)\) for the original partial substitution, with sequential error propagation. Literals remain fixed; a name becomes \(r\) exactly when its label equals \(x\); join and same recurse in their original child order. For a let term, its branches are
\[
 S_x^r(\operatorname{let}(x,a,b))
 =\operatorname{let}(x,S_x^r(a),b),
\]
\[
 S_x^r(\operatorname{let}(y,a,b))
 =\operatorname{let}(y,S_x^r(a),S_x^r(b))
 \quad(y\ne x,\ \neg\operatorname{free}([],y,r)),
\]
and, for a conflicting \(y\ne x\) with selected fresh \(z\),
\[
 S_x^r(\operatorname{let}(y,a,b))
 =\operatorname{let}(z,S_x^r(a),S_x^r(\operatorname{rename}(y,z,b))).
\]
These equations denote successful constructor assembly inside the source's sequential error operation. In the conflicting branch the fresh search runs before either recursive substitution. Composition of whole-tree name maps,
\[
 \operatorname{mapNames}(f,\operatorname{mapNames}(g,t))
 =\operatorname{mapNames}(f\circ g,t),
\]
and its substitution execution counterpart justify the accumulated-map implementation of the exact whole-body rename. No alternate renaming convention is introduced.

194. Capture preservation at the evaluator substitution boundary

The original evaluator enters let substitution only after the initializer is a value. This source guard is equivalent to the value-constructor shape. On that exact call domain, structural value replacement agrees with the original substitution operation for every generated body and cannot fail through fresh-name exhaustion. Formation under the discharged binding produces formation in the remaining scope, including shadowing and initializer recursion.

The complete binder ledger retains every original structural address and binder name unchanged. Substitution removes the discharged free name and preserves every other free name. Thus all evaluator substitution calls preserve the stated binder identities and free-name distinctions. The standalone helper admits open replacements outside this evaluator call boundary; these results do not assert its unrestricted capture behavior.

The evaluator's ready-initializer guard is
\[
 \operatorname{isValue}(a)=\operatorname{true}
 \iff\exists v,\ a=\operatorname{value}(v).
\]
For this replacement shape, define \(R_x^v\) structurally: values are unchanged, matching names become \(\operatorname{value}(v)\), and join and same recurse through all children. Its let equation is
\[
 R_x^v(\operatorname{let}(y,a,b))
 =\operatorname{let}\bigl(y,R_x^v(a),
       \text{if }y=x\text{ then }b\text{ else }R_x^v(b)\bigr).
\]
Because a value has no free name, the conflicting-binder branch cannot be entered. Structural induction proves the exact agreement and formation result
\[
 S_x^{\operatorname{value}(v)}(t)=\operatorname{ok}(R_x^v(t)),\qquad
 \operatorname{Formation}(x::B,t)
 \Longrightarrow\operatorname{Formation}(B,R_x^v(t)).
\]
The shadowing case keeps the body intact and transports its scope after removing the redundant discharged binding.

Let \(\operatorname{binders}(t)\) retain every let binder with its full structural address. The same recursion proves
\[
 \operatorname{binders}(R_x^v(t))=\operatorname{binders}(t),\qquad
 \operatorname{free}(B,x,R_x^v(t))=\operatorname{false},
\]
\[
 y\ne x\Longrightarrow
 \operatorname{free}(B,y,R_x^v(t))=\operatorname{free}(B,y,t).
\]
Combining value-shape extraction with these identities constructs a successful substitution result for every evaluator call satisfying the original guard, with all binder identities and all other free-name decisions preserved.

195. Canonical replacement in the registered expression grammar

Binding identity is the exact name admitted by the original environment uniqueness check. Successful admission of a new target binding proves its identity is absent from the surrounding environment; conversely, a fresh identity constructs an admitted extension. The new lookup returns its recorded value, while every other binding is unchanged.

Structural replacement has a complete native derivation for each value, name and join. The derivation and computed result reconstruct one another, preserving original payloads and complete child trees. For an arbitrary open replacement evaluated in the surrounding environment, substitution reconstructs the original evaluation under the discharged target binding. Every resulting occurrence resolves to that exact surrounding environment row. No binder or presentation alias is introduced by replacement. Formation, identity replacement, sequential composition and generated join successor are preserved. All 256 original candidates, witnesses, controls and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the registered Fold Binding and Substitution Law.

For the value/name/join grammar, canonical replacement obeys
\[
 [x:=r]\operatorname{value}(v)=\operatorname{value}(v),\qquad
 [x:=r]\operatorname{name}(y)=
 \begin{cases}r,&y=x,\\ \operatorname{name}(y),&y\ne x,\end{cases}
\]
\[
 [x:=r]\operatorname{join}(a,b)
 =\operatorname{join}([x:=r]a,[x:=r]b).
\]
The executable operation and its value, matching-name, other-name and join derivation constructors reconstruct one another. Generated replacement and original syntax imply generated output. Identity replacement gives \([x:=\operatorname{name}(x)]e=e\).

Under the original uniqueness admission, adjoining \((x,v)\) requires \(x\notin\operatorname{names}(\rho)\); uniqueness of \(\rho\) and this absence also suffice. Its lookup equations are
\[
 \operatorname{lookupFirst}((x,v)::\rho,x)=\operatorname{some}v,\qquad
 y\ne x\Longrightarrow
 \operatorname{lookupFirst}((x,v)::\rho,y)=\operatorname{lookupFirst}(\rho,y).
\]
Induction on evaluation in the extended environment proves binding discharge:
\[
 \rho\vdash r\Downarrow v\ \land\ ((x,v)::\rho)\vdash e\Downarrow w
 \Longrightarrow \rho\vdash[x:=r]e\Downarrow w.
\]
In the matching-name case the inserted derivation supplies \(v\); other names use the unchanged lookup; the join case combines the two induction results. Replaying under the admitted environment gives the same implication for successful executable evaluations. The occurrence theorem then resolves every resulting name to its surrounding environment row, even when \(r\) itself contains names. Sequential replacement preserves a result when each replacement evaluates to its corresponding retained binding value.

196. Complete source evaluation traces

The evaluation record retains each entered expression and exact environment, every visited recursive child, and its terminal value or original failure. The native execution derivation preserves duplicate-binding rejection before descent and left-before-right evaluation. A first-child failure stops the join; a second-child failure retains the first completed trace. Successful joins retain both complete traces and their exact sum.

Computation and native derivation reconstruct one another. Every recorded result equals the original evaluator result, and two valid executions of the same expression in the same environment have identical results and complete event lists. Each accepted evaluation has such a trace. The literal base retains its identity record; join composition and successor preserve every visited child record, its syntax and its binding environment. All 256 original candidates, operational witnesses, controls and full program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Evaluation Law.

For environment \(\rho\), expression \(e\), child-event list \(C\), and result \(r\), define the complete frame
\[
 \operatorname{frame}(\rho,e,C,r)
 =[\operatorname{enter}(\rho,e)]
  \mathbin{+\!\!+}C
  \mathbin{+\!\!+}[\operatorname{leave}(\rho,e,r)].
\]
Duplicate bindings return their original error with \(C=[]\) before any descent. Under distinct bindings, leaves use their original evaluator result and an empty child list. For a join, if the left run returns \((\operatorname{error}q,L)\), the parent retains only \(L\); if it returns \((\operatorname{ok}a,L)\), the right run is evaluated and both lists \(L\mathbin{+\!\!+}R\) are retained. A right error is propagated; two successful values yield \(\operatorname{ok}(\operatorname{add}(a,b))\).

The native execution judgment has one constructor for each of these branches. Replay and structural reconstruction establish
\[
 \operatorname{Execution}(\rho,e,r,T)
 \iff\operatorname{run}(\rho,e)=(r,T),\qquad
 \operatorname{Execution}(\rho,e,r,T)\Longrightarrow
 \operatorname{eval}(\rho,e)=r.
\]
Consequently two execution certificates for the same environment and expression satisfy
\[
 \operatorname{Execution}(\rho,e,r,T)\land
 \operatorname{Execution}(\rho,e,r',T')
 \Longrightarrow r=r'\land T=T'.
\]
Every successful evaluation derivation under distinct bindings supplies this complete trace. Frame concatenation preserves every visited child event, including the completed left trace when the right child fails; it does not insert events for an unvisited child.

197. Operational and compositional meaning correspondence

Constructor-indexed meaning records retain the original syntax, environment, exact result and ordered failure clauses. Soundness transforms each complete operational execution into its meaning record. Adequacy reconstructs a complete operational execution from every meaning record. The result map is equivalent to the original evaluator, and meaning is unique. Consequently no admitted execution and corresponding meaning can disagree on their result within the frozen grammar.

The positive literal supplies the base correspondence. Join assembly preserves both complete component traces and their exact sum. Equality of meaning observations is equivalent to equality of the original result, including failure. Identity, serial composition and every frozen expression context preserve this observation relation. Existing complete occurrence and execution ledgers preserve the original syntax and binding identities throughout assembly. All 256 candidates, source operational witnesses, controls and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Operational and Denotational Correspondence Law.

The meaning judgment uses the same duplicate-binding, leaf, left-failure, right-failure and successful-join clauses as the source execution. In particular, under distinct bindings,
\[
 \frac{\rho\models a\Downarrow\operatorname{ok}u\quad
       \rho\models b\Downarrow\operatorname{ok}v}
      {\rho\models\operatorname{join}(a,b)\Downarrow
          \operatorname{ok}(\operatorname{add}(u,v))}.
\]
A left failure determines the parent result without a right premise; a right failure requires the successful left premise. Induction on execution gives soundness, and induction on these meaning constructors rebuilds the corresponding complete event frame, giving adequacy:
\[
 \operatorname{Meaning}(\rho,e,r)
 \iff\exists T,\operatorname{Execution}(\rho,e,r,T)
 \iff\operatorname{eval}(\rho,e)=r.
\]
Two meanings therefore have equal results. An execution result and a meaning result for the same input must also agree.

Define observational equality over the complete result type, including errors:
\[
 e\equiv_\rho f
 \iff\forall r,\ \operatorname{Meaning}(\rho,e,r)
                  \iff\operatorname{Meaning}(\rho,f,r).
\]
The exact-meaning equivalence proves
\[
 e\equiv_\rho f\iff\operatorname{eval}(\rho,e)=\operatorname{eval}(\rho,f).
\]
Identity and transitivity follow directly; case analysis of the ordered join results gives join congruence, and induction on a context gives
\[
 e\equiv_\rho f\Longrightarrow
 \operatorname{plug}(C,e)\equiv_\rho\operatorname{plug}(C,f).
\]
Successful join assembly retains the concatenated complete child traces inside the parent frame, so correspondence preserves the same syntax and environments as operational evaluation.

198. Positive semantic-value typing and preservation

Within the registered value/name/join grammar, the typing derivation retains a complete premise tree for positive exact literals, exact positive name bindings and binary composition. The typing decision is equivalent to this derivation and implies generated syntax. Every typed expression evaluates to a positive exact value under the original unique-environment admission, and its complete operational trace reconstructs. Structural substitution preserves this typing relation; evaluating the substituted expression retains a positive result and complete trace.

Rejected typing checks have explicit mismatch derivations: invalid literal payloads, absent or nonpositive name bindings, unsupported constructors, or a mismatched child. Rejection and mismatch derivation are equivalent, and a typed expression cannot have such a mismatch. These judgments characterize the positive semantic-value type; the original evaluator and its raw signed environment behavior remain unchanged. They establish the positive typing and preservation component of the Type Theory formalisation.

The positive semantic-value judgment has exactly three rules:
\[
 \frac{p>0}{\rho\vdash\operatorname{value}(p):+},\qquad
 \frac{\operatorname{lookupFirst}(\rho,x)=\operatorname{some}(\operatorname{positive}p)}
      {\rho\vdash\operatorname{name}(x):+},\qquad
 \frac{\rho\vdash a:+\quad\rho\vdash b:+}
      {\rho\vdash\operatorname{join}(a,b):+}.
\]
The decision procedure checks the positive constructor for literals and looked-up names and conjoins the two child decisions at a join. Structural reconstruction and replay give
\[
 \operatorname{checks}(\rho,e)=\operatorname{true}\iff\rho\vdash e:+.
\]
Induction on typing supplies generated syntax and a positive evaluation derivation. With the original distinct-binding condition, this yields
\[
 \exists p>0,\ 
 \operatorname{eval}(\rho,e)=\operatorname{ok}(\operatorname{positive}p)
 \land
 \operatorname{Execution}(\rho,e,\operatorname{ok}(\operatorname{positive}p),
                           (\operatorname{run}(\rho,e)).\operatorname{events}).
\]
Typing substitution is the structural implication
\[
 \rho\vdash r:+\ \land\ ((x,v)::\rho)\vdash e:+
 \Longrightarrow\rho\vdash[x:=r]e:+.
\]
Applying positive evaluation to this conclusion supplies the substituted expression's positive result and complete trace.

The mismatch constructors identify absent, zero or negative literals; missing, zero or negative name bindings; unsupported constructors; or a mismatched child. They satisfy
\[
 \operatorname{checks}(\rho,e)=\operatorname{false}
 \iff\operatorname{Mismatch}(\rho,e).
\]
A typed term and a mismatch would force the same Boolean decision to be both true and false, so they are incompatible. This judgment leaves the original evaluator's signed name values unchanged.

199. Generated type support and exact interface derivations

Types retain the original source-bound predicate on a finite generated carrier. Extending the carrier while preserving earlier verdicts retains the entire prior ordered type support and adds exactly the admitted new rows. Product extension retains the old joint support and every new pair. Sum extension retains the original held left/right labels and their order. These are generated source inputs, with no additional scientific parameter.

An interface derivation records every original composition call, its two arrows, the intermediate result or its first rejection. Replay and reconstruction establish equivalence with the source path fold. Successful composition requires exact interface identity; unequal interfaces produce the original rejection. Serial assembly preserves complete component traces, and a rejected prefix remains rejected under extension. The reconstructed flattened trace contains every earlier and appended component record. Together with positive typing, substitution and evaluation preservation, these results formalise the registered structural-admissibility requirements. All 256 original TYPE candidates, witnesses, controls and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Type-Theory Law.

For an admitted type support \(T\) selected from carrier \(C\), extending the carrier by \(E\) and preserving every old admission decision gives the exact list equation
\[
 T'=T\mathbin{+\!\!+}\operatorname{filter}(\operatorname{allowed}_{\rm new},E).
\]
Thus earlier support members persist with their order. The generated product and held-label sum obey
\[
 \operatorname{product}(L\mathbin{+\!\!+}E,R)
 =\operatorname{product}(L,R)\mathbin{+\!\!+}\operatorname{product}(E,R),
\]
\[
 \operatorname{sum}(L,R\mathbin{+\!\!+}E)
 =\operatorname{sum}(L,R)
  \mathbin{+\!\!+}[(\text{right-held},e):e\in E].
\]
The singleton product extension includes exactly \([(n,r):r\in R]\).

For a retained arrow \(h\), interface execution over the remaining arrow list has the base rule \(I(h,[],\operatorname{ok}h)\), a rejection rule when the next composition fails, and the successor rule
\[
 \frac{\operatorname{compose}(h,n)=\operatorname{ok}j\quad I(j,R,r)}
      {I(h,n::R,r)}.
\]
Replay and reconstruction yield
\[
 I(h,R,r)\iff\operatorname{foldRest}(h,R)=r.
\]
A successful first composition requires \(h.\operatorname{target}=n.\operatorname{source}\); inequality produces the original exact-interface rejection. Induction over successful execution gives
\[
 r.\operatorname{trace}
 =h.\operatorname{trace}
  \mathbin{+\!\!+}\operatorname{flatMap}(\operatorname{trace},R).
\]
Every original and appended component event is retained. Serial executions compose through the shared resulting arrow; a failed prefix stays failed under any appended suffix, because the rejected constructor performs no further composition.

200. Program equivalence on registered observations

For each input in the common finite support, the ledger retains both complete source executions and their registered observation labels. Ledger membership reconstructs the original input and both execution derivations. The equivalence certificate is true exactly when observations agree at every registered input. A failed certificate constructs an input with unequal observations.

Equal and unequal observation rows form exhaustive disjoint closed and retained distinction ledgers. Equivalence closes every observation row, while a retained distinction refutes equivalence. The complete underlying traces remain available in both cases. Identity, symmetry and serial equivalence composition preserve the declared observation relation. Support assembly and successor retain every earlier execution record; extending support requires agreement on its new inputs. A declared post-map preserves already equal observations. These results respect the original observation and input boundary. All 256 original candidates, witnesses, controls and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Program-Equivalence Law.

Let \(S\) be the registered input-environment list and \(o\) its observation map on complete executions. For programs \(a,b\), each input row is
\[
 r_\rho=(\rho,\operatorname{run}(\rho,a),\operatorname{run}(\rho,b),
                 o(\operatorname{run}(\rho,a)),o(\operatorname{run}(\rho,b))).
\]
The complete ledger is \([r_\rho:\rho\in S]\), retaining ordering and repetitions. Its checker recursively conjoins observation equality:
\[
 C([],o,a,b)=\operatorname{true},\qquad
 C(\rho::S,o,a,b)=[o(\operatorname{run}(\rho,a))=o(\operatorname{run}(\rho,b))]
                  \land C(S,o,a,b).
\]
Induction gives the exact equivalence
\[
 C(S,o,a,b)=\operatorname{true}
 \iff\forall\rho\in S,\ o(\operatorname{run}(\rho,a))=o(\operatorname{run}(\rho,b)).
\]
A false decision reconstructs a member of \(S\) where those observations differ. Filtering ledger rows by equality and inequality yields exhaustive, disjoint closed and retained ledgers, respectively. Every row still reconstructs both complete execution judgments.

Equality supplies identity, symmetry and transitive composition on the same \(S,o\). For support concatenation,
\[
 \operatorname{ledger}(S\mathbin{+\!\!+}T)
 =\operatorname{ledger}(S)\mathbin{+\!\!+}\operatorname{ledger}(T).
\]
Agreement on both components therefore supplies agreement on the combined support. For any declared post-map \(h\), equality of \(o\)-observations implies equality of \(h\circ o\)-observations by congruence.

201. Generated descent and terminal coverage

Every recursive call of the registered expression evaluator follows a strict child of its generated syntax tree. Structural induction constructs accessibility, and the retained node count decreases at each call. The call relation preserves the original duplicate-binding guard and admits the right child only after successful left evaluation. Every expression and input reconstructs a complete execution ending in its exact value or original failure.

For the original transition system, a retained descent certificate decreases on every transition. Each complete path has length bounded by its initial rank; native execution reconstructs both that bound and its exact state trace. Serial assembly and successor preserve the complete records and bound. A nonempty return cycle contradicts strict descent, and the registered loop provides an explicit counterexample to the existence of such a certificate. These results preserve the stated boundary: no total termination decider for the universal language is claimed. All 256 original candidates, witnesses, controls and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Termination Law.

The evaluator's structural measure is
\[
 n(\operatorname{value}(v))=n(\operatorname{name}(x))
 =n(\operatorname{outside}(t))=1,\qquad
 n(\operatorname{join}(a,b))=n(a)+n(b)+1.
\]
Each child has strictly smaller measure. Structural induction constructs accessibility for the child relation. The actual call relation additionally requires distinct bindings, and a right call requires a successful left evaluation. Every actual call is a child step, so it also decreases \(n\); duplicate bindings admit no recursive call. Complete execution reconstruction gives
\[
 \forall\rho,e,\ \exists r,C,\
 \operatorname{Execution}(\rho,e,r,\operatorname{frame}(\rho,e,C,r))
 \land\operatorname{eval}(\rho,e)=r.
\]

For the original transition system \(M\), a rank certificate is the explicit property
\[
 \operatorname{Ranked}(M,\nu)
 \iff\forall s,a,t,\ \operatorname{step}(M,s,a)=\operatorname{ok}t
                 \Rightarrow\nu(t)<\nu(s).
\]
Path induction yields, for a path from \(s\) to \(t\) with action list \(A\),
\[
 \nu(t)\le\nu(s),\qquad |A|\le\nu(s),
\]
and \(|A|>0\) gives \(\nu(t)<\nu(s)\). The successor argument uses the next strict decrease and the bound on the remaining path. A nonempty return path would therefore imply \(\nu(s)<\nu(s)\), excluding every such rank certificate. The original loop supplies that counterexample. Serial and one-step path constructors retain the entire concatenated trace and the same initial-rank bound.

202. Correctness against the registered specification

The specification retains its exact precondition, postcondition on complete execution records, and partial or total termination declaration. Partial correctness establishes the postcondition for each retained execution of an admitted input. Total correctness additionally retains execution existence, supplied here by the finite registered evaluator. The complete-support certificate is equivalent to the declared correctness relation in either mode. Rejection reconstructs an admitted input whose postcondition fails.

Every input row retains its complete execution, specification decisions and termination declaration. Failure rows are exactly those carrying original runtime errors; their acceptability remains governed by the registered postcondition. Support assembly and successor preserve every earlier row. Specification consequence retains the same original execution under proved precondition and postcondition implications. Serial agreement connects original path certificates through their exact middle interface and retains both component traces. All 256 original candidates, witnesses, controls and complete native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Program-Correctness Law.

For precondition \(P\), postcondition \(Q\) on complete executions, program \(e\), and registered support \(S\), partial correctness is
\[
 \forall\rho\in S,\ P(\rho)=\operatorname{true}\Rightarrow
 \forall r,T,\ \operatorname{Execution}(\rho,e,r,T)
                  \Rightarrow Q(\rho,(r,T))=\operatorname{true}.
\]
Total correctness conjoins existence of such an execution for every admitted input. Here that existence is supplied constructively by the finite evaluator's complete execution theorem, including its original error outcomes.

The certificate is
\[
 K([],P,Q,e)=\operatorname{true},
\]
\[
 K(\rho::S,P,Q,e)
 =(\text{if }P(\rho)\text{ then }Q(\rho,\operatorname{run}(\rho,e))
                  \text{ else true})\land K(S,P,Q,e).
\]
Induction, together with uniqueness of complete execution, proves that \(K=\operatorname{true}\) is equivalent to the correctness judgment selected by the specification's partial or total mode. A false certificate constructs
\[
 \rho\in S,\qquad P(\rho)=\operatorname{true},\qquad
 Q(\rho,\operatorname{run}(\rho,e))=\operatorname{false}.
\]
The row ledger retains the input, complete execution, both decisions and mode. Its failure filter selects exactly rows whose result is an original error; the postcondition determines whether such a row is acceptable.

Consequence preserves an execution when the new precondition implies the old one on \(S\), and the old postcondition implies the new one for that same execution. Serial path agreement uses the first path's established middle condition as the second path's premise and reconstructs the concatenated action and state records.

203. Generated formal specification relations

A specification retains its generated input and output carriers and exact allowed relation under the original relation-admission guard. Membership proves both carrier identities. The complete observation certificate is true exactly when every observed row is allowed; falsification retains every observed row excluded from that relation. Refinement is allowed-row inclusion, preserving verified observations through identity and composition. Removing an allowed row produces an explicit falsification at the new boundary.

Invariant checking covers every state of the retained source trace and follows from the admitted initial invariant and transition preservation. A finite liveness witness retains original input admission, a complete executable path and its visited registered goal. A terminal obligation retains the original halted execution and normal terminal state. Both reconstruct native execution, and serial extension preserves the earlier goal and component records. The generated relation supplies the postcondition of the existing correctness checker; every verified execution therefore reconstructs an allowed pair in the original input and output carriers. All 256 candidates, witnesses, controls and full native program fields and hashes agree with pinned V3, completing the Fold Formal-Specification Law.

An admitted relation specification consists of input carrier \(I\), output carrier \(O\), and allowed rows \(R\), with the original generated-relation admission. It supplies
\[
 (i,o)\in R\Longrightarrow i\in I\land o\in O.
\]
For observed rows \(V\), the checker and falsification ledger are
\[
 C_R(V)=\bigwedge_{v\in V}[v\in R],\qquad
 F_R(V)=[v\in V:v\notin R].
\]
Thus \(C_R(V)=\operatorname{true}\iff\forall v\in V,\ v\in R\), and membership in \(F_R(V)\) refutes acceptance. If \(R_1\subseteq R_2\), a certificate against \(R_1\) supplies one against \(R_2\). If a row is removed from \(R_2\) to obtain \(R_1\), its singleton observation remains accepted under \(R_2\) and is an explicit falsification under \(R_1\).

For an invariant \(J\), trace checking is
\[
 \operatorname{invariantChecks}(J,T)=\operatorname{true}
 \iff\forall s\in T,\ J(s)=\operatorname{true}.
\]
An admitted initial \(J\) and preservation across every successful original transition give this statement by path induction. Finite liveness retains initial-state admission and a path whose full state trace contains a registered goal. A terminal witness additionally retains a halted path and normal terminal state in the goals. Both reconstruct actual execution; extending an existing path preserves goals already in its retained prefix.

For the original input and output observation maps \(\iota,\omega\), the induced postcondition is
\[
 Q(\rho,(r,T))=[(\iota(\rho),\omega(r,T))\in R].
\]
Applying the correctness certificate to an admitted input and its execution yields this exact allowed pair and hence both original carrier memberships.

204. Retained program transformation traces

A generated transformation retains the registered rule identity, source syntax, target syntax and exact contextual occurrence at every step. Semantic correspondence follows through arbitrary finite traces by contextual preservation and serial composition of the original evaluation relation. Every successful source evaluation reconstructs a complete native target execution with the same value. The proof retains every rule and both generated endpoints.

The recorded predecessor rows supply a reverse traversal of the complete transformation; a target without its record cannot recover distinct predecessors that share it. Resource evidence remains the original separate resource-ledger operation, retaining the initial observation, each rule event and occupied support. Semantic preservation alone asserts no resource improvement. The complete 256-member grammar, operational witnesses, controls and native program fields agree with pinned V3.

A transformation row retains a rule \(q\) and context \(C\), with
\[
 \operatorname{source}(q,C)=\operatorname{plug}(C,q.\operatorname{source}),
 \qquad
 \operatorname{target}(q,C)=\operatorname{plug}(C,q.\operatorname{target}).
\]
Admission retains rule membership, generated context and endpoints, and successful-value equivalence of the rule endpoints for every environment in the registered support. Contextual equivalence gives the same relation for the entire source and target expressions. Induction over the retained transformation trace therefore gives
\[
 \operatorname{Trace}(R,S,e,L,f)\land\rho\in S
 \Longrightarrow e\sim_\rho f.
\]
A successful source evaluation then supplies a complete target execution with that same value. Induction also retains admission of every row and generated syntax of both endpoints. Serial traces concatenate through their exact common expression.

The reverse judgment walks recorded target-to-source rows. Induction on the forward trace constructs a reverse traversal returning to the original expression. Without the predecessor record, two distinct predecessors \(a\ne b\) sharing output \(z\) cannot both be recovered by one function:
\[
 \neg\exists g,\ g(z)=a\land g(z)=b.
\]
For initial identity \(i\), the resource event trace is \(i::[q.\operatorname{identity}:q\in L]\). Its accepted ledger satisfies
\[
 \ell.\operatorname{timeCount}=|L|+1,\qquad
 \ell.\operatorname{spaceCount}=|H|,
\]
and reconstructs that entire event trace and supplied occupied support \(H\). No resource inequality follows merely from semantic preservation.

205. Semantics-preserving compilation relations

Let \(R\subseteq I\times O\) be the admitted compiler relation. Its coverage check has the exact meaning
\[
 \operatorname{coverageCheck}(R)=\operatorname{true}
 \iff\forall s\in I,\exists t,\ (s,t)\in R.
\]
The runtime interface retains input environments \(S\), source and target expression maps \(E_s,E_t\), and observation \(o\). Each row check is
\[
 \operatorname{checked}(s,t)
 =C(S,o,E_s(s),E_t(t)),
\]
using the complete program-equivalence checker. The complete compiler check conjoins coverage with every admitted row check:
\[
 \operatorname{compilerCheck}
 =\operatorname{coverageCheck}(R)
   \land\bigwedge_{(s,t)\in R}\operatorname{checked}(s,t).
\]
Consequently it supplies, for every \(s\in I\), a target \(t\in O\) with both \((s,t)\in R\) and equal runtime observations for every input in \(S\). Every observation row reconstructs both complete native executions.

Serial correspondence requires equal input supports, equal observation maps, and exact equality of the first target expression and second source expression at the intermediate form. Transitivity then gives the source-to-final-target equivalence. Support extension conjoins the new input checks and concatenates complete observation ledgers. A boundary \(B\) records source and program identities, program \(e\), specification \(\sigma\), input list \(S\), premises \(P\), rules \(R\), and conclusion \(c\). A proof object \(p\) supplies its identities and step list \(T\). The exact checker theorem is
\[
 \operatorname{check}(B,p)=\operatorname{true}\iff
 p.\operatorname{sourceId}=B.\operatorname{sourceId}
 \land p.\operatorname{programId}=B.\operatorname{programId}
 \land\operatorname{AcceptedTrace}(R,P,T)
 \land c\in\operatorname{afterSteps}(P,T).
\]
The registered interpretation \(\mu\) retains the proof obligations
\[
 \forall r\in R,\ 
   (\forall a\in r.\operatorname{premises},\mu(a))
       \Rightarrow\mu(r.\operatorname{conclusion}),\qquad
 \forall a\in P,\mu(a),
\]
\[
 \mu(c)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Correct}(S,\sigma,e).
\]
Inference-trace induction transports these premise and rule witnesses to every proposition in the final state. Applying it to the retained conclusion gives \(\mu(c)\), hence the declared program correctness and its complete-support certificate. The interpretation's soundness witnesses are explicit premises of this construction.

An identity mismatch contradicts the corresponding checker conjunct; an invalid trace or missing conclusion contradicts its own conjunct. With \(c\in P\), the empty proof is accepted. For two accepted traces, the second begins at \(\operatorname{afterSteps}(P,T_1)\); the final-state composition identity
\[
 \operatorname{afterSteps}(P,T_1\mathbin{+\!\!+}T_2)
 =\operatorname{afterSteps}(\operatorname{afterSteps}(P,T_1),T_2)
\]
then constructs the combined proof while retaining every earlier step.

207. Positive causal relation expansion

Causal expansion retains the original edges and adds exactly endpoints joined through a held intermediate event. A positive path contains at least one original edge; reflexive edges are not inserted by convention. Native proofs show that every finite expansion round preserves original edges and introduces only original positive paths. A closed result containing the original edges is their least transitive closure. Its self-edge exclusion is equivalent to exclusion of positive cycles. These results establish the relation-level expansion and cycle boundary; the complete Concurrency entry is verified below.

For held edge rows \(R\) and generated pair support \(U\), one expansion is the complete filtered list
\[
 E_U(R)=[(a,c)\in U:(a,c)\in R
             \ \lor\ \exists b,\ (a,b)\in R\land(b,c)\in R].
\]
Its membership equivalence follows from the exact two-row linking predicate. Original supported edges persist; each new edge is the concatenation of two held paths. Define a positive path by an original-edge constructor and a path-concatenation constructor, with no empty-path constructor. Induction over expansion rounds proves
\[
 (a,b)\in R_n\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Path}(R_0,a,b).
\]
Original-edge containment persists whenever those edges belong to \(U\).

If linked endpoint pairs are supported and a round adds no new members, its rows are transitive. More generally, any transitive relation \(T\) containing \(R_0\) contains every positive \(R_0\)-path, by induction on the path constructors. Combining that leastness result with round soundness gives, for a closed round containing the original edges,
\[
 (a,b)\in R_n\iff\operatorname{Path}(R_0,a,b).
\]
In particular,
\[
 \bigl(\forall a,\ (a,a)\notin R_n\bigr)
 \iff\bigl(\forall a,\ \neg\operatorname{Path}(R_0,a,a)\bigr).
\]
This excludes exactly positive cycles. Reflexive edges arise only when justified by such a positive path; no reflexive closure is added by convention.

320. Squared Planck/proton support and terminal transport

Write c=3 for the derived generator and b=2 for the derived binary count. The successor volume and its least cover are
  V = c^(c+1) = 3^4 = 81,
  d = min{k ∈ ℕ : k>0 and 81 ≤ 2^k} = 7.
Indeed 2^6=64<81≤128=2^7; monotonicity gives 2^k≤64 for every positive k<7. The complete support is N=b^d=128. Its positive predecessor m satisfies m+1=N, hence m=127 uniquely. Consequently the squared hierarchy is H²=b^m=2^127, and the corresponding unsquared exponent is m/2=127/2. These are successive operations on the derived counts; no exponent is independently selected.

For terminal transport let α=3676744786/503846395469 and g_c=2/3. The removed share and retained complement are
  g_c α = 7353489572/1511539186407,
  t = 1−g_c α = 1504185696835/1511539186407.
The inequalities 0<7353489572<1511539186407 imply 0<t<1. Multiplication of the bare squared carrier by this complement gives
  H_terminal² = 2^127 t
             = 255923934603817488008405160690199418432572494970880/1511539186407.
Thus 0<H_terminal²<H². This equation describes the terminal component; completion of that separate source entry is recorded separately from completion of the base hierarchy.

Generator-three successor volume is 81, whose least binary cover depth is 7. Complete support has 128 positions and its unique positive predecessor has 127 massive positions. The retained unsquared exponent is 127/2; the exact squared comparison carrier is 2^127. Native proofs retain the least-cover exclusion, predecessor uniqueness and both exact exponent records.

322. Complete prime-sector construction

Using the least cover d=7 derived above, define
  P = {p ∈ {2,3,4,5,6,7} : ∀a ∈ {2,…,p−1}, a does not divide p}.

The excluded counts have explicit divisors: 4=2·2 and 6=2·3. Therefore P={2,3,5,7}, with every count in the source range decided. For each admitted p, complete squared support and its positive predecessor give
  M(p)+1=p²,  M(p)=p²−1,
  g(p)=(p−1)/p.
Evaluation in source order yields
  (M(2),M(3),M(5),M(7))=(3,8,24,48),
  (g(2),g(3),g(5),g(7))=(1/2,2/3,4/5,6/7).
Although 11 is prime, 11>7, so it is outside this derived sector support.
