323. Exact Fold return and binary multiplicative order

For q>0, let h_q(x) be the held representative of x modulo q: h_q(x)=x mod q when that remainder is nonzero, and h_q(x)=q otherwise. The executed recurrence is
  r_0=p,  r_(k+1)=h_q(2r_k).
Induction gives r_k ≡ p·2^k (mod q): the initial step is p≡p·1, and doubling the induction identity gives r_(k+1)≡2p·2^k=p·2^(k+1). For a reduced input, gcd(p,q)=1. Because held representatives are unique in {1,…,q},
  r_k=p ⇔ p·2^k≡p (mod q)
        ⇔ q divides p(2^k−1)
        ⇔ q divides 2^k−1
        ⇔ 2^k≡1 (mod q).
The cancellation uses gcd(p,q)=1. Taking the least positive k on both sides identifies first Fold return with first binary multiplicative order whenever that return exists. For odd q>1, existence follows from the finite-carrier argument below; the even-denominator transient is treated separately.

For every supplied positive reduced part p/q through the One, native repeated double-and-cast execution satisfies rank_k modulo q = p times 2^k modulo q. The complete-denominator representative is retained when a remainder vanishes. Reducedness permits cancellation of p: an exact return to the original held rank occurs if and only if 2^k modulo q equals 1 modulo q. The first-positive-return and first-positive-order predicates therefore have the same least index. These identities hold at arbitrary supplied finite depth; they do not use a bounded prime list. All 25 definitions and supporting theorems have zero Lean axiom dependencies.

Successful finite witness replays preserve the original order-loop and orbit-loop guard, update and stopping branches. The native correspondence proofs connect returned replay records to those source loop relations. An unfinished replay cannot pass a witness; its internal execution budget supplies no scientific value or survivor selector.

324. Counted binary transient and complete orbit support

Before that depth, at depth k with a=k+r and positive r, its denominator is exactly 2^r times m and is even. Thus a is exactly the first depth with an odd denominator. The proof includes the source complete-denominator representative, normalization and greatest-common-divisor cancellation; it concerns actual held-rank execution.

For any first binary order, distinct positions before that first return cannot collide. The complete indexed cycle has exactly that many positions, and every admitted reduced numerator repeats with that period. All four original finite witnesses also pass: the five displayed order values, every reduced rank through odd denominator 99, the ten prime-period divisibility cases and the denominator 73 tiling count. 325. Complete cyclotomic counting and source-loop termination

For odd q>1, gcd(2,q)=1. If two powers at indices i<j have the same residue, cancellation of 2^i gives 2^(j−i)≡1 (mod q). The q+1 residues at indices 0,…,q cannot all be distinct in a carrier of q residues, so a positive return exists. Let ℓ be the least such index. For a reduced starting rank p, an equality r_i=r_j with 0≤i<j<ℓ would imply 2^(j−i)≡1, contradicting minimality. Hence its complete cycle C(p) has exactly ℓ members.

For any cycles C(p), C(s), suppose r_i(p)=r_j(s). Each cycle has period ℓ, so advancing within the cycle by the complementary index ℓ−j expresses s as an iterate of p. The same reasoning expresses p as an iterate of s. Thus C(p)=C(s). Every reduced rank lies in its own cycle, and distinct cycles are disjoint. If R_q={r:1≤r<q, gcd(r,q)=1} and C is the number of cycles, successive removal of complete cycles therefore gives
  |R_q| = ℓ C.
For prime q, each 1≤r<q is coprime to q, so |R_q|=q−1 and
  q−1 = ℓ C, hence ℓ divides q−1.
This is a finite partition derivation for each admitted denominator, independent of the finite witness table.

For every admitted odd denominator above the One, the finite carrier forces a first binary return. If no return occurred through the carrier bound, one more executed position than there are held ranks would have to remain distinct, contradicting the exact finite count. The original order loop therefore terminates, and its returned count is the first binary multiplicative order. For every reduced numerator, the original orbit loop returns a complete ordered trace of exactly this length.

The executed cycles preserve reducedness. Two cycles that share a rank have identical members; every reduced rank belongs to its own cycle. Removing complete cycles successively from the full reduced-residue support proves that the residue count equals orbit length times orbit count. For a prime denominator, every positive rank below the prime is reduced, so the period divides one less than the prime. These are arbitrary supplied finite-denominator results, not conclusions extrapolated from the witness census.

The orbit loop also terminates for positive held parts with even denominators: each fresh step adds a distinct held rank, and the finite carrier bounds the number of additions before repetition. Combined with the counted binary-depth result, this retains the transient prefix before the odd recurrent core. The new derivation batch has 71 audited definitions and theorems with zero axiom dependencies; the original 256-form program batch has a further 108.

327. Exact positive-ratio composition

Let N(a,b) denote division of a positive numerator and denominator by their greatest common divisor. Its normalized pair (u,v) satisfies av=ub. For an arbitrary reduced positive ratio x=(n,d), define its reciprocal y=N(d,n) and composition x·y=N(ny_n,dy_d). Value preservation for y gives dy_d=y_n n=ny_n. The composite therefore has equal positive raw numerator and denominator, and normalization gives (1,1). Every admitted positive ratio composed with its reciprocal returns the One, including ratios above the One.

For positive counts a,b,c, the raw product of the views a/b and b/c is N(ab,bc)=(u,v). Value preservation gives abv=ubc. Associativity and commutativity place the common positive factor b on the same side; applying finite division by b and the identity (bk)/b=k gives av=uc. Thus this composite represents a/c exactly. For any positive m, the raw product of m/1 and 1/m is (m·1,1·m); commutativity makes the sides equal, and normalization yields (1,1). This reconstructs the One from m corresponding equal parts.

These native component results have thirteen audited definitions and theorems with zero axiom dependencies. The full source entry additionally includes shorter separation, complement, local transport and finite-family cycle composition.

The same telescoping law holds for actual composition after normalizing both input views. Let x=N(a,b), y=N(c,d), and z=compose(x,y). Value preservation gives ax_d=x_n b, cy_d=y_n d, and (x_n y_n)z_d=z_n(x_d y_d). Put e=x_d y_d>0. Multiplying and rearranging the first two identities gives (ac)e=(x_n y_n)(bd). Multiplication by z_d and substitution of the third identity therefore yield e(ac z_d)=e(z_n bd). Exact positive cancellation gives ac z_d=z_n bd. Specializing the second view to N(b,c) gives ab z_d=z_n bc; cancellation of b gives a z_d=z_n c. Thus normalization of either input and the composite preserves the telescoping relation itself.

328. Unique positive holding complement

For a generated count m>1, write m=k+2 and define h=N(k+1,k+2), the exact (m−1)-of-m part. A positive ratio x=n/d is the holding complement of the equal part 1/m precisely when x+1/m=1. Clearing the positive denominator dm gives the entirely positive-side equation
  nm+d=dm.
Expanding dm=d(k+1)+d and removing the same terminal d from both sides forces nm=(k+1)d. Normalization of h gives h_n m=(k+1)h_d, so h satisfies the holding equation. If any other positive ratio x satisfies it, multiply its forced equality by h_d and the equality for h by d. Rearranging gives m(nh_d)=m(h_n d). Cancellation by positive m yields nh_d=h_n d, establishing uniqueness of the exact rational value. At m=2, h=N(1,2)=(1,2), the half-One witness. The condition m>1 is precisely the positive-complement domain: at m=1 the holding equation would require n+d=d, which admits no positive numerator. These seven native definitions and theorems have zero axiom dependencies; the source entry's other relation and cycle requirements remain separate.

330. Positive local separation before casting

After a common finite refinement, let the held ordered positions be l/d and u/d, where 0<l<u≤d. Their positive directed gap is N(u−l,d).   (u+u)−(l+l)=2u−2l=2(u−l)=(u−l)+(u−l).
Normalizing the two equal positive numerators against the same denominator gives identical positive Fraction objects. For the resulting transported pair (t_n,t_d),
  2(u−l)t_d=t_n d.
Thus the uncast local gap transports by the generated binary factor. This statement concerns the local gap before whole-turn casting; it does not identify every directed gap with the shorter circular separation or omit a change of cast branch. The component's seven audited declarations have no axiom dependencies.

For two positions on the same Fold branch, the actual casting operation preserves this doubled gap. If 2u≤d, then also 2l≤d; both cast numerators are unchanged, and their difference is 2(u−l). The shared-removal identity follows by restoring d to both retained numerators and cancelling the common added term. Thus both same-branch cases are connected to actual complete-One removal, not only to uncast arithmetic. 331. Least common return of every positive finite cycle family

For supplied positive periods a,b, let g=gcd(a,b). The actual native least-common-multiple operation is L=ab/g. Since g divides both a and b, exact division yields L=a(b/g)=b(a/g). Thus a divides L, b divides L, and L>0. Also gL=ab.

To prove minimality, let c>0 be any shared return, so a divides c and b divides c. Then ab divides both ac and bc. The constructive greatest-common-divisor property gives ab dividing gcd(ac,bc). Positive scaling gives gcd(ac,bc)=c·gcd(a,b)=cg. Therefore cg=abk=gLk for a finite count k. Cancelling the positive g gives c=Lk, so L divides every positive shared return and L≤c. This establishes the first common return, not merely one common multiple.

For a nonempty supplied finite family, retain its first period p and the remaining finite list P. Define J(p,[])=p and J(p,q::P)=J(lcm(p,q),P). Induction proves positivity and the exact characterization, for every positive n,
  J(p,P) divides n ⇔ p divides n and every period in P divides n.
The empty-tail case is the single cycle. At a successor, divisibility by lcm(p,q) is equivalent to simultaneous divisibility by p and q, by the two-period result; the induction hypothesis handles the remaining list. Applying the characterization to n=J(p,P) shows that every supplied period returns there. Applying it to any other positive shared return n shows J(p,P) divides n and hence J(p,P)≤n. The original witness is J(2,[3,4])=12. All sixteen native definitions and theorems in this derivation have zero axiom dependencies; the construction uses no completed infinite family or fixed period table.

335. Exact finite-difference identities

Put q=2^k and h=1/q at a positive generated depth k. For the centered quadratic construction, clearing the denominator q² gives
  (q+1)²+(q−1)²=2q²+2.
Thus (1+h)²+(1−h)²=2+2h², and the centered second difference divided by h² is exactly 2. The native proof establishes the denominator identity for q=m+1 by distributivity and exact addition; it is not inferred from the finite witness list.

For the forward cubic construction, clearing q³ gives
  (q+2)³+q³=2(q+1)³+6q+6.
Equivalently (1+2h)³+1=2(1+h)³+6h²+6h³. Its forward second difference divided by h² is therefore 6+6h, with exact positive error E_k=6/2^k. The native error carrier is the normalized positive fraction N(6,2^k); its numerator and denominator satisfy 6E_d=E_n2^k. The denominator successor is 2^(k+1)=2^k+2^k. These seven audited component declarations have zero axiom dependencies.

For normalized E_k=(a_n,a_d) and E_(k+1)=(b_n,b_d), value preservation gives 6a_d=a_n2^k and 6b_d=b_n(2^k+2^k). Multiplying these identities by the opposite denominators and cancelling the positive 2^k yields (b_n+b_n)a_d=a_n b_d. Therefore 2E_(k+1)=E_k exactly. Since b_n a_d>0, it follows that b_n a_d<a_n b_d, proving strict decrease in the actual normalized representation.

There is also a finite certificate for every supplied positive rational tolerance a/d. Induction gives n+1≤2^n: at the successor, n+2≤2(n+1)≤2·2^n. Choose the finite depth K=6d. 344. General odd-denominator vacuum recurrence

For any supplied denominator q > 1 with q mod 2 = 1, let r_k be the held rank obtained after k executions of the original binary Fold from unit rank r_0 = 1. The existing exact rank calculation gives

\[
 0<r_k\le q,\qquad r_k\equiv 2^k\pmod q,\qquad
 \gcd(r_k,q)=1.
\]

The first inequality follows from the positive held-rank transition. Reducedness follows from gcd(1,q) = 1 and coprimality of every binary power with odd q. Exact held-rank recovery identifies return with the binary order condition:

\[
 r_k=1\iff 2^k\bmod q=1\bmod q.
\]

If no positive return occurred through q steps, cancellation of the common binary factor would make all q+1 positions r_0,…,r_q distinct. They lie in the complete q-member positive rank carrier, contradicting its finite cardinality. Ascending search therefore supplies a first period p with

\[
 0<p\le q,\qquad r_p=1,\qquad
 \forall j,\ 0<j<p\Longrightarrow r_j\ne1.
\]

The executed transition then gives r_(p+k) = r_k. Repeated removal of a complete period yields

\[
 r_k=r_{k\bmod p}\qquad(k\ge0).
\]

Thus positivity and exact recurrence persist at every finite continuation, without a cutoff on denominators or Fold depth. Each generated odd successor q = 2n+3 satisfies q > 1 and q mod 2 = 1 by the exact remainder identity (3+2n) mod 2 = 3 mod 2 = 1, so the same first-return theorem applies for every supplied n. Five native theorems establish these statements with no axiom dependencies. The executable odd-denominator wrapper and its complete registered entry checks are separate from these general recurrence theorems.

346. Exact Fraction and held-rank recurrence correspondence

Let c_q(n) denote the original positive cast: repeatedly subtract q while the retained numerator is greater than q. Each subtraction preserves the remainder, so induction on the actual removal trace proves

\[
 c_q(n)\bmod q=n\bmod q.
\]

For positive n and q, the cast lies in 1,…,q. The held-rank Fold is

\[
 F_q(n)=\begin{cases}q,&2n\bmod q=0,\\2n\bmod q,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
\]

It lies in the same held-rank interval and has remainder 2n mod q. Two ranks in 1,…,q with equal remainders are equal: a nonzero remainder names the rank itself, while zero names the retained whole q. Hence

\[
 c_q(n+n)=F_q(n).
\]

Write N(n,q) for the original normalized positive Fraction n/q. The already derived normalization and Fold-cast identity now yields the exact state equation

\[
 \operatorname{Fold}(N(n,q))=N(c_q(n+n),q)=N(F_q(n),q).
\]

For the unit orbit define x_k = N(r_k,q), where r_k is the actual held-rank iteration from section 344. Then Fold(x_k) = x_(k+1). The rank return r_p = 1 supplied for every odd q > 1 therefore gives x_p = x_0. This is equality of the actual normalized Fraction states, including their positive-cast convention, rather than only equality of modular residues. Eight native declarations establish this correspondence and Fraction-return existence with no axiom dependencies. The original loop's first-return branch and successful output for arbitrary odd q follow from the visited-state argument below.


347. General success of the original odd-denominator first-return loop

For odd q, reducedness gives gcd(r_k,q) = 1. Normalization therefore leaves the numerator unchanged:

\[
 \operatorname{numerator}(x_k)=r_k/\gcd(r_k,q)=r_k.
\]

Equality of Fraction states implies equality of their held ranks. Before the first binary period p, the rank-cancellation proof excludes every collision at distinct positions, hence

\[
 0\le i<j<p\quad\Longrightarrow\quad x_i\ne x_j.
\]

At loop position k < p, retain the invariant that the current state is x_k and every stored visited key is the key of some x_i with i ≤ k. The actual next Fold is x_(k+1). If k+1 = p, this state is x_0, so the original return-to-source branch succeeds before testing visited keys. If it differs from the source, k+1 < p. A visited-key match would then give x_(k+1) = x_i with i ≤ k, contradicting the displayed collision exclusion. Thus the non-source-repeat error branch is impossible. Advancing adds exactly the next key and preserves the invariant. Induction over the terminating original Run proves successful output.

The retained first-return theorem supplies the complete trace structure

\[
 \operatorname{firstReturn}(x_0)=\operatorname{ok}(T),\qquad
 T=B\mathbin{+\!+}[x_0],\qquad
 \operatorname{Steps}(x_0,T),\qquad
 \forall x\in B,\ x\ne x_0.
\]

This also proves T is nonempty. For odd q, the even-denominator guard cannot hold: if floor(q/2)·2 = q, taking remainders gives q mod 2 = 0, contradicting q mod 2 = 1. The wrapper therefore reaches the original loop, and its final positive-domain guard preserves T by section 345. For every supplied n with (n+2) mod 2 = 1,

\[
 \exists T,\quad
 \operatorname{oddDenominatorOrbit}(n+2)=\operatorname{ok}(T)
 \land\operatorname{FirstReturn}(1/(n+2),T).
\]

Nine native theorems establish the Fraction-state collision exclusion, actual loop success, guard passage and complete wrapper result with no axiom dependencies. The theorem is quantified over the full admitted odd-denominator domain.
